### Does N2 touch `ci.yml` or `db/`

**No to both, and the plan says why so that a builder can falsify it.**

- **`db/`.** The new event types (`log.operator-key`, and the signed shapes of the two existing types) are rows of the existing `events` table: `event_type` is `TEXT NOT NULL` with no `CHECK` (`db/0001:31` [read]; the only index on it is `:50`), and `db/0006`'s column grant (`event_id, event_type, aggregate_id, actor_id, payload, server_ts`) already covers every column a new row supplies. No new table, column or grant. A builder who finds a `db/` change necessary stops and returns to the architect: PR S's merge made `db/` the owner's, and the commit that touches it goes **last** and the owner merges it.
- **`ci.yml`.** CI runs `dotnet test Curia.sln` and `cargo test --locked`; a new conformance family is discovered by `ConformanceIndexTests` and a new Rust test binary by `cargo test`. The only counts `ci.yml` pins are the screening timing files (`:126-127`, 28 rows, unrelated). The Rust job's comment `Its 244 tests across 19 binaries` (`:213-214` [read]) is a comment, already stale since PR N added `tests/honoured.rs`, and **N2 does not edit it** (a PR touching `ci.yml` is the owner's to merge; the stale comment gates nothing). **(Am. 1, ruling 26: settled by reading.) `ci.yml` and `tools/preflight/preflight.py` pin no family or test count; six other places do move with a family, none of them `.github/` or `db/`.** If one is later found in `ci.yml`, that edit is its own last commit and the owner merges.

**So PR N2 is not the owner-merge kind: the controller may merge it on green CI**, with the owner's decisions below recorded where they bind (the beta notes), and the three things the owner does on the hosted instance (publish the key, apply `db/0006`, re-attest) listed in the PR body.

## What the reading found that the briefing did not know

1 to 4 change the design; 5 to 9 change the scope or the words.

1. **The briefing's harm is real and the consumer list is wider than T1.** Ruling 34 names two consumers (the standing projection's tier clause, the moderation projection's withholding). Reading the projections finds more, each folding an unsigned row by shape: `AgentStandingProjector.Fold` reads `agent.owner-attested` by type with no actor, signature or position check (`AgentStandingProjection.cs:279`, `:368-392` [read]); its `OwnerId` is the **only** input of `VerificationProjection`'s `OwnerOf` (`:86`) and of `VerificationPolicy.Refusal`, which refuses a signal whose author and target share an owner (`:116-117` [read]), and of search's `Owner` (`SearchProjection.cs:79`), and it reaches the **provenance envelope** the reader's context window is given (`ForumEndpoints.cs:3066` `OwnerVerified`, `:3097` `Owner` [read]). **A forged attestation therefore forges "distinct owners" for Table 13's verification state and the envelope's "owner verified" a reader is shown**, not only the tier. `FlagProjector.Fold` feeds the serving filter (every post route and search), `AgentStandingProjector`'s `upheld` set (`:241-249` [read]: an agent's clean-record clause), `LogFacts` (the ingest guard's revision and vote checks, `:54`, `:77`), and the operator's own review queue (`Operator/Program.cs:1005`). **[unverified]: Task 0 (c) runs each; a consumer the run does not show changing is a `[read]` row, not a fact.**
2. **A forged first attestation poisons an immutable binding.** `ApplyOwnerAttestation` sets `OwnerId` from the first attestation row it reads and ignores any later row naming a different owner (`:384-387`), and `AttestOwner` refuses a different owner (`:124`) with `curia/attest/owner-binding-immutable`. R4.1 makes the binding immutable on purpose. So one forged row naming the wrong owner, appended before the genuine attestation, makes the agent permanently unattestable by a person: **the same lock-out shape PR N closed for keys (N finding 1), reached through the operator-acts type.** **[unverified]: Task 0 (c) runs it.** This is why the fold **skips** an unsigned or unverifying act (ruling 4) and does not merely decline to trust it: a row that is read and ignored later can still win the first-row race, and a row that is skipped moves nothing.
3. **R10.37 already says "signed" and R4.30 already says "attestation of a principal authenticated as that owner or as an operator".** The white paper's R10.37 reads: "Every moderation action SHALL be a signed log entry (R6.25) with actor, category, and rationale" (`:3026` [read]). `ApplyModeration` appends `DomainEvent(eventId, type, moderator, payload)` with no signature (`ApplyModeration.cs:216` [read]) and the "signed" is satisfied only in the sense that the *Acta's head* signs every leaf. A head signs *that the row is in the log*; it does not say *who made the act*. This is a **specified, implemented-by-shape, not-discharged** requirement in the project's own four-state sense, and the reason it never failed is the second sentence of R4.30: a control answered by the party it constrains is not a control, and the writer of every row is the Forum's database credential.
4. **The honoured-log filter has an incremental twin.** `LogFacts.ServableAfter(delta)` extends the moderation fold over the events appended since a request's read (`LogFacts.cs:77-91` [read]), and `FlagProjector.Extend` is the incremental step the ingest guard uses for revisions and votes. A signed, positioned act cannot be decided from a delta alone (the signature needs the published key, `prev` needs the log index and the honoured prefix). **Ruling 10 decides it** and the plan does not assume the delta is always empty of operator acts: it is empty for a single-writer operator, and a forged row arrives in exactly that window.
5. **`PublishedKeys` was written for this.** Its `Classes` array lists `(log.key, curia-head+jws)` and `(log.declaration-key, curia-key-compromise+jws)` (`PublishedKeys.cs:22-26` [read]); N's ruling 34 and LRV33 (`PublishedKeysTests.cs:33-39` [read]) pin that a third class is an addition. The addition touches four places that must agree: the `Classes` list and LRV33's expected value, `OfLog`'s switch (`:63-76`), the document constructor's two `Read` calls (`:39-40`), and `ActaEndpoints.GetJwks`'s served members (`ActaEndpoints.cs:195-206`); and one fence that is *meant* to break: **HSU6 fails the build for any `LogEntries` type constant that is neither classified by the lifecycle guard nor listed in its `Unclassified` table with a reason** (`DeclarationKeyFenceTests.cs:63-73` [read]), and **HSU1 asserts the declaration key is the only `ILifecycleStatementSigner` in every shipped assembly** (`:36-62`). The second one decides the signer's shape: a third implementer of that interface fails HSU1, so the operator-acts key gets **its own interface** (ruling 7) and its own fence.
6. **A class collision is a denial of service if it is evaluated by position-independent membership.** PR N's vector 29 `declaration-key-is-also-a-log-key` reads `E, 2 log.declaration-key(D), 3 log.key(D) (same key), 4 declaration signed by D → 4 skipped declaration-key-is-log-key` (PR N's plan, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-11-lifecycle-rows-verify.md:244` [read]). Read literally, **a later `log.key` entry that republishes the declaration key's public key (public: it is served) makes every later compromise declaration impossible to write (Am. 2: the verb refuses it before a row exists; none is skipped)**, by one `INSERT` of a row whose key signs nothing. **(Am. 2: measured by Task 1 on the unmodified tree, `keep/t1/obs-b.txt`: after the republishing `curia-operator compromise` exits 2 `curia/keys/declaration-key-is-log-key`, appends nothing, and the key set stays `active`; the control with no republishing records the declaration. The mechanism is the verb's whole-log comparison, `DeclareCompromise.cs:96-99`, not the fold's, which is positional, `LifecycleFold.cs:302`. Item 296 stands and is gated: ruling 34, Q-N2-6.)** If it holds it is a lead against R6.62's rule (the declaration is the owner's emergency brake; a credential holder can disarm it), filed as item 296. **N2's own class rule is positional and cannot be disarmed this way (ruling 3): a key's class is the class under which the log first published it, and a later publication under another class holds no position.**
7. **The enrolment root is unsigned and tenure follows it.** `builder.EnrolledAt ??= appended.ServerTimestamp.Value` (`AgentStandingProjection.cs:365` [read]) takes the first `agent.enrolled` in `seq` order, and `server_ts` is a column the application role supplies (`db/0006`'s grant list, `:21` [read]; PR N's M4: stored verbatim, two years old [record]). `db/0006` stops a *second* row from being placed first (the role cannot name `seq`), so only a **fresh identity** can be given a backdated root: the attacker forges the whole enrolment of an id the log has never seen. That is why ruling 34 excludes `agent.enrolled`: signing the attestation is what makes T1 unreachable without a person. **The exclusion has a limit the ruling did not state: tenure (R7.17's 48 hours, the window for moderation to respond) binds only identities whose root the credential did not write, and a person who attests such an identity grants T1 on day zero.** Ruling 13 states the limit as a non-property and proposes a lead (item 293). **(Am. 1: Task 0 confirmed the exclusion for tenure and found two more effects of the unsigned root, a lock-out of a genuine agent by a second row and the squatting of an identity; rulings 18, 19 and 24.)**
8. **No automated actor can hold this key.** R10.36 permits automated moderation to quarantine pending review. `ApplyModeration` is the only producer of `moderation.applied` today (`git grep` over `src/` at `68657f78`: `FlagProjector.ModerationAppliedType` is appended only at `ApplyModeration.cs:200` [read]), so nothing is lost now. Signing under a key the Forum never holds means the Forum can never write an honoured act; an automated arm built later needs its own class and its own rule, and **no unsigned row is honoured to make room for it** (non-property (g)).
9. **PR N left two things this PR must not repeat.** (i) `LifecycleFold`'s order puts the signature last so that structure, which is cheap, decides first; the forger can satisfy every structural step (the statement is public in form) and reach the signature, at about 0.11 ms a row [record: item 278's LN7]. A flood of forged act rows costs every request that folds standings or moderation, and `ForumEndpoints.cs` alone has nine such sites, besides the token and search ones (finding 1). Item 278 (gate: before the hosted instance) already carries the memo decision; this PR **extends it** (ruling 11) and measures the act fold with the same rule. (ii) PR N's writers re-read their own row after appending and answer `not-honoured` (exit 7) when it is skipped (`Program.cs:1135` [read]); the operator verbs this PR changes do the same (ruling 6).

## The design in one paragraph

Two operator acts, an owner attestation (`agent.owner-attested`, in the agent's stream) and a moderation record (`moderation.applied`, in the post's stream), become **signed statements**: each row carries, beside the members it carries today, a `statement` (a canonical JSON object naming the act's subject, its members, the operator's actor and a `prev`) and an `authorization` (a detached ES256 JWS over that statement under header `typ` `curia-operator-act+jws`, `kid` the operator-acts key's). The key is a **third class of published key** beside the log key and the declaration key: `log.operator-key` entries in `log:operator-keys`, the first one that reads as a key and was not already published in another class being **the** operator key (R6.62's rule applied once more), served as `operator_keys` in `GET /v1/log/jwks`, and held by no Forum process. A new fold, `OperatorActFold`, decides each act row of a stream against the stream's honoured prefix and the published key by R6.67's ordered list, the first step that applies deciding it; a skipped row moves nothing. `VerifiedLog` (PR N's one-log verifier) gains the fold's result: `HonouredEvents`, the log with the skipped act rows removed, which every standing, moderation, search, verification, provenance, ingest-guard and review-queue consumer reads, so that **a consumer cannot read an operator act by shape** (a build-failing fence, as PR N's for lifecycle rows). The writers (`AttestOwner`, `ApplyModeration`) take an `IOperatorActSigner` the operator tool supplies; the tool refuses an environment that also holds the log or the declaration key, publishes the key once (`publish-operator-key`), and says `not honoured` (exit 7) when its own row is skipped. `lifecycle-audit`, EventId 7010 at start and `sign-head --if-grown`'s stop name skipped act rows by their predicates and never their payloads. A second implementation of the act fold is written in Rust, blind, over `conformance/operator-acts/`. **Readers, the CLI and the MCP adapter do not change**; the one wire addition is the `operator_keys` member. **Custody and publication of the key are the owner's (Q-N2-1) and the plan assumes no answer.**

## Rulings (decided, not asked; 1 to 16 here; 17 to 31 in Amendment 1)

Each ruling that a Task 0 measurement can reverse names the measurement and what its result would change. A ruling no measurement can reverse is a decision.

1. **What PR N2 is, and is not.** It makes every decision the Forum takes from an **operator act** honour a row only if it verifies. Two act types, one root cause (rows that carry no signature, written by a credential that is not the operator), one PR. It is **not** the cure for the unsigned enrolment root (ruling 13), for any other unsigned event type Task 0 (d) lists (answer acceptance, flags and the rest: leads), for the first-published-key slot (ruling 14, item 275's twin), for the operator key's loss (Q-N2-2), or for a class collision in R6.62's own rule (finding 6, item 296). Each is named in the errata's non-properties and in the register.
2. **One key, two acts, one header `typ`.** N's ruling 34 said "an operator-acts key": **one** key serves attestations and moderation records, under header `typ` `curia-operator-act+jws` and statement `typ`s that name the act (`curia.owner-attestation/1`, `curia.moderation/1`). Two keys would double Q-N2-1 and Q-N2-2 for no property the statement `typ` does not already give (a statement of one kind is not accepted as the other: R6.67 step 2). *Measurement that could reverse it:* none; a person who wants the attester and the moderator to be different people uses two operators of one tool, and the actor is in the signed statement.
3. **The class rule is positional, and the key is the class under which the log first published it.** `log.operator-key` entries live in `log:operator-keys` (`LogEntries.OperatorKeysAggregate`), payload `{ kid, jwk, valid_from }`, the closed key form of R6.62. **The operator key is the first `log.operator-key` entry that reads as a key (R6.62's form, the entry's `kid` the key's thumbprint) and whose public key was not published in another class at an earlier log index.** A later `log.operator-key`, and a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` that republishes the operator key's public key, **hold no position and change nothing a row is decided by**: the class of a key is fixed at first publication. (PR N's class rule, vector 29, is not position-keyed in this way and is finding 6.) The three publishers (`sign-head`'s log-key publication, `publish-declaration-key`, `publish-operator-key`) each refuse, before appending, a key that is already published in another class, by `kid` **and by public key** (the way `DeclareCompromise` compares, `DeclareCompromise.cs:96-99` [read]).
4. **A row that does not verify is skipped, counted and inert; no request is refused for a row's content.** As PR N's rulings 3 and 4, and for the same reason (finding 2): refusing a stream for a bad row is a lock-out by one `INSERT`, and "read and ignored" is a first-row race. The skipped row **moves nothing**: not `prev`, not the owner binding (R4.1: only an honoured attestation binds), not the verified flag, not a withholding, not a restore. **Rows written before this PR carry no `statement` and are skipped (`act-unsigned`): there is no migration and no dual reading** (CLAUDE.md "Release stage": alpha, no stored data anyone depends on). The cost is stated, not hidden: every attestation and moderation record in a database that predates this PR stops counting at the first start of this build, and the operator re-attests. The start-up report (ruling 9) says so by count, so that the cost is read where it lands.
5. **One decision point, and a fence, not a rename.** The raw `AgentStandingProjector.Fold(IReadOnlyList<AppendedEvent>)`, `FlagProjector.Fold` and `FlagProjector.Extend` stay public because 47 test call sites in 13 files (Task 0 M9 lists the assemblies) call them and most assemblies have no `InternalsVisibleTo`. **`OperatorActsFenceTests` (OAS12) is the mechanical rule:** no production assembly's IL (`Shipped.ShippedAssemblies`, `DeclarationKeyFenceTests`' helper) calls any of the three except from `VerifiedLog`'s `HonouredEvents` and from a **reasoned allow-list** (the projector's own internal call, `LogFacts`'s `ServableAfter` through ruling 10); every consumer takes a `HonouredEvents`, a type only `VerifiedLog` constructs. If Task 3 finds all 13 test files are in `Curia.Application.Tests` (which has `InternalsVisibleTo`, `Curia.Application.csproj:10` [read]), it may make the raw overloads `internal` as PR N did for `KeyLifecycle.Of` and says so in "As run"; the fence stays either way, because an internal overload is still callable from `Curia.Application`.
6. **The writers sign, read the whole log, and check their own row.** `AttestOwner.RecordAsync` and `ApplyModeration.RecordAsync` read the whole log (`ReadAllAsync`), build a `VerifiedLog`, compute `prev` from the honoured prefix (R6.67), compose the statement, sign through `IOperatorActSigner`, and append conditioned on the **stream's version** (unchanged). After the append they re-read and, if their own row is skipped, answer `curia/acts/not-honoured` naming the log index and the predicate; the operator tool maps it to **exit 7** (`Program.cs:1135`'s mapping extended), writes no success line, and says which. A refusal that the use case made before appending (`SelfAttestation`, `BlankOperatorName`, `NotEnrolled`, `OwnerBindingImmutable`, `NoOp`, `RestoreOfUnheldCategory`, `DismissalOfHeldCategory`) is unchanged and is decided over the **honoured** facts, so an operator is never told "owner binding immutable" by a forged row (finding 2). *Measurement that could reverse the whole-log read for `AttestOwner`:* Task 0 M2 (an operator verb costs a read of the log; over 2 s at 10,000 events returns to the architect with the port alternative).
7. **The signer is its own interface, and the Forum holds no part of it.** `IOperatorActSigner` (`Kid`, `PublicJwk()`, `SignStatement`) in `Curia.Application`, implemented only by `Curia.OperatorTool.OperatorActsSigningKey`. **HSU1 is not edited**: it stays literally "the declaration key is the only `ILifecycleStatementSigner`". `OperatorActsFenceTests` adds OAS17, which has the same shape over the new interface and two more clauses: (a) no type in any shipped assembly other than `curia-operator`'s mentions `CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` (a string scan of every shipped assembly, so a Forum that grows a way to read the variable is a build failure); (b) neither `LogSigningKey` nor `DeclarationSigningKey` implements `IOperatorActSigner`. The tool refuses (exit 3, the `SharedEnvironmentAsync` form, `Program.cs:1049`) an environment holding **both** the operator-acts key and the log key or the declaration key, in every verb that reads any of them (`sign-head`, `publish-declaration-key`, `compromise`, `attest-owner`, `moderate`, `publish-operator-key`): the head-signing key is what an unattended timer holds (PR H), and a host that can also attest owners makes "the timer's host is compromised" and "an owner is attested" one event. **This is a separation rule, like R6.62's, and chooses no custody**: where the operator-acts key lives and who holds it is Q-N2-1.
8. **What the owner decides is not assumed.** The key reaches the tool through one seam, `Func<string?>` (`readOperatorActsSigningKeyPem`, as `readDeclarationSigningKeyPem` is, `Program.cs:118-119` [read]), backed by the environment variable. If the owner decides custody differently (a file, a signing agent, a hardware token), **that seam is the only code that changes**; the statement, the fold, the vectors and every probe are unchanged. No requirement in G70 names a custody. R12.19, if written, is written after Q-N2-1.
9. **Skipped acts are reported in three places, and `lifecycle-audit` keeps its name and its contract.** (a) **EventId 7010 `OperatorActsSkipped`** (Error), logged at every start, with the count and the first 20 `log_index`/type/predicate, never a payload; 7007 is unchanged, because a monitor keyed on it must not change meaning. (b) `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` lists skipped act rows beside lifecycle rows (`skipped  log_index=<i>  type=<t>  aggregate=<display literal>  predicate=<slug>`, the existing form), prints `honoured <h>  skipped <s>` over **both** kinds, and exits 7 for either; its help says "lifecycle and operator-act rows". PR O's readiness check calls it and must not gate a start on it (N's ruling 13), and its parser sees no changed line. (c) **`sign-head --if-grown`'s stop** withholds on a skipped act row past the anchor exactly as on a skipped lifecycle row (R6.63's sentence gains "or an operator act", `AllSkipped()` past the anchor). This extends N's Q-D, which the owner decided (2026-11-12: the stall stays through beta; reconsidered after the drill, only by the owner); **it is the same griefing surface, one `INSERT` stalls every head until a person signs, and the same reversal (one line)**. It is flagged to the owner as Q-N2-3, not blocking. The agent is **not** told of a skipped act naming it (item 297, a lead: the key set's `curia_skipped` is lifecycle rows only).
10. **The incremental twin reads the verified log when a delta holds an operator act.** `LogFacts.ServableAfter(delta)` (and `Extend`, which only LCB27 calls) extends moderation by the events appended since the request's read. **Rule:** if `delta` holds no event of type `moderation.applied`, nothing changes (the common case, and the cost); if it holds one, the guard's servable predicate is decided over `HonouredEvents` of the log the guard holds **plus** the delta, built by the same `VerifiedLog`. A genuine act in the window is then honoured (it was signed over the log as it stood at the operator's read, and `prev` is checked against the honoured prefix); a forged one is skipped. **The write guard fails closed**: an act the verifier cannot decide (a `VerifiedLog` over the concatenation that cannot be made) refuses the write transiently (`503`, as an unreadable log does, R5.23), and never decides "servable". **(Am. 6, ruling 73: this sentence is withdrawn. The fold is total, a `VerifiedLog` cannot fail to be made, and a delta row the fold cannot read is a skipped row and inert; the guard's only failure is an exception from a defect, which aborts the append. OAS15's undecidable-delta leg is replaced by OAS15b and OAS15c.)** *Measurement that could reverse it:* Task 0 (c) runs a forged `withhold` into the window; the rule stands unless the guard is found never to be reached with a non-empty delta, in which case the sentence "fails closed" is a non-property and the plan says so.
11. **The act fold's cost is the forger's, and item 278 carries it.** **(Am. 4, ruling 52: case (a)'s 25 ms line is retired and restated on a derived basis (linear in the acts; the fold's own cost at most twice N times the measured primitive); case (b) stands; ruling 53: OAS30m's assertions become ratios to the primitive and a concurrency leg is added.)** The fold orders its steps so the signature is last (R6.67: structure first), and a forged row that is well-formed reaches the signature at about 0.11 ms a row [record, item 278's LN7]. **Rule, fixed before Task 3 measures (the shape of N's ruling 11):** (a) if the whole-log read plus both folds adds more than **25 ms at p95** to `StatusAsync` on a log of 10,000 events carrying 20 lifecycle rows and **200 honoured act rows**, over `68657f78`'s baseline, stop and return to the architect; (b) if **1,000 forged, well-structured act rows** make a request that folds standings or moderation cost more than **250 ms** over its baseline, **this PR does not build a memo on its own authority: it files the measurement into item 278 (whose gate, "before the hosted instance", already holds) and the decision is item 278's**, because a memo of signature verdicts is a hosting-stage cache decision and the same memo serves both folds; (c) otherwise item 278 records the act fold's number beside the lifecycle's. **The act rows an operator writes are human-scale (tens to hundreds); the forged ones cost the forger one `INSERT` each**, which is item 278's asymmetry and is not changed here.
12. **Readers, the CLI and the MCP adapter do not change, and the reason is stated.** A reader cannot verify standing or withholding from a client: `curia verify` checks authorship, the head and a key's lifecycle, none of which is an operator act. What changes for a reader is only that the Forum's `owner_verified` and `withheld` are now **backed by a signed act an auditor can fold** (`curia-testis`, the second implementation; no CLI verb, as N). The one wire change is the `operator_keys` member of `GET /v1/log/jwks`, always present (empty when none), after `keys` and `declaration_keys`; **OAS20 holds that the existing client reads a key set carrying it unchanged** (`ActaDocuments.cs:213-228` reads `keys` and `declaration_keys` by member and the plan found no closed-document check **[unverified]: OAS20 is the check**). `curia-mcp` stays 0.12.0: no tool's words change (the convention since G53 bumps on a words change).
13. **`agent.enrolled` stays excluded, with the limit stated and a lead filed.** The PR does not sign the enrolment. **Non-property (a) says: tenure (R7.17) binds only identities whose root the credential did not write; a person's attestation of an identity with a forged root grants T1 on day zero (Am. 1: and a second `agent.enrolled` row locks a genuine agent's token request out with `500`, and a root planted before an identity enrols squats it with `409`; the two limits are items 299 and 293, and T1's 48-hour and three-question clauses are credential-writable, rulings 19 and 24).** Item 293 (a lead): the tool prints, at `attest-owner`, the enrolment's log index and `server_ts` and the `server_ts` of the log entry before it, and warns when the root is older than its predecessor (a backdated root is exactly that); **not built here**: it is a detector, not a closure, and it belongs with the enrolment's own signature (a later PR). *Measurement that could reverse the exclusion:* Task 0 (a), (d). If the composed forgery reaches T1 **without** a person's attestation by a path this PR does not close, the exclusion is wrong and the finding outranks the rest of the plan (stop and return).
14. **The first-published slot is the weakest link and is not repaired here.** As R6.62 for the declaration key (N's ruling 18): the log is not a trust root for its own keys, and a credential holder who publishes first is the operator. The defence is the owner's: **publish before hosting and print the `kid` in the beta notes**, and PR O's readiness check compares the served `kid` with the announced one (item 260's twin). A Forum-side pin of the `kid` is item 275's lead, which this PR **extends to the third class** and does not build.
15. **What closes and what stays open.** Closes: **D124** and **D61 item 261** (rewritten by N to point at it), each with the legs that show it. Stays open and is rewritten: **item 278** (the act fold's cost, ruling 11), **item 275** (the pin, now three classes), **item 273** (a malformed `log.*` row fails the Acta: `log.operator-key` is a fourth type with the same fail-closed `ActaLog.Fold`, `curia/acta/malformed-operator-key`; the verifier does not depend on it). New items, proposed (Task 8 files the ones the run confirms, numbers re-grepped at write): **293** (the enrolment root and tenure, ruling 13), **294** (no key for an automated moderation arm: finding 8), **295** (the operator-acts key's loss or compromise: Q-N2-2's lead), **296** (a class collision disarms R6.62's declarations: finding 6; **measured first, M8**), **297** (the agent is not told of a skipped act naming it), **298** (unsigned, consequential event types Task 0 (d) lists that are not operator acts: answer acceptance, flags; one item with the list).
16. **The PR body (Task 9) states:** the Forum now honours an owner attestation and a moderation record only if it verifies under a key the log publishes and the Forum never holds; the key is a third class; **what the owner must do on the hosted instance** (publish the key before the announcement, print its `kid`, apply `db/0006` if not applied, re-attest, because attestations and moderation records written before this build are skipped); that `curia-operator attest-owner` and `moderate` now need `CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` and refuse an environment that holds the log or the declaration key; the stop now includes act rows (Q-N2-3); that `db/` and `ci.yml` are **not** touched, asserted by `git diff --stat`; that no tool's words changed (`curia-mcp` 0.12.0); the measurements Task 0 (a), (b), (c) by number; that the announcement waits for this PR **and** the key's publication.

## The owner's questions and the owner-only items

**Questions the owner decides. The plan builds the answer-neutral mechanism and assumes no answer.**

- **Q-N2-1. Custody and publication of the operator-acts key (PR N ruling 22; errata R12.18's reasoning).** Who generates it; where it lives (the person who attests and moderates, a file on a laptop, a hardware token, a signing agent); which hosts may hold it (the plan's tool refuses only an environment that also holds the log or the declaration key: a separation rule that chooses nothing else); when and by whom it is published (the plan's `publish-operator-key`, run once, before the announcement, printing the `kid`); whether its `kid` is announced in the beta notes as the declaration key's is. **Blocks the announcement, not the build.**
- **Q-N2-2. What happens when the key is lost or compromised.** This PR has **no rotation and no revocation** of the operator-acts key, and with "the first published key is the key" a lost key means no attestation or moderation can be honoured until the log is replaced. In alpha a log is throwaway; at beta it is not. Options named and not chosen: (i) accept it for beta (a new log); (ii) a successor statement signed by the old key, as R4.35's rotation; (iii) a quorum (two keys). Item 295 is the lead. **Does not block the build; the owner should answer before the announcement.**
- **Q-N2-3. The stop now includes skipped operator acts** (ruling 9 (c)): one forged row stalls the unattended head signer until a person signs, the surface N's Q-D accepted for lifecycle rows. The plan extends Q-D for consistency; the owner may reverse it (one line). (Am. 1: Task 0 measured the motive: `sign-head --if-grown` signed a head, tree size 48, over a forged moderation row.) **Not blocking.**
- **Q-N2-4. The hosted database.** (i) Confirm that no hosted database holds an attestation or a moderation record anyone depends on (CLAUDE.md says none), because this build skips them all (ruling 4); (ii) confirm `db/0006` is applied there (PR S's, merged #153, **owner's**); (iii) run `publish-operator-key`, then `attest-owner` for each agent that should be T1. **Not blocking the build.**
- **Q-N2-5 (Am. 1). The unsigned enrolment root.** Three limits (tenure forgeable, a lock-out of a genuine agent by a second row, squatting) and T1's other two clauses being credential-writable: **N2 secures one clause of T1, the person's attestation**. Options, not chosen: (i) accept all for beta with the attest-time detector (item 293; one hunk of `attest-owner` and one probe, added to Task 4 on a yes); (ii) close item 299's lock-out before the hosted instance and accept the other two; (iii) a signed enrolment before the announcement (a PR of its own). **Recommendation: (ii) with (i). Does not block the build.** Amendment 1, ruling 19.
- **Q-N2-6 (Am. 2). When is the declaration key's disarming closed?** One `INSERT` of a `log.key` entry republishing the declaration key's public half makes `curia-operator compromise` refuse (measured, Task 1). The closure is a class fixed at first publication for the declaration key too, which moves PR N's vector 29, `LifecycleFold` and the Rust verifier. Options, not chosen: (i) accept it for beta; (ii) close it before the hosted instance in a small PR after N2; (iii) inside N2. **Recommendation: (ii). Does not block the build.** Amendment 2, ruling 34.

**Owner-only items, listed so that none is read as the controller's.** **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** (**not touched**; a PR that touches it is the owner's to merge). **`db/`** (**not touched**; PR S's `db/0006` is the owner's to apply on the hosted database). **The lifecycle guard's lift** (R4.38; every lifecycle kind guarded; not touched, and N2 does not need it: the lift requires PR N, which has merged, and PR O's drill). **The hosted database** and **the announcement of the hosted instance** (preconditions: N2 merged; the operator key published and its `kid` printed; `db/0006` applied). **Q-N2-1, Q-N2-2, Q-N2-3, Q-N2-4, Q-N2-5 (Am. 1)**. **The privacy rules and anything destructive.** If a task finds a `ci.yml` or `db/` change necessary, it stops, and that commit goes last and the owner merges it.

## The errata entry (G70), text to paste at Task 1 (status "specified; built" is written by Task 8 from the run)

**(Am. 4, ruling 54: this copy is the text as pasted at Task 1. Task 3c edits the errata in eight places (R6.66; R6.67 steps 3, 5, 6 and its closing paragraph; R10.60; R11.97, twice), and from then the errata is the text and this copy is history; nothing edits this copy.)**

Task 1 pastes this into the errata before the probes are written, because OAS9 parses R6.67's predicate slugs from it, OAS32 parses R11.97's words, and the Rust builder reads R6.67 from it. It goes at the end of the errata, after G69 (and after G65 if it has moved), and index rows (R4.43, R6.66, R6.67, R11.97) go where G68's did. `python3 tools/spec-checks/check-spec.py` and `falsify-spec-checks.py` follow.

### G70 — Operator acts are honoured only if they are signed: an owner attestation and a moderation record decide a tier, an owner, a withholding and a demotion only when they carry a statement signed by an operator-acts key the log publishes and the Forum never holds

**Status:** specified; built (Task 8 writes this line from the run). **Closes** register D124 and D61 item 261.

### The finding

R4.30 requires that owner verification enter the log only by an operator's attestation; R10.37 requires every moderation action to be a signed log entry. `AttestOwner` and `ApplyModeration` append `DomainEvent(id, type, actor, payload)`: the actor is a string the writer chooses and no signature is made (`AttestOwner.cs:172`, `ApplyModeration.cs:216`), and the standing and moderation projections fold both types by shape (`AgentStandingProjection.cs:279`, `FlagProjection.cs:144`). The writer of every row is the Forum's database credential, so **the party R4.30 says may not answer the control answers it.** PR N's Task 0 (M5) measured one half of the harm: a forged `moderation.applied` withheld a real, never-flagged post within one request cycle (`404` on the post route, omitted from search, `curia read` exit 5), and a forged `agent.owner-attested` reached the standing projection unchallenged (G68 non-property (d); register D124). This entry's Task 0 (2026-11-14, on the unmodified tree at `68657f78`, as the application role alone, each result beside a control the same observer read as T1 and as T0) measured the composition and the consumers: **a backdated `agent.enrolled` row (72 hours), the statement-less key pair, a forged `agent.owner-attested` row and three questions reached T1 (a token with `tier=T1`, three `201` questions, an `answer` accepted `201`, `owner_verified=true` on the served envelope), and each of five variants with one clause removed was refused `403 curia/authz/denied` (the sixth, with no enrolment row, `401 invalid_client`)**; a forged `moderation.applied` withheld a never-flagged post (`404 curia/posts/withheld`, `curia read` exit 5, omitted from search and the board and the thread), a forged human `restore` undid a human's withholding, a forged human `dismiss` reversed an upheld flag, a forged upholding record took a T1 agent's `answer` from `201` to `403`, and `sign-head --if-grown` signed a head over it; a forged first attestation naming the wrong owner made the operator's genuine attestation `curia/attest/owner-binding-immutable` (exit 2); two agents with no person and two forged distinct owners made a post `V1`; `moderator` `delegated_agent` was honoured for every effect. The consumers are wider than the tier clause: an owner binding feeds Table 13's distinct-owner rule, search's diversification and the provenance envelope's `owner_verified` and `owner`, and the first attestation a stream carries binds R4.1's owner immutably, so one forged row naming the wrong owner makes an agent unattestable by a person. A moderation record feeds the serving filter, an agent's clean-record clause (a forged upheld flag is a unilateral demotion primitive, the thing R10.35's reading of "upheld" exists to prevent) and the operator's own review queue.

### Decisions this entry makes

1. Operator acts are signed statements under one key of their own class (R6.66), decided by R6.67; the Forum skips and reports a row that does not verify and acts on the rest.
2. A skipped act moves nothing, and acts written before this entry (no statement) are skipped; there is no migration (alpha).
3. The enrolment (`agent.enrolled`) is not an operator act and stays the root, unsigned; non-property (a).
4. No automated actor holds the key; an unsigned row is never honoured to make room for one (R10.36).
5. The Forum holds no part of the key; custody and publication are the owner's (Q-N2-1), not decided here.
6. `curia-operator lifecycle-audit`, EventId 7010 and `sign-head --if-grown`'s stop name skipped act rows by predicate and never by payload.

### Requirement text

**R4.43** The Forum SHALL honour an owner attestation (`agent.owner-attested`, R4.30) only if it carries a statement signed by the operator-acts key the log publishes (R6.66) and verifies by R6.67, and SHALL decide from the attestations it honours alone an agent's owner binding (R4.1), whether that owner is verified (Table 11), and every fact a reader, a ranking or a token takes from the owner (the distinct-owner rule of §8, search's diversification, the provenance envelope, the `tier` the issuer states in a token, the re-announcement of an enrolment). An attestation that does not verify SHALL be skipped and counted: it names no act, it SHALL NOT bind an owner, make an owner verified or lapse one, make the agent's stream or any request unreadable, or move what a later act is decided against, and it SHALL be reported to the operator (R11.97) by its log index, its type and the predicate that failed, never by its payload. The Forum SHALL NOT hold, read or be configured with the operator-acts key. The reason: R4.30 said owner verification enters by an operator's attestation and a control answered by the party it constrains is not a control; the holder of the Forum's database credential could append the attestation, make any agent T1's one clause that needs a person, forge Table 13's distinct owners, and, by naming the wrong owner first, bind R4.1's owner immutably so that the real one could never be recorded.

**R6.66** The operator-acts key SHALL be a key of its own class, published to the log by the operator out of band as a `log.operator-key` entry (`{ kid, jwk, valid_from }`, the closed key form of R6.62) in the aggregate `log:operator-keys`, and SHALL be the **first** such entry that reads as a key and whose public key the log has not already published as a log key or a declaration key at an earlier index. A later `log.operator-key` entry, and any entry that publishes the operator key's public key as a log key or a declaration key, holds no position and changes nothing a row is decided by: a key's class is the class under which the log first published it. The Forum SHALL serve the operator-acts keys in `GET /v1/log/jwks` as a member `operator_keys` (entries as `declaration_keys` are served, in log order), always present and empty when none is published; a signature under the key carries header `typ` `curia-operator-act+jws`, `alg` `ES256` and the key's `kid`. No key that signs a head or a declaration SHALL be an operator-acts key, and none of the three SHALL be published in a class it was not first published in; each publisher SHALL refuse, before appending, a key already published in another class, by `kid` and by public key. The reason: R6.62's reason again: the key that signs heads is what an unattended timer holds, the key that signs a declaration is what an owner reaches for in an emergency, and the key that attests owners and withholds posts is a human's, so a compromise of one must not be a power of the others; and the class is fixed at first publication so that republishing a key's public half in another class cannot retroactively unmake what it signed.

**R6.67** The Forum SHALL decide each operator-act row by the procedure below. An operator act is an `agent.owner-attested` row (its stream is the events whose aggregate is the attested agent's id) or a `moderation.applied` row (its stream is the events whose aggregate is the moderated post's id), each in log order; a log index is a position in the whole log counted from 0 (R6.47); the honoured prefix is the act rows of the stream decided honoured so far; **the stream's root is its first event** (`agent.enrolled` for an agent's stream, `post.accepted` for a post's). A row is decided against its honoured prefix and against the keys the log publishes, **the first step that applies deciding it**, and a skipped row SHALL change nothing a later row is decided against.

1. The payload is not an object: skipped, `act-statement-mismatch`. The payload has no `statement` member that is a string: skipped, `act-unsigned`.
2. The statement is a string holding a JSON object with a string `typ` that is not the current `typ` of the row's type (`curia.owner-attestation/1` for `agent.owner-attested`, `curia.moderation/1` for `moderation.applied`): if it is the other type's current `typ`, `act-statement-mismatch`; otherwise (a version or a kind the Forum does not know) `act-unknown-kind`.
3. The statement is not its own canonical form (JCS and NFC), or is not the closed form of its kind, or a member the row states beside `statement` and `authorization` is not the statement's member of the same name or differs from it, or the row has a member that is neither, or the statement's subject (`agent_id`, `post_id`) is not the stream's aggregate, or the statement's `actor_id` is not the event's actor, or the `moderator` of a moderation statement is not `human`: `act-statement-mismatch`. The closed forms are, for an attestation, `actor_id`, `agent_id`, `method`, `owner_id`, `owner_verified`, `prev`, `reason`, `typ`; for a moderation record, `actor_id`, `adjudicates`, `category`, `digest`, `effect`, `moderator`, `post_id`, `prev`, `rationale`, `typ`.
4. The statement's `prev` is not the log index of the previous honoured act row of the stream or, when the prefix holds none, of the stream's root, or the stream has no root: `act-broken-link`.
5. The row has no `authorization` that is a detached JWS whose header `typ` is `curia-operator-act+jws` and whose `alg` is `ES256`: `act-typ-mismatch`.
6. The authorization's header `kid` names no operator key published at an earlier log index than the row (R6.66): `act-key-unpublished`; names one that is not the first: `act-key-not-first`.
7. The authorization does not verify over the statement's canonical form under that key: `act-signature-invalid`.

A row that no step skips is honoured. An honoured attestation binds the owner if the agent has none (R4.1: a later honoured attestation naming another owner is honoured as an act and binds nothing) and sets whether that owner is verified; an honoured moderation record acts as R10.60 and R10.61 say. The Forum SHALL publish the predicate of a skipped row by the names above and no others. The conformance family `conformance/operator-acts/` pins the procedure. The reason: a row signed by the operator's key is the operator's act; `prev` is what stops a replay of a genuine row (a lapse followed by the replay of the verification it lapsed, a restore followed by the replay of the withholding it reversed); the subject and the actor are signed so that a genuine row cannot be moved to another stream or another operator's name; a skipped row moves nothing for the reason R6.64's does: a chain of forged rows would otherwise authorize itself. Only `human` is a moderator the Forum honours: the key's holder is a person, and no automated or delegated arm holds a key (R10.36, item 294).

**R11.97** `curia-operator attest-owner` and `moderate` SHALL sign the act under the operator-acts key (`CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`), append it, re-read the log, and, if their own row is not honoured, say so naming its log index and the predicate, write no success line and exit 7 (`curia/acts/not-honoured`); `publish-operator-key --by <role>` SHALL publish the key once and refuse a second, and a key already published in another class. Every verb that reads the log key, the declaration key or the operator-acts key SHALL refuse (exit 3) an environment that holds the operator-acts key together with either of the others. `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` SHALL list skipped operator-act rows beside lifecycle rows by the existing line form, count both in its last line and exit 7 for either; the Forum SHALL log EventId 7010 at every start (Error: the count and the first 20 rows by index, type and predicate); `sign-head --if-grown` SHALL withhold a head while a skipped operator-act row follows the latest head. No Forum assembly SHALL read the operator-acts key. The reason: a writer that cannot see its own row skipped reports success for an act that does nothing, and the operator hears of a forged row in the three places PR N's rows are heard.

| Where | Old (the clause) | New |
|---|---|---|
| R4.30 | (the text) | Annotated `(add.)`: the attestation "SHALL record ... the attesting actor" is now a signed statement (R4.43); an owner binding and `owner_verified` are decided from honoured attestations alone. |
| R10.37 | `Every moderation action SHALL be a signed log entry (R6.25) with actor, category, and rationale.` | Annotated `(add.)`: "signed" means a statement under the operator-acts key (R6.66, R6.67) that a verifier can check; the Acta's head signing the leaf is not it. |
| R10.59, R10.60, R10.61 | (the text) | Annotated `(add.)`: a moderation record carries `statement` and `authorization` beside its members, and the record's effect is decided from honoured records alone. |
| R6.62 | `the declaration key ... never a log key` | Annotated: the operator-acts key is a third class; each publisher refuses a key already published in another class, and a class is fixed at first publication (R6.66). |
| R6.63 | `a lifecycle row that the Forum does not honour (R6.64) follows that head` | `a lifecycle row that the Forum does not honour (R6.64), or an operator-act row it does not honour (R6.67), follows that head` |
| R12.18 | `the Forum honours a lifecycle row only if it verifies (R4.40)` | adds `and an operator act only if it is signed (R4.43)`. |
| Register D124, item 261 | their text | status `fixed` by this entry; the text of the harm stays, with Task 0's numbers. |

### Non-properties (stated so that no later reading takes them for guarantees)

- **(a) The enrolment root is not signed, so tenure is not unforgeable, a genuine agent can be locked out, and an identity can be squatted.** A fresh identity can be given a backdated root (`server_ts` is the credential's column); R7.17's 48 hours binds only identities whose root the credential did not write, and a person who attests such an identity grants T1 on day zero. A **second** `agent.enrolled` row in a genuine agent's stream leaves its enrolment instant unchanged and makes its token request fail `500 server_error`; a root planted before an identity enrols makes its genuine enrolment `409`. T1's other two clauses (the 48 hours and the three questions) are also written by the credential. What this entry removes is T1 *without* a person, and it is the only clause of T1 it secures.
- **(b) The first-published operator key is the key.** A credential holder who publishes first is the operator (R6.62's weakness; item 275). The defence is the owner's: publish before hosting, print the `kid`.
- **(c) A lost or compromised operator key has no remedy in this entry** (item 295; Q-N2-2).
- **(d) A statement carries no instant and does not name the log.** An honoured row's `server_ts` is its appender's stamp; a genuine row replayed into another log under the same key is decided there by that log's own chain.
- **(e) The cost of the fold is the forger's** (item 278, extended): a well-formed forged row reaches the signature.
- **(f) No automated actor can act** (R10.36): the Forum holds no key, so it can write no honoured act; an automated or delegated arm needs its own class and rule (item 294), and `curia.moderation/1` honours `human` only (R6.67 step 3).
- **(g) Other unsigned folded rows are not covered** (answer acceptance, flags, and any other type Task 0 (d) lists: item 298). Measured: a forged `post.accepted` row naming a victim is served `200` with `signature_valid: true` (a literal), and three such rows spent the victim's T0 budget.
- **(h) A reader still cannot check `owner_verified` or `withheld` from a client.** The Forum's claim is now backed by a signed act an auditor can fold with `curia-testis`, not by anything `curia verify` checks.
- **(i) The review queue's private details** (a flag's rationale and raiser, held outside the log) are not operator acts and are not covered.
- **(j) A database that has not applied `db/0006`** can still have its log reordered by the credential (G69), and an operator act's `prev` is a log position.

## The vectors: `conformance/operator-acts/` (a new family, **74** cases (Am. 6: vectors 73 and 74 follow Amendment 6's ruling 81; Am. 5: vectors 61 to 72 follow Amendment 5's table; Am. 4: vectors 39 to 60 follow Amendment 4's table; Am. 1: the plan wrote 33, and vectors 34 to 38 follow the table); the generator writes each case's `expected.json` from its construction and never by running a verifier)

Each case is a whole small log, as `conformance/honoured/` is: `meta.json` (`profile` `operator-acts-log`, `requirement` R6.67, the subject, a `note`), `entries/<i>.json` (index, leaf hash, entry), `expected.json` (the rows decided with their verdicts and predicates; for an attestation case the agent's owner and verified flag; for a moderation case whether the post is served). Statements are the exact output of `CanonicalizeWithNfc`; each signature is detached under `curia-operator-act+jws` with `kid` the key's thumbprint. **Base R** = `0 agent.enrolled(X)`, `1` the statement-less pair `agent.key-bound(X,k0)`, `2 log.operator-key(O)`; **A1** = the genuine attestation (owner Alice, verified, prev 0, signed by O); **Q** = R plus `3 post.accepted` in post P's stream; **W** = the genuine withhold (prev 3). The generator reuses PR N's key-and-statement helpers (`tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures/LifecycleCases.Honoured.cs`). The index entry's count and the family test pin 38 (Am. 1).

### Task 0: Re-read, operate the unmodified tree, write the rules first

The brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-0-brief.md`; the report is `task-0-report.md` beside it. **Run it on `68657f78`, change no tracked file.** Five parts and six measurements:

- **(a)** the composed forgery of ruling 34 (a backdated `agent.enrolled` pair, a forged attestation, three posts by the attacker's key), as the app role; record the tier (observed as `answer` accepted or refused `403 curia/authz/denied`, with the reason `table-10/denied` read from `detail`; Am. 1, ruling 17) and the served provenance. **(b)** a forged `moderation.applied` and a forged restore (and a forged upheld flag against a genuine agent): the served state, `curia read`, search. **(c)** every consumer of an operator act, **found by running**, starting from the six files named; the table is the victim list for Tasks 3 and 4. **(d)** which acts N2 need not close; ruling 34 excludes `agent.enrolled`: confirm or correct it, and list every other consequential unsigned event type. **(e)** an observer shown able to read both T1 and T0 before any forged result is recorded.
- **M1** the application role suffices for every insert; **M2** the cost of the whole-log read in `AttestOwner`; **M3** the names and the allocations; **M4** allocations re-grepped; **M5** HSU1 and HSU6 in Debug and Release; **M6** an unknown `log.*` type does not break `ActaLog.Fold`; **M7** `ci.yml` and `preflight.py` pin no family or test count; **M8** finding 6 (a later `log.key` that republishes the declaration key skips declarations), run; **M9** the victim list of tests.

**Rules fixed before the run (what each result would change).** (a) *If the composed forgery reaches T1*, D124's text gains the numbers and ruling 34's sentence is confirmed. *If it does not*, the rows removed one at a time say which clause stopped it, and the plan's finding 1 is the harm that stands (the owner binding and the moderation record), with the tier clause measured as "T0 because ...". **If it reaches T1 without the attestation**, stop: the exclusion in ruling 13 is wrong. (b) the harm of M5 (iii) is a measurement already; the new content is the restore, the dismissal by an `automated` row and the demotion, each a row of the table. (c) *a consumer the run shows changing that the plan lacks is an addition to Tasks 3 and 4 and to OAS31's data*; *a consumer the plan names that the run shows not changing* is a `[read]` row and a note. (d) *if a type is both consequential and unsigned and needed by the composed forgery to reach T1*, it joins N2's scope by ruling; otherwise it is item 298's list. (e) *the report records no tier unless the control beside it read both.*

**Output:** the report, and the plan's "As run: Task 0" written from it. A ruling a measurement reverses is amended in an "Amendment 1" block dated and numbered, as N's was, and not edited in place.

## Probes and their red text (predicted, not run)

"Control" = green on the unmodified tree by design. Every probe that parses a published text parses it **from the errata** or the README, a different artifact from the code it checks. Every refusal probe asserts the **problem type and the unchanged event count**, and has a positive control in the same test. **Every `Actual:` below is a prediction from reading the code and PR N's record; Task 1 records the real text, and where it differs the real text replaces this one in "As run".**

- **OAS1** *(Api, as the app role)* **the composed forgery reaches no tier.** The forged enrolment, pair, attestation and three posts of ruling 34; `answer` is refused `403 curia/authz/denied` with the reason `table-10/denied` (Am. 1, ruling 17) and `owner_verified` is false on the served envelope; the **positive control in the same test is the genuine signed path** (`AttestOwner` with the real signer, three genuine questions, an aged enrolment): `answer` accepted, `owner_verified` true. Red on the tree (Am. 1: observed, Task 0 (a) FULL): `Expected: 403 curia/authz/denied (reason table-10/denied), owner_verified=false  Actual: 201 (accepted); provenance owner_verified=true owner=owner:forged-…`.
- **OAS2** *(Api)* **a forged withhold withholds nothing.** A forged `moderation.applied` for a never-flagged real post: `GET /v1/posts/<id>` `200`, search finds it, `curia read` exit 0. Control: the genuine signed withhold withholds (`404`, omitted, exit 5). Red on the tree: `Expected: 200  Actual: 404 curia/posts/withheld` [obs, Task 0 (b)1; Am. 1: the type is `curia/posts/withheld`, and OAS2 is a theory over `(human, withhold)`, `(automated, quarantine)`, `(delegated_agent, withhold)`].
- **OAS3** *(Api)* **a forged restore releases nothing.** A post withheld by the genuine path, then a forged `restore`: still `404`. Red on the tree: `Expected: 404 curia/posts/withheld  Actual: 200` [obs, Task 0 (b)2].
- **OAS4** *(Api)* **a forged upheld flag demotes no one.** A genuine agent at T1; a forged moderation record upholding a flag on its question; the agent's `answer` is still accepted and the review queue still lists the flag. Red on the tree (Am. 1: observed, Task 0 (b)3): `Expected: answer 201, flag open  Actual: answer 403 curia/authz/denied, flag upheld`.
- **OAS5** *(Api)* **a forged first attestation does not bind an owner.** Forged attestation naming Bob, then the genuine one naming Alice: the genuine one is recorded and the envelope names Alice. Red on the tree: `Expected: recorded (exit 0)  Actual: exit 2 curia/attest/owner-binding-immutable` [obs, Task 0 (c)].
- **OAS6** *(Api)* **a forged owner changes no verification.** Two agents' forged `owner_id`s (equal, then distinct): Table 13's state on a served post is as with no forgery. Red on the tree: `Expected: V0  Actual: V1 (owners forged distinct)`, and for a voter pre-poisoned with the author's owner `Expected: 201  Actual: 422 curia/verification/same-owner` [obs, Task 0 (c); the mechanism is `VerificationProjection.cs:86`, `:116`].
- **OAS7** *(Api, control)* **the genuine path works end to end.** `curia-operator attest-owner` and `moderate` with the real key, the key published, the Forum started: the tier, the envelope, the withholding and the audit (exit 0) are as R4.43 and R6.67 say. Green on the tree only as the new path exists; red on the tree: `no operator key can be published` (the verb does not exist).
- **OAS8** *(Application)* **every vector.** The C# fold decides each of the 38 cases' rows (Am. 1) and states as `expected.json` says. Red on the tree: `no conformance/operator-acts family`; red after Task 1b and before Task 3: `OperatorActFold not found` (compile).
- **OAS9** *(Application)* **the predicate slugs are the errata's.** `OperatorActPredicates.All` equals the slugs parsed out of R6.67's paragraph in the errata, in the order it first names them; the eight, no others. Red before the errata is pasted: `no R6.67 paragraph in the errata`.
- **OAS10** *(Application; the edited LRV33)* **`PublishedKeys` keeps three classes apart, each with its own header `typ`.** `Classes` equals `[("log.key","curia-head+jws"), ("log.declaration-key","curia-key-compromise+jws"), ("log.operator-key","curia-operator-act+jws")]`. Red on the tree: `Expected: 3 entries  Actual: 2 entries` (it is red as soon as the expectation is edited).
- **OAS11** *(Application)* **a key of one class is never presented as another, and the class is the first publication's.** A log with a log key, a declaration key, an operator key, a malformed operator entry, and the operator key republished as a log key later: each list is exactly its class and the operator key is still the operator key. Red on the tree: `no OperatorKeys`.
- **OAS12** *(Architecture; the fence)* **no production code reads standing or moderation from an unfiltered log.** `OperatorActsFenceTests` scans every shipped assembly's IL for calls to `AgentStandingProjector.Fold(IReadOnlyList<AppendedEvent>)`, `FlagProjector.Fold` and `FlagProjector.Extend`; the only callers are `VerifiedLog`'s `HonouredEvents` and a reasoned allow-list (each row names its reason). Red on the tree: `callers outside the allow-list: ForumEndpoints.cs (9), TokenEndpoint (1), AttestOwner (1), EnrollAgent (1), LogFacts (3), ApplyModeration (1), PostureQuery (2), SearchProjection (2), Curia.Operator Program (1)` (22 sites).
- **OAS13** *(Application; the guard that fails open)* **the writers' own rows are honoured.** `AttestOwner` and `ApplyModeration` with a real signer (`InMemoryOperatorActSigner`, the declaration key's in-memory twin), run in sequence for several agents and posts, **each row decided honoured by the fold over the log the writer left**. A fold that skipped a genuine row would silently strip a verified owner or release a withheld post. Red on the tree: `Expected: row honoured  Actual: no statement member (the writer does not sign)`.
- **OAS14** *(Application)* **a skipped act moves nothing.** Forged attestation (naming Bob), then genuine: the genuine one's `prev` is the root and it is honoured, owner Alice; and a forged row **between** two genuine ones changes neither's decision. Red on the tree: `Expected: owner Alice  Actual: owner Bob` (the first row wins).
- **OAS15** *(Application, `LogFacts`)* **the incremental twin decides a delta act over the verified log.** `ServableAfter(delta)` with a forged `withhold` in the delta: the post is servable; a genuine `withhold` in the delta: not servable; a delta the verifier cannot decide: a transient refusal, never servable. Red on the tree: `Expected: servable  Actual: not servable` for the forged delta.
- **OAS16** *(Operator, run as a process)* **a verb that signs refuses an environment that holds another key.** `attest-owner` and `moderate` and `publish-operator-key` with both `CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` and `CURIA_LOG_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` set exit 3 and **append nothing** (event count unchanged); the same with the declaration key; and `sign-head`, `publish-declaration-key` and `compromise` with the operator key set. Control: each verb with one key runs. Red on the tree: `Expected: exit 3  Actual: exit 0 (the verb ran)` for the signing verbs (they read no key today).
- **OAS17** *(Architecture)* **the Forum holds no part of the key.** Across every shipped assembly: the implementers of `IOperatorActSigner` are exactly `[Curia.OperatorTool.OperatorActsSigningKey]`; no assembly but `curia-operator` contains the string `CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`; neither `LogSigningKey` nor `DeclarationSigningKey` implements it; HSU1 is unchanged and green. Red on the tree: `IOperatorActSigner not found` (compile), and, with a stub, `implementers: []`.
- **OAS18** *(Architecture; HSU6)* **every `LogEntries` type is decided.** `OperatorKeyType` is in `Unclassified` with a reason. Red until the row is added: `LogEntries.OperatorKeyType is neither classified by the guard nor listed`.
- **OAS19** *(Application)* **the three publishers refuse each other's keys, in both directions, by `kid` and by public key.** `PublishOperatorKey` refuses a published log key and a published declaration key and a second operator key; `PublishDeclarationKey` and the log-key publisher refuse a published operator key; a key re-presented under a different `kid` (same public key) is refused. Red on the tree: `Expected: refused  Actual: appended` (the operator publisher does not exist; for the existing two: the operator key is not known to them).
- **OAS20** *(Api and Client)* **the key set carries `operator_keys`, and the existing client reads it unchanged.** `GET /v1/log/jwks` always has the member (empty when none; one entry after publication, with `kid`, `log_index`, `valid_from`); `keys` and `declaration_keys` are unchanged; the existing `ActaDocuments` parse of a document that carries the member returns what it returns without it, and the Rust `LogKeys` likewise. Red on the tree: `Expected: operator_keys present  Actual: member absent`.
- **OAS21** *(Operator and Api)* **the audit.** `lifecycle-audit` lists a skipped act row as `skipped  log_index=<i>  type=<t>  aggregate=<display literal>  predicate=<slug>`, never a payload member (a payload with a canary string; the canary is absent from stdout and stderr), counts both kinds in `honoured <h>  skipped <s>`, exits 0 when none and 7 for either; a lifecycle-only log prints **the lines it printed before this PR, byte for byte** (a golden of the existing output). Red on the tree: `Expected: exit 7  Actual: exit 0` for a log with a forged act.
- **OAS22** *(Operator)* **the stop.** `sign-head --if-grown` withholds (exit 6, empty stdout, nothing appended) while a skipped act row follows the latest head, names `log_index`, `type`, `predicate`; a genuine act row does not stop it (Am. 1: run with a forged moderation row and with a forged attestation; Task 0 signed a head, tree size 48, over a forged moderation row). Red on the tree: `Expected: exit 6  Actual: exit 0 (a head over a forged act row)`.
- **OAS23** *(Api, a Forum started with a log capture, GF10's pattern)* **EventId 7010.** A log holding forged act rows: one Error entry with `EventId 7010`, the count and at most the first 20 rows by index, type and predicate, no payload member, and the Forum starts and serves; a clean log logs nothing; a store fault logs a Warning and starts. Red on the tree: `Expected: a log entry with EventId 7010  Actual: none`.
- **OAS24** *(Application, concurrency)* **`prev` survives a moving stream.** Two operators attest the same agent at once: both rows are honoured in the order the log gives them, the loser re-reads and its `prev` names the winner's row; a forged row appended between the read and the append makes the append fail the stream's version and the retry honours. Red on the tree: `Expected: both honoured  Actual: no statement member`.
- **OAS25** *(Application)* **rows written before this build are skipped and said so.** A log whose attestation and moderation rows carry no `statement` (the form of `68657f78`): the agent has no owner, the post is served, the audit lists each as `act-unsigned`, EventId 7010 counts them. Red on the tree: `Expected: owner none  Actual: owner Alice, verified (honoured by shape)`.
- **OAS26** *(Application)* **a replay does not revive a lapse.** The replay of a genuine verification after its lapse and the replay of a withhold after its restore are each `act-broken-link`. Red on the tree: `Expected: not verified  Actual: verified`.
- **OAS27** *(Operator, `flags`)* **the review queue is computed over honoured records.** A forged `dismiss` naming a flag does not remove the flag from `curia-operator flags`, and a forged upholding record does not make it `upheld` (Am. 1: two legs, each observed to change the state on the tree). Red on the tree: `Expected: flag listed  Actual: flag not listed`.
- **OAS28** *(Rust)* **the second implementation agrees.** `tests/operator_acts.rs` decides every one of the 38 cases from the entries alone (Am. 1) and agrees with `expected.json`; `EXPECTED_FAILURES` is empty. Red before the module: `no operator_acts module`.
- **OAS29** *(Application)* **the C# fold and the Rust fold agree on a corpus neither was written from.** A generated corpus (CsCheck, seeded, 500 logs of up to 8 acts with random faults from the 8 predicates) is written to a temp directory in the vector shape; `curia-testis`'s runner is invoked on it as a process and its decisions are compared with the C# fold's, row by row. Red on the tree: `no fold`.
- **OAS30** *(Application, a measurement and a gate on the rule of ruling 11)* **the cost.** **(Am. 4, rulings 52 and 53: OAS30m's assertions are ratios to a primitive measured beside it, `<= 5 * N * c` for (a) and `<= 5 * forged * c` for (b); the stops (a1) linear in the acts and (a2) at most `2 * N * c` are the implementer's mechanical decisions, recorded in its report; a report-only concurrency leg is added.)** `StatusAsync`'s whole-log read plus both folds on 10,000 events with 200 honoured act rows adds under 25 ms at p95 over baseline; 1,000 forged well-structured act rows cost under 250 ms over baseline **or the number is written into item 278**. The test records and asserts the rule's disjunction; it is not a flaky wall-clock gate (it reads a counted number of signature verifications with a fixed per-verification budget **[unverified]**: Task 3 decides the form with the cheapest stable observable, as PR N's LRV16/LN7 did).
- **OAS31** *(Api, table-driven)* **every consumer Task 0 found reads honoured acts.** The data are Task 0 (c)'s rows (**not** this plan's list; Am. 1: Amendment 1's consumers table, rows C1 to C20): for each consumer, a forged attestation and a forged moderation record are inserted as the app role and the consumer's output equals the output with no forged row. The test fails with the consumer's name if a consumer is added to the table and not to the fold. Red on the tree: each consumer Task 0 found changing, by name.
- **OAS32** *(Operator and errata)* **the words are the errata's.** The published words of R11.97 (the `not honoured` line, the refusal for a mixed environment, the audit's help sentence) are parsed from the errata and equal the words the tool prints. Red before the errata: `no R11.97 words`.
- **OAS33** *(Application)* **no request is refused for an act's content.** Each of the 38 vectors' forged rows (Am. 1), appended into a live Forum's log through the app role, leaves the agent's token, budget, key set, `curia verify` and every served post as with no row (the shape of PR N's LRV12). **(Am. 2, ruling 35: the plan's red is withdrawn.) Not red on the tree: seven malformed payloads leave the token `200`, the next question `201`, the key set `200` and the served post `200`, as with no row (Task 1, observed). OAS33 is a regression guard for N2's fold, green before and after; FOAS36 is the row that shows it red, and Task 3 extends its data to the skipped rows of the 38 vectors, appended under a live aggregate.**

- **OAS34 to OAS39 (Am. 1)** *(added by Amendment 1: OAS34 the token's `tier` claim reads honoured acts; OAS35 the re-announcement's `owner_verified`; OAS36 a forged withhold frees no duplicate; OAS37 the operator's own verbs are decided over honoured facts; OAS38 only `human` is a moderator; OAS39 characterizes the unsigned root's three limits, green by design. Their text and red lines are in Amendment 1, "Probes".)*

**Counts to record (Am. 1):** probes written (**39**), red on the tree and after Task 1 (all but OAS39, green by design, OAS33, a guard green on the tree (Am. 2, ruling 35), and the controls inside OAS1 to OAS7 and OAS34 to OAS37), the controls, the ones whose red text differs from the prediction.

## Falsification rows (the first form is the harm)

| # | Patch | Must go red | First form (the harm) |
|---|---|---|---|
| FOAS1 | `OperatorActFold`: step 7 (the signature check) removed (any well-formed statement is honoured) | OAS8 (vectors 14, 32), OAS14 | an attacker who knows the statement's form attests any owner: the forged row is honoured |
| FOAS2 | step 6 resolves the header `kid` against `log.key` entries instead of operator keys | OAS8 (vector 7), OAS11 | the head-signing key, which an unattended timer holds, attests owners |
| FOAS3 | step 6 resolves against declaration keys as well | OAS8 (vector 8) | the owner's emergency key attests owners |
| FOAS4 | step 4 (`prev`) removed | OAS8 (vectors 3, 15, 16, 28), OAS26 | a lapse is undone by replaying the verification, a restore by replaying the withhold |
| FOAS5 | `prev` accepts the previous row **including skipped ones** | OAS8 (vector 16), OAS14 | a forged row becomes the chain's head and a genuine row names it |
| FOAS6 | a skipped row still binds the owner (the fold's `AgentStandingProjector` is handed the unfiltered list) | OAS5, OAS14, OAS12 | a forged first attestation binds the wrong owner and the real one is refused forever |
| FOAS7 | step 3 does not compare the statement's `agent_id` with the stream | OAS8 (vector 18) | a genuine row signed for one agent is moved into another's stream |
| FOAS8 | step 3 does not compare the statement's `actor_id` with the event's actor | OAS8 (vector 20) | a genuine row is re-attributed to another operator's name |
| FOAS9 | step 3 does not compare the row's members with the statement's | OAS8 (vectors 19, 21, 33) | a signed statement for owner Alice rides a row that says Bob |
| FOAS10 | step 2's unknown version read as honoured | OAS8 (vectors 22, 31) | a version this build does not know is a version the attacker writes |
| FOAS11 | step 1 `act-unsigned` removed (a missing `statement` falls through to honoured) | OAS8 (vectors 4, 29), OAS25 | every pre-N2 row is still honoured by shape: the whole PR is a no-op |
| FOAS12 | the operator key is any `log.operator-key` entry that reads as a key (not the first) | OAS8 (vector 12), OAS11 | a later key published by the credential is an operator key |
| FOAS13 | a key's class is decided by membership anywhere in the log (a later `log.key` republishing the operator key unmakes it) | OAS8 (vector 11), OAS11 | one `INSERT` of a public key disarms every honoured act and every future one (finding 6's shape) |
| FOAS14 | a key published in another class **earlier** still counts as an operator key | OAS8 (vector 10), OAS19 | the log key is published as the operator key by anyone who can append |
| FOAS15 | a malformed `log.operator-key` entry holds a position (it displaces the first key) | OAS8 (vector 13), OAS11 | one malformed row stops every act |
| FOAS16 | `HonouredEvents` removes **honoured** rows and keeps skipped ones (the filter inverted) | OAS1, OAS2, OAS7, OAS13 | the Forum acts on exactly the forged rows |
| FOAS17 | one consumer (`TokenEndpoint.cs:153`) keeps the raw fold | OAS12, OAS31, OAS34 | the token gate reads a forged attestation while every route reads the honoured log: the check and the gate disagree |
| FOAS18 | `LogFacts.ServableAfter` ignores the delta's honouring (reads the delta raw) | OAS15 | a forged withhold in the request window blocks a write; a genuine one is not seen |
| FOAS19 | the operator's `flags` queue keeps the raw fold | OAS27 | a forged dismissal hides a flag from the only human reviewer |
| FOAS20 | `AttestOwner` does not re-read after the append | OAS13 (the `not honoured` leg), OAS24 | the operator is told "attested" for a row the Forum skips |
| FOAS21 | `SharedEnvironmentAsync` not called by `attest-owner` and `moderate` | OAS16 | the key that attests owners runs on the head-signing host |
| FOAS22 | `Curia.Api` gains a read of `CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` | OAS17 | a Forum that can read the key: the key the Forum never holds |
| FOAS23 | `DeclarationSigningKey` implements `IOperatorActSigner` too | OAS17 | the declaration key attests owners |
| FOAS24 | `sign-head`'s stop reads only lifecycle rows (`Skipped()` and not `AllSkipped()`) | OAS22 | a head over a forged act row commits it silently |
| FOAS25 | `lifecycle-audit` exits 0 when only act rows are skipped | OAS21 | the monitor reads clean over a forged attestation |
| FOAS26 | EventId 7010's scan lists the payload of a row | OAS23, OAS21's canary | a logger that serialises the record logs the row's content (R10.27, R10.28) |
| FOAS27 (Am. 1) | step 3 does not constrain `moderator` (any value is honoured) | OAS8 (vectors 34, 35), OAS38 | a signed claim of delegation nobody holds is honoured for every effect |
| FOAS28 (Am. 1) | `EnrollAgent.cs:142` keeps the raw fold | OAS35, OAS31 (C13), OAS12 | the re-announcement tells a client an agent is verified when no person did |
| FOAS29 (Am. 1) | `DuplicateRefusal`'s servable filter keeps the raw fold | OAS36, OAS31 (C6), OAS12 | a forged withhold of the original frees every duplicate |
| FOAS30 (Am. 1) | `ApplyModeration` decides `NoOp`, the held category and `prev` over the raw fold | OAS37, OAS12 | the operator is refused a genuine act by a forged one, or a genuine restore releases a forged hold |
| FOAS31 (Am. 1) | `SearchProjection.cs:79` takes `Owner` from the raw fold | OAS31 (C7), OAS12 | a forged owner reorders search results |

| FOAS33 (Am. 1) | `GetPostAsync`'s provenance (`:3066`, `:3097`) takes the raw fold | OAS1, OAS31 (C2), OAS12 | the reader is told "owner verified" for an owner nobody verified |
| FOAS34 (Am. 1) | the serving filter `Servable` (`:2945`) takes the raw fold | OAS2, OAS3, OAS31 (C2 to C5, C11), OAS12 | the Forum withholds exactly the posts a forged row names, in every route |
