## The live run and the cold read

Throwaway Postgres (`curia_n2_*`, dropped after, never mid-run); the built Forum, `curia`, `curia-operator`; hostname, user and paths appear in no record. **Every leg runs first on `68657f78` (as built by Task 0) with the control's result recorded, then on this PR's tip.** A leg whose "green" would have been reported by a broken harness is not a leg (Task 0 (e)).

- **LN2-1 (the key's life).** Generate a key; `publish-operator-key` (the `kid` printed); a second publication is refused; `GET /v1/log/jwks` shows `operator_keys` with one entry; the old `curia keys` and `curia verify` against the same Forum are unchanged.
- **LN2-2 (the genuine path).** `attest-owner` and `moderate` with the key; T1 reached by the genuine path (the observer of Task 0 (e)); a post withheld, served again after a restore; the audit exits 0.
- **LN2-3 (the composed forgery of ruling 34, the forged withhold, the forged restore, the forged upheld flag, the forged first attestation).** As the app role against the built Forum: each reaches nothing (the control on `68657f78`: what Task 0 recorded); the audit exits 7 and lists each by predicate; `sign-head --if-grown` withholds; the start-up log carries 7010; the victim agent's token, budget, key set, a `curia verify` and a genuine attestation are unaffected, and the genuine attestation **succeeds** after the forged first one.
- **LN2-4 (the environment refusals, run as processes).** Every verb with the operator key and another key in one environment: exit 3, nothing appended.
- **LN2-5 (the old rows).** A database built by the `68657f78` build holding attestations and moderation records: this build's start logs 7010 with the count; the owner re-attests; nothing else changes. (The cost of ruling 4, observed.)
- **LN2-6 (the cost).** 1,000 forged well-structured act rows for one agent and one post: a bystander's `GET` and `StatusAsync` timed, against ruling 11's rule; the number into item 278.
- **LN2-7 (the owner's runbook).** The README block, run as written on a throwaway database by a reader who has only it.

**The cold read (words, scaled to the words).** The words this PR adds that a person reads: the README's operator-key and attestation block, `curia-operator`'s help for the new verb and the changed ones, the `not honoured` and mixed-environment refusals, the audit's new sentence, and 7010's message. **Three fresh Sonnet 5.5 readers**, each in an empty directory with the block and a throwaway database's credentials, none told what is being tested: each is asked to publish the key, attest an agent and say how they know it was honoured. **Gate (named now):** each publishes the key once and finds the `kid`; attests with the key; reads `lifecycle-audit` and says what exit 0 and exit 7 mean; **none runs a verb with both keys set believing it should work**; and none takes a skipped row for a success. A reader who fails is a finding about the words, fixed and re-run with a fresh reader.

## Traps applied (the point of each, in this PR) and new traps proposed

- **171** (a premise a plan's "untouched" never read): the consumers section. The briefing named two consumers; the reading found at least six more (finding 1), and it found them by grep and **ran none**: Task 0 (c) is the run.
- **Does a missing row fail, or masquerade as a deliberate deny? (question 3):** a row with no statement is `act-unsigned`, a named predicate in the audit; it is never "no standing" by silence (OAS25).
- **Does the check derive from the same artifact as the thing it checks? (question 4):** the vectors are written from each case's construction; OAS9 parses the slugs and OAS32 the words from the errata; OAS29's corpus is neither the vectors nor the fold's own.
- **A probe that has only ever been green has not been shown to work:** FOAS1 to FOAS35 (Am. 1) are the shown-red; Task 0 (e) is the observer's.
- **263 (proposed).** *An observer that cannot read the other outcome measures nothing.* PR N's M5 recorded "the tier stayed T0" after forging one clause of three; the observation was true and carried no information about the forgery. Every measurement of "nothing happened" is recorded beside a control that read the other outcome through the same observer.
- **264 (proposed).** *A fence that lists implementers exactly is a design instrument.* HSU1's "exactly one `ILifecycleStatementSigner`" is why the operator key has its own interface: the failing fence said *a different key class*, and the cheap edit (add the type to the list) would have made two key classes one.
- **265 (proposed).** *A class decided by membership anywhere in the log is a retroactive switch.* PR N's vector 29 reads as one (finding 6); N2's rule is positional and says why. A rule over a set the credential can append to must be decided at the position the thing happened.
- **266 (proposed).** *"Signed" in a requirement is a claim about a verifier, not about the container.* R10.37 said signed log entry from the white paper's first version; the head signs the leaf, so every row was "signed" and no act was attributed. A requirement that says signed names who signs and who checks.
- **267 (proposed, Am. 1).** *A refusal has a type and a reason; a probe that asserts the type alone cannot tell the cause it was written for from any other the type covers.* The plan's `table-10/denied` was the reason inside a `curia/authz/denied`, and a victim's spent budget was refused by the same type (ruling 17).

## Gates

`dotnet build Curia.sln` (0 warnings); `dotnet build Curia.sln --configuration Release` then `dotnet test Curia.sln --no-build --configuration Release` (needs `CURIA_TEST_POSTGRES` and, for `Curia.Embedding.Tests`, `CURIA_EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIR`); `dotnet build Curia.sln` in Debug then `dotnet test tests/Curia.Architecture.Tests --no-build --configuration Debug` (**both configurations: CS-15 and the fences read IL**); `dotnet restore Curia.sln --locked-mode`; `python3 tools/spec-checks/check-spec.py` and `python3 tools/spec-checks/falsify-spec-checks.py`; `cd rust/curia-testis && cargo fmt --check && cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings && cargo test --locked`; `node tools/differential-oracle/compare.mjs --fail-on-divergence` (both endpoints built first; the differential covers canonicalization and admission, so it is the gate that the shared canonical form did not move, and **the agreement this PR adds is `conformance/operator-acts/` read by C# and Rust**); the closed request fuzzer; **`python3 tools/preflight/preflight.py` before the push**. Clean the `curia_e2e_*` and `curia_n2_*` databases after, never mid-run.

## The seam with the PRs after, and the neighbours

- **PR O** (the deployment pack) calls `lifecycle-audit` and reads its exit codes; this PR adds lines and one meaning ("or an operator act") and changes no existing line (OAS21's golden). Its readiness check compares the served `kid` with the announced one for the operator key too (ruling 14).
- **The join** (the announcement): N2 merged; the key published and its `kid` printed; `db/0006` applied; the owner's answers to Q-N2-1 and Q-N2-2 recorded.
- **PR 8, PR 9:** the nine edited sites in `ForumEndpoints.cs` are mechanical conflicts.
- **The enrolment's own signature** (item 293) is a later PR; the plan names it so that no one reads "operator acts are signed" as "tenure is".

The errata (G70, with the index rows and revision marks, the status written from the run); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (the "Current work" and "Next stage" blocks; D124 and item 261 closed; items 275, 278 and 273 rewritten; items 293 to 298 and 299 filed as working labels (Am. 1: Task 8 files from the first free, with a mapping); traps 263 to 267; the first-free line); `README.md` (the Beta paragraph says T1 is reachable only by a person's signed attestation, and what the credential can still do: append rows the Forum skips and reports, flood, publish a key before the owner, forge a root; the operator's runbook: publish the key, attest, moderate, audit); `CLAUDE.md` (the status paragraph: operator acts signed; the range of entries to G70); `conformance/README.md` (the family). **The white paper and the scoping document are not edited** (the errata carries the requirement text; R4.30 and R10.37 get `(add.)` annotations in the errata).

- **(299)** (working label, Am. 1) *a second `agent.enrolled` row makes a genuine agent's token request `500`* (no `(Active, SuccessfulEnrollment)` transition; closure: a second root in a stream that holds one is skipped and counted; **gate: before the hosted instance**, recommended, Q-N2-5; OAS39 pins it and FOAS35 is its reverse row).
- **(300)** (working label, Am. 3; Task 8 re-greps the first free number) *a publisher that passes a projection's length as an aggregate's expected version conflicts after any row that holds no position: `sign-head` (`acta.Keys.Length`) and `PublishDeclarationKey` (`acta.DeclarationKeys.Length`); N2's rule (c) adds one way to write such a row, and a repeated `kid` already was one; one `INSERT` stalls the first head or the first declaration key* (closed by Task 4: the version is the count of the aggregate's events; OAS19c, OAS19d; FOAS37, FOAS38).
- **(278)** amended (Am. 4, rulings 52 and 53: Task 3's and Task 3c's per-row cost, the break-even row counts and the contention figure, pasted by Task 8 from `keep/t3c/numbers.txt`): the act fold's cost beside the lifecycle's (Am. 1: and Task 0's M2, the whole-log read adds 198 ms at p95 on 10,004 events, Debug, no signature, to an operator verb). **(275)** amended: three classes. **(273)** amended: `log.operator-key` is a fourth type that fails the Acta closed.

## Open risks (named so the reviewer can look)

1. **The composed forgery may not reach T1** on the unmodified tree for a reason this plan did not read (a further gate in `TierPolicy`, the per-day budget, dedupe). Task 0 (a) says; the PR's justification then rests on the other consumers (finding 1) and on R10.37, which is no weaker.
2. **Legacy rows are skipped.** Ruling 4 is alpha's licence and a database that predates this build loses every attestation and moderation record at the first start. The start-up report says so by count. If the owner holds a database where that matters, it is a question, and the answer is re-attest, not a dual reader.
3. **The incremental guard.** Ruling 10's "fails closed" holds only if the guard's delta can hold an act and the verifier can refuse; Task 0 (c) runs it.
4. **Every request that folds standings pays a signature check per act row.** Honoured rows are human-scale; forged rows are item 278's flood. The memo decision is item 278's and is gated before the hosted instance.
5. **The key's custody is unspecified.** A key on a laptop that also holds the database credential is a credential-holder's key. The tool's separation rule helps against the head-signing host only; Q-N2-1 is the owner's.
6. **One key for both acts** couples the attester's and the moderator's compromise (ruling 2). Q-N2-2's options include two keys; the statement `typ` already separates the acts if the owner later wants two classes.
7. **A blind Rust builder may need more than one fix round** (PR N's needed four rounds of findings, one fix round budgeted); the vectors may change in response, which is the process, not a failure.
8. **(Am. 1) A probe that reads a refusal by its type reads a tier denial and a budget denial alike** (ruling 17; ruling 31).

## What this plan did not read or verify

**Nothing in this plan was run.** Not read: `PostureProjector` (the effect of a second `agent.enrolled` in a stream on credential state; Task 0 (a) 4), `ModerationPolicy.Authorize` and `ModerationEffects` (what a signed row with a policy-violating member does; the fold's policy is unchanged by this PR and OAS-level probes assume it is not reached by an unsigned row), `TierPolicy`'s evaluation of the three clauses (the plan takes the AND from N's ruling 34), `VerificationPolicy.Refusal`'s exact rule (`:116-117` was read as "a signal by the same owner as its target is refused"; Task 0 (c) runs the weight leg), `Curia.Operator`'s `DeclarationSigningKey.cs` (how much of it can be extracted), the tests named in "What consumes what" by name (Task 0 M9 lists them), `tools/preflight/preflight.py` for a pinned family count (M7), whether any `src/` assembly other than `Curia.Application` has an `InternalsVisibleTo` (ruling 5's tightening), `Curia.Client`'s `ActaDocuments` for a closed-document check (OAS20 is the check), the Rust crate's `LogKeys` parse for an unknown member (OAS20), and whether another PR is open on a shared file (`gh pr list` at Task 0). Not opened: `curia-csharp-scoping.md` (no convention it states is changed), the Appendix D schema beyond `events` and `agent_keys`, and the white paper's §8 and Table 13 beyond what the code cites.

### Rulings

**17. A refusal has a type and a reason; a probe that reads "tier" asserts both.** Every probe that reads a denial as a tier denial asserts the problem type `curia/authz/denied` **and** the reason `table-10/denied`; **Task 1 observes the reason on a T0 control and on a budget-spent control before any probe asserts it** (the member is `detail` by the client's reading, which Task 0 did not observe). A probe that asserted the type alone would pass for a victim whose budget is spent, which Task 0 produced by accident (vacuity question 1). **The reason is the problem's `detail` member** (`ForumClient.cs:551` reads `error.Detail` for `table-11/rate-budget-exhausted`; `ForumResult.cs:117` reads the tier reason the same way [read]). The same rule holds for the `answer` of every positive control: accepted is `201`.

**18. Ruling 34 is confirmed, with the numbers, and the composed forgery's harm is stated as measured.** T1 needs a person's attestation: no other row stands in for it (`OwnerVerified` is assigned only at `AgentStandingProjection.cs:389`; every variant without the attestation is `403`). The stop condition (a path to T1 without an attestation) did **not** trigger. **N2 must close: `agent.owner-attested` and `moderation.applied`.** G70's "The finding" carries the numbers now (edited in place), so Task 1 pastes the measured text, not a placeholder.

**19. The unsigned enrolment root: N2 files, and does not close, tenure, the lock-out and the squat; and says why.** Three effects of the one cause (an `agent.enrolled` row nobody signs): (i) a backdated root buys the 48 hours; (ii) a **second** row locks a genuine agent out of its token (`500`); (iii) a root planted before an identity enrols squats the identity (`409`). **Reasons N2 does not close them:** (a) none is an operator act; closing (i) and (iii) needs the enrolment to carry a signature somebody checks, which is a different key class, custody and registrar decision (the Registrar's key store is authoritative, R4.16), not this PR's; (b) the cheap closure of (ii), "a second `agent.enrolled` in a stream that already holds a root is skipped and counted like a lifecycle row", lives in the posture fold (`PostureProjector`/`AgentStandingProjection`, Domain and Application, a different subject), and it is a rule over unsigned rows in which the first planted root still wins, so it ends one denial of service and nothing else; (c) PR N2 already blocks the announcement, carries a blind second implementation and a 38-vector family; a PR that closes a second subject delays the first; (d) the credential holder's other denials of service have the same shape and are filed with gates, not closed by whichever PR met them first (item 273: one malformed `log.key` makes the Acta `500`, measured, M6; the budget lock-out by forged `post.accepted` rows). **Filed:** working label **293** (tenure and squatting: the closure is a signed enrolment; the detector of ruling 13 is its interim) and **299** (the lock-out by a second row: the cheap closure above; **gate: before the hosted instance**, recommended, and the owner's to confirm in Q-N2-5). **OAS39 pins all three as they are**, in the layer where the fix would land (the token endpoint and the enrolment route, through the Forum), so it cannot go stale the way `R10_11_HomoglyphSubstitutionIsNotYetDetected` did, and its doc comment names items 293 and 299 so the fix must edit it (FOAS35 shows it red when the lock-out is closed).

**20. `curia.moderation/1` honours only `moderator: human`.** The closed form of a moderation statement fixes `moderator` to `human`; a row whose statement says `automated` or `delegated_agent`, signed or not, is skipped `act-statement-mismatch` (R6.67 step 3; the eight predicate slugs are unchanged, so OAS9 is unchanged). **Reason:** the key's only holder is a person (ruling 2, R4.43); a signed claim of automation or delegation is not backed by anything the Forum can check (no automated key exists: item 294; no delegation exists: Phase 4's delegated moderation); honouring the claim would let the string a signer writes pick which row of R10.36's table applies to a row the fold has just verified, and the fold would have honoured a vocabulary no writer produces. `ApplyModeration` writes `human` and nothing else (`ApplyModeration.cs:207` [read]), so no writer changes. **Cost, stated:** R10.36's permitted automated quarantine cannot be written after this PR (nothing wrote it before either); item 294 now covers both `automated` and `delegated_agent`, each of which needs a key class and a statement kind of its own. **Four test files name `automated`** ([obs]: `git grep` over `tests`); any that insert raw `automated` rows and expect them honoured are Task 3's victims (M9). **Vectors 34 and 35; OAS38; FOAS27.**

**21. The consumer list is Task 0's table, and the scope grows by what it shows.** "What consumes what this PR changes" is superseded by the table below. **Edited, additionally to the plan's list:** `ForumEndpoints.cs` `DuplicateRefusal` (`:2418`: the dedupe reads the servable filter, not only standings), `:2945` `Servable`, `:2899-2904` `SignalRefusal`; `Issuer/TokenEndpoint.cs:153` (the **`tier` claim** as well as the gate); `Credentials/EnrollAgent.cs:142` (the re-announcement's `owner_verified`); `Moderation/ApplyModeration.cs:126,158,203-216` (a genuine `withhold` of a forged-withheld post is refused `no-op` today, a genuine `restore` releases the forged hold); `Operator/Program.cs` `FlagsAsync`, `sign-head --if-grown`, `lifecycle-audit`. **Also edited (found by M7's reading, ruling 26):** `tests/Curia.Canon.Tests/Vectors/ConformanceIndexTests.cs` (`VectorNames`' shape switch recognises a closed set of shapes and throws on another), `rust/curia-testis/src/conformance.rs` (the family list `:323`, `has` `:384`, the loader `:1092`, the shape match `:1450`) and `rust/curia-testis/tests/loader_errors.rs` (`scaffold_empty_corpus`'s family list `:55-70` and the index fixture `:347`). **Every consumer is an OAS31 row**; a row that Task 0 did not run (batch, inbox, a forged `flag.committed`) is run by Task 3 and recorded.

**22. The token's `tier` claim is computed from the honoured log, and the gate with it.** Task 0 observed `tier=T1` in a token issued to the forged agent [obs]. R7.7 and R5.8 say the tier is never **read** from a claim, so no gate reads it; but the claim is stated by the issuer to the holder and to anything that decodes the token, so a forged attestation would have the Forum assert T1 in its own signed token. `TokenEndpoint.cs:153` reads `HonouredEvents` (it already gates a token on `standings.ContainsKey(sub)`; an attestation with no root and no honoured key stays `401`). **OAS34; FOAS17's must-red list gains it.**

**23. The search `floor` is not changed.** `excluded_by_floor` and `excluded_disputed` count what the floor and the dispute mark removed; Task 0 observed that a forged withhold moves neither count; whether the servable filter runs before the floor is `[read]` and not run, and the observation says a genuine withhold is as invisible in them. After N2 a forged withhold is skipped and the results equal the results with no row (OAS31's search row). Task 0's observation is a fact about what a reader cannot learn from the floor, not a consumer to change; no ruling and no probe beyond that row.

**24. `post.accepted`, `answer.accepted`, `signature_valid`: item 298's, with Task 0's numbers, no probe in N2.** A forged `post.accepted` row naming a victim as author, carrying another agent's envelope and signature, is served `200` with `signature_valid: true` (a literal, `ForumEndpoints.cs:3070` [read]); three such rows in the victim's name spent the victim's T0 budget and its genuine question was refused `403 curia/authz/denied` (control agent `201`) [obs, (d)]. `curia read` verifies the author's signature itself, so a reader who verifies is not deceived; a reader who relies on the Forum's literal is (the skill already tells agents the field is a tautology). The forged `answer.accepted` marks an answer accepted though the asker accepted nothing and feeds Table 11's count; T2 requires T1 first (`TierPolicy.cs:214-224` [read]). **Neither is a path to T1 without a person (ruling 18).** **They do widen non-property (a):** after N2, T1 needs a person's attestation, a 48-hour tenure and three questions, and the **latter two are both credential-writable**; a person who attests an identity the credential built grants T1 on day zero, and G70 now says so (edited in place). The closure is a signed enrolment and signed envelopes at read, which are item 298 and item 293.

**25. The report's unmeasured items, one ruling each.**

| Item | Ruling | Measured by |
|---|---|---|

Result files item 296 or strikes it. | Task 1, before any `src/` edit |

**26. M7, by reading (`[read]`, `main`).** `ci.yml` pins the screening timing files at 28 rows (`:126-127`, unrelated) and a **comment** "Its 244 tests across 19 binaries" (`:213-214`; a comment, stale since PR N and not edited here). `tools/preflight/preflight.py` pins no family or test count (it checks that the run left `conformance/` unchanged, `:226-229`). **The places that do move when a family is added** (so Task 1b and Task 5 edit them, and none is `.github/` or `db/`): `conformance/index.json` (the entry's `count`), `conformance/README.md` (a section and "the family holds N"), `ConformanceIndexTests.VectorNames`' shape list, the Rust loader (`conformance.rs`: four places) and `loader_errors.rs`' two lists, and the new test's own asserted count (as `honoured.rs:126`). **So PR N2 still touches neither `ci.yml` nor `db/`, and the controller may merge it on green.**

**27. A row that only the fence turns red is masked.** OAS12 reads IL; a falsification of a consumer that turns red OAS12 and no behavioural probe has shown the fence, not the consumer. For every FOAS row that keeps a raw fold at a consumer (FOAS16 to FOAS19, FOAS28 to FOAS34), Task 7 runs the named **behavioural** probes **with OAS12 excluded** and records them separately; a row whose behavioural probes stay green is recorded `masked-by-fence`, and the consumer's probe is a finding.

**28. A characterization probe is a probe of an open defect and says so.** OAS39 asserts today's behaviour of items 293 and 299; it is green on the tree and green after N2, **by design**, and it is not counted among the probes that must be red. Its doc comment names the items and says what the fix must edit. (This is the rule `R10_11_HomoglyphSubstitutionIsNotYetDetected` broke: it promised to go red and sat one layer below where the fix landed.)

**29. What Tasks 1 to 7 now do.** **Task 1** pastes the measured G70, writes the probes (compile-red ones as `*.cs.pending`: see the Task 1 brief), measures M8 and M9, and generates **38** vectors; its edits include `ConformanceIndexTests.cs`'s shape list and add no `src/`, `db/` or `.github/` file. **Task 3** runs the three consumer rows Task 0 did not, and records ruling 11's numbers and the delta-path result. **Task 4** re-measures `AttestOwner`. **Task 5** (blind) also edits the Rust loader's four places and `loader_errors.rs`; builder B is not given any C# file, so these edits are Rust-only. **Task 7** runs FOAS1 to **FOAS35**. Nothing changes the order; nothing needs `ci.yml` or `db/`.

**30. Numbers.** Item labels **293 to 298** in this plan are **working labels, not allocations** (the first free item is 292, not 293, C10): Task 8 re-greps, files in label order from the first free number, and writes the mapping in "As run"; this amendment adds the working label **299**. Probes **OAS34 to OAS39**, falsification rows **FOAS27 to FOAS35**, vectors **34 to 38**, ruling numbers **17 to 31** and trap **267** are in the plan's own namespaces and were re-grepped (`OAS`/`FOAS` occur nowhere; the traps' highest is 262 on `main`, the plan's own 263 to 266 being proposed). Errata numbers G70, R4.43, R6.66, R6.67, R11.97 are written at Task 1 after a re-read of the errata's end; if it changed since 68657f78, Task 1 stops and returns.

**31. Open risks 1 and 3 are answered, and one is added.** Risk 1 (the composed forgery may not reach T1): it does. Risk 3 (the delta): ruling 25. **Added (8): a probe that reads a refusal by its type reads a tier denial and a budget denial alike (C1); ruling 17.**

### Probes: what changes, and the six that are added (OAS34 to OAS39)

**Red text, corrected from Task 0 (the real text is recorded by Task 1 and replaces these where it differs).** **OAS1** `Expected: 403 curia/authz/denied (reason table-10/denied), owner_verified=false  Actual: 201 (accepted); provenance owner_verified=true owner=owner:forged-…` [obs]. **OAS2** is a **theory over the pairs Task 0 measured honoured on a forged row** `(human, withhold)`, `(automated, quarantine)`, `(delegated_agent, withhold)`: `Expected: 200  Actual: 404 curia/posts/withheld`. **OAS3** the forged human restore after a genuine withhold: `Expected: 404 curia/posts/withheld  Actual: 200`. **OAS4** the forged upholding record: `Expected: answer 201, flag open  Actual: answer 403 curia/authz/denied, flag upheld`. **OAS5** the forged first attestation then the genuine one: `Expected: recorded (exit 0)  Actual: exit 2 curia/attest/owner-binding-immutable` [obs]; no `[unverified]` remains. **OAS6** two legs: forged distinct owners `Expected: V0  Actual: V1`, and a voter pre-poisoned with the author's owner `Expected: 201  Actual: 422 curia/verification/same-owner` [obs]. **OAS22** runs with a forged **moderation** row as well as an attestation. **OAS27** has two legs: a forged human `dismiss` and a forged upholding record, each leaving the flag's state as with no row. **OAS31's data are the table above, rows C1 to C20.** **OAS33's** `[unverified]` stays: Task 0 measured only the no-root `401 invalid_client`, not a malformed payload. **Every probe that reads a denial asserts type and reason (ruling 17).**

- **OAS34** *(Api)* **the token's `tier` claim reads honoured acts.** The composed forgery's agent: the issued token's `tier` claim equals the claim of an agent with no forged row (`T0`); control, same test: a genuine, signed attestation of an aged agent and a new token: `T1`. Red on the tree: `Expected: tier=T0  Actual: tier=T1 (forged)` [obs].
- **OAS35** *(Api)* **the re-announcement does not say `owner_verified` for a forged attestation.** `POST /v1/agents` for the forged identity: `owner_verified: false`; control: a genuine attested identity `true`. Red on the tree: `Expected: false  Actual: true` [obs].
- **OAS36** *(Api)* **a forged withhold frees no duplicate.** A forged withhold of a question, then a near-duplicate from another agent: `409 curia/posts/duplicate-question` (event count unchanged); control: the genuine withhold of the original frees it (`201`). Red on the tree: `Expected: 409  Actual: 201` [obs, Task 0 (c), `obs-c.txt`].
- **OAS37** *(Api and Operator)* **the operator's own verbs are decided over honoured facts.** A forged withhold on a post, then a genuine signed `moderate withhold`: recorded and honoured, exit 0, not `curia/moderation/no-op`; then the genuine `restore` releases it. Red on the tree: `Expected: recorded (exit 0)  Actual: exit 2 curia/moderation/no-op` [obs].
- **OAS38** *(Application)* **only `human` is a moderator.** A statement with `moderator` `automated` or `delegated_agent`, signed correctly, for every effect: skipped `act-statement-mismatch`; the same statement with `human`: honoured; `ApplyModeration` writes `human` (a round trip). Red on the tree: `OperatorActFold not found` (compile; vectors 34 and 35 are the assertion-level form of it).
- **OAS39** *(Api, characterization, green by design, ruling 28)* **the three limits of the unsigned root are as filed.** (i) a second `agent.enrolled` row, as the role, in a genuine agent's stream: `POST /v1/agents` re-announce still `201` with `enrolled_at` unchanged and the agent's token request `500 server_error`; (ii) a root planted before the identity enrols: its genuine `POST /v1/agents` `409 curia/enroll/already-enrolled`; (iii) a backdated root: `enrolled_at` served is the planted instant. Its doc comment names items 293 and 299. It is expected **green before and after N2**; FOAS35 applies the lock-out's closure and it must go red.

**Counts.** Probes written: **39** (OAS1 to OAS39). Red on the tree and after Task 1: all but **OAS39** (green by design) and the controls inside OAS1 to OAS7, OAS34 to OAS37 (each control is green in the same test as its red arm). ### Rulings on Task 1's seven corrections

**32. The reason is the first word of `detail`; ruling 17 stands, narrowed.** [obs] A T0 `answer` is `403 curia/authz/denied`, `title` "Not permitted at this trust tier", `detail` `table-10/denied tier=T0`; a spent budget is the same type, `title` "Not permitted: today's posting budget is spent", `detail` `table-11/rate-budget-exhausted tier=T0 next_slot_at=...`. A probe asserts the type and **the first space-delimited token of `detail`** (`table-10/denied` or `table-11/rate-budget-exhausted`), never the suffix (the tier and the slot instant are the Forum's state, not the rule). The `title` is not asserted (it is prose a later edit may reword); it is what a human reads to tell the two apart. **Trap 267 (Am. 1) is confirmed by the observation and is filed as written.**

**33. The R10.37 index row stays dropped, and G70 does not get a defining line for it.** [read] `check_index_matches_bodies` demands the index list **exactly** what entries define, and `**R10.37**` is defined only in the white paper; G70 annotates it. A defining line in the errata would be a second definition of a white-paper requirement (an unqualified one is a build failure, `check-spec.py:69-96`; a qualified one is an amendment G70 does not make: its revisions table says *annotated `(add.)`*, not *revised*). The annotation is carried by the revisions table of the entry, where R10.59 to R10.61 and R6.62 are carried as well, and **Task 8's docs say so: "annotated by G70" means the entry's revisions table, not an index row**. Nothing for the owner.

**34. M8 is confirmed, as a refusal at the verb; item 296 stands, reworded, and is gated.** [obs, verified in `obs-b.txt`] The plan's finding 6 said a later `log.key` republishing the declaration key's public half "makes every later compromise declaration skip". The mechanism is the verb's: `DeclareCompromise.cs:96-99` compares the key to **every** log key in the whole log, by `kid` and by public key, with no position, so the declaration is refused before a row exists, and `LifecycleFold.cs:302` (positional: `k.LogIndex < index`) is not reached. The harm is the same and the wording is not: **one `INSERT` of a public value, by the credential holder, disarms `curia-operator compromise`, the owner's emergency brake, before it is pulled.** Item 296 is reworded in "Register" below. **N2 does not close it, and says why:** closing it is a revision of R6.62's class rule (a class fixed at first publication, as R6.66 does for the operator key), which moves PR N's vector 29 (`declaration-key-is-also-a-log-key`: a row N pinned as skipped becomes honoured), N's `honoured` family, `LifecycleFold`, the Rust `lifecycle.rs`, and `DeclareCompromise`; a different subject, two implementations and a frozen family, in a PR that already carries a 38-vector family and a blind builder. **Gate: before the hosted instance (recommended), owner confirms in Q-N2-6 (new, below).** The reason it should not wait for beta: the declaration is the only act that ends a compromised agent key after the guard is lifted, and the surface is a public key and one `INSERT`.

**35. OAS33: the plan was wrong, and the probe is right.** The plan's `[unverified]` predicted that a malformed act payload "reads as no standing" and a fresh token is `401`. [obs] It does not: seven malformed payloads (a JSON string, an array, wrong-typed members, a non-string `statement`: the shapes Task 1 thought of, not the vectors' rows, which is why ruling 39 extends them) leave the token `200`, the next question `201`, the key set `200` and the served post `200`, as with no row. Today's folds read an object member only after checking its type, so there is nothing to refuse. **OAS33 is restated as a regression guard for N2's fold, green on the tree and green after, by design of the tree** (not a characterization of a defect, which is OAS39's class): a fold that returns a failure for a row's content, throws on it, or lets one reach a consumer as an error would turn it red, and that is the lock-out shape of PR N's finding 1. **Its red leg has never been seen, so it is a probe that has only ever been green (the project's dangerous state): FOAS36 (below) is the row that shows it red, and Task 3 extends its data to the skipped rows of the 38 vectors (ruling 39).** The plan's body line for OAS33 is edited (`(Am. 2)`); the withdrawn clause is "red on the tree".

**36. OAS12's call list is Task 1's IL list, and its own output is the authority from now on.** The plan's "22" (a grep) and Amendment 1's "23 grep lines" were both counts of text. [obs] The IL scan finds **19 callers, 23 call sites**: `ApplyModeration.RecordAsync`, `AttestOwner.RecordAsync`, `EnrollAgent.RecordAsync`, `ForumEndpoints` (`BatchAsync`, `DuplicateRefusal`, `GetPostAsync`, `GetThreadAsync`, `InboxAsync`, `ListBoardAsync`, `SearchAsync`, `Servable`, `SignalRefusal`), `LogFacts` (`Extend`, `Of`, `ServableAfter`), `OperatorCommands.FlagsAsync`, `PostureQuery.Of` (2), `SearchProjector.Fold` (2), `TokenEndpoint.IssueAsync`. **Task 3 edits the consumers on that list, and the probe's red text is the check, not this sentence.** The two writers (`AttestOwner.RecordAsync`, `ApplyModeration.RecordAsync`) are **Task 4's** (ruling 6), so **OAS12 goes green when Task 4 ends, not Task 3**: Task 3's done condition for OAS12 is that its remaining callers are exactly those two, and its report says so with the probe's own text. (A consumer that appears on the list and on no row of Amendment 1's table is a finding for the architect.)

**37. Regeneration of the vectors is a fixed point, not a from-scratch reproduction, and the plan's "byte-identical regeneration" is restated.** [obs, and verified: the committed corpus's SHA-256 list equals `hash3.txt`] ES256 signatures are randomized (.NET's `ECDsa` has no deterministic mode), so the generator keeps an existing authorization whose statement, header `kid` and signer match (`LoadOaPins`); run 1 signs fresh, run 2 normalises same-statement rows to one signature, run 3 equals run 2. **The committed corpus is that fixed point. Rule:** the corpus is regenerated **in place** (the pins are read from it), never from an emptied directory; an emptied directory changes signature bytes and never a decision, as PR N's declarations do. The rule of the plan ("`expected.json` is written from the construction, never by running a fold", "the keys are derived deterministically") is unchanged and holds; **only the sentence "run the generator twice and diff" is replaced by "run it until two consecutive runs agree (run 3 equals run 2)".** A change to a statement changes its pin and so its signature, which is as it should be. Nothing for Task 5: builder B reads the committed files.

**38. `VectorLoader.cs` is accepted, and the brief did not forbid it.** It is under `tests/` (the Task 1 brief's rule 3 lists files under `tests/` and names examples), and the index test throws on a profile it does not know, so the edit is required, not optional. The plan's "Tests edited" list gains `tests/Curia.Canon.Tests/Vectors/VectorLoader.cs` and `OperatorActsFamilyTests.cs`; Task 5's builder B edits no C#.

**39. Who owns each open leg and each probe still pending.** The plan's Task 3 and Task 4 lists disagreed with the probes' own needs in three places (OAS26, OAS30, OAS29); corrected here.

### What Task 2 now does (rulings 40 to 42; Task 2's brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-2-brief.md`)

**40. The class rule, exactly.** R6.66 is a sentence; the implementer needs a function. **Decided:** an entry of type `log.key`, `log.declaration-key` or `log.operator-key` *reads as a key* by R6.62's closed form (a string `kid`, a closed `jwk` carrying that `kid`; `PublishedKeys.ReadEntry` today), and **holds a position in its class's list** unless (a) an earlier entry of the same class that reads as a key lists the same `kid` (today's rule, unchanged for the first two classes and added for the third); or (b) it is a `log.operator-key` entry and an **earlier** entry **in the log keys' or the declaration keys' list** has the same `kid` or the same public key (`ClosedJwk.SamePublicKey`): the key was first published in another class (vector 10); or (c) it is a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` entry and an **earlier** operator key that holds a position has the same `kid` or the same public key: the operator key's class is fixed (vector 11). **The operator key is the first entry of `OperatorKeys`; the others are listed and are `act-key-not-first` in Task 3's fold** (vector 12), so `OperatorKeys` holds every entry that holds a position, in log order, as `declaration_keys` does. **Not changed:** the relation between a log key and a declaration key (PR N's vector 29, item 296; ruling 34): (c) removes an entry only for an earlier **operator** key. **Three consequences stated so that no one reads them as bugs.** (i) A `log.key` that republishes the operator key's public half disappears from `LogKeys`; a head signed under it would not verify, which is R6.66's "none of the three SHALL be published in a class it was not first published in" doing what it says. (ii) The exclusion keys on a public key the forger must know **before the operator publishes it**; a forged `log.operator-key` entry placed ahead of a genuine key's first publication needs that key's public half in advance, so it is not a new surface and not an item (item 275's first-slot weakness is the general case). (iii) `ActaLog` (what the Forum serves) and `PublishedKeys.OfLog` (what a verifier reads) read key entries by **different** structural rules today (`ReadKey` demands `valid_from` and nothing about the jwk's form and fails the whole log on a miss; `ReadEntry` demands the closed jwk and skips a miss): the plan's "`ReadKey` is shared, so the Acta and the verifier cannot read an entry differently" is **not true of the tree** [read]. **Rule:** the served `operator_keys` is `PublishedKeys.OfLog(log).OperatorKeys` (one decision function, the verifier's), after `ActaLog.Fold`'s structural pass has failed closed on a `log.operator-key` entry with no `kid`, `jwk` or `valid_from` (`curia/acta/malformed-operator-key`, the slug of its two siblings; plan ruling 15, which makes item 273 a **fourth** type, pinned by a characterization test whose doc comment names item 273), and the served `keys` and `declaration_keys` drop an entry that (c) says holds no position. Two readings of one list were the plan's named hazard, and one function decides here.

**41. The signer interface comes to Task 2, and the publishers compare by public key.** `IOperatorActSigner` (the plan's Task 4 file) is needed by `PublishOperatorKey` and so is built here, **with exactly `ILifecycleStatementSigner`'s three members** (`string Kid`, `JsonValue.Object PublicJwk()`, `Result<byte[]> SignStatement(ReadOnlySpan<byte> signingInput)`) and a doc comment saying what differs (its statements are `curia.owner-attestation/1` and `curia.moderation/1`, its header `typ` `curia-operator-act+jws`); Task 4 supplies `OperatorActsSigningKey` and changes no member. The test twin `InMemoryOperatorActSigner` (beside `InMemoryLifecycleStatementSigner`) is built here too. **HSU1 is not edited; OAS17's first clause stays red** (`implementers: []`) because no shipped assembly implements the interface until Task 4. **R6.66 says each publisher refuses "by `kid` and by public key", and one existing publisher does not:** [read] `PublishDeclarationKey` refuses a log key by `kid` only (`acta.Keys.Any(k => k.Kid == key.Kid)`); `DeclareCompromise` (the verb) compares both. Task 2 adds the public-key comparison to `PublishDeclarationKey` (a behaviour change of an existing publisher, stated in the PR body) and gives `PublishOperatorKey` all three refusals. **Problem types, fixed here so that tests assert a literal and not the constant they check:** `curia/keys/operator-key-is-log-key`, `curia/keys/operator-key-is-declaration-key`, `curia/keys/operator-key-already-published`, `curia/keys/declaration-key-is-operator-key`; the existing `curia/keys/declaration-key-is-log-key` and `declaration-key-already-published` are unchanged. **The log-key publisher** is `sign-head`'s inline publication in `Curia.Operator/Program.cs`; Task 2 edits **no** `src/Curia.Operator` file, and provides one public Application method that any publisher can call to ask which class the log published a key under (its name is Task 2's, recorded in the report); Task 4 calls it from `sign-head` and writes OAS19b.

**42. Task 2's scope, what turns green, what must stay red.** Files and exact commands are in the brief. **Turns green:** OAS10 (LRV33's first test), OAS18 (HSU6 with four rows; the `.pending` file becomes `.cs`), OAS20's Api half; **new and green when written:** OAS11 (moved), OAS19 (moved and extended), and the lettered legs **OAS11b** (an operator entry whose key was published earlier as a log key holds no position, by `kid` and by public key), **OAS11c** (the served lists and the verifier's agree), **OAS20b** (the served document lists a published operator key with `kid`, `log_index`, `valid_from`, and `keys` and `declaration_keys` are unchanged), **OAS20c** (the existing Client reads a key set carrying `operator_keys` as it reads one without). **Stays red, with the same text as Task 1 recorded:** Api 22 of the `OperatorActs*` tests (Task 1's 23 less OAS20), Application 1 (OAS15's forged-delta), Architecture 2 (OAS12, OAS17's first clause); OAS7 stays red because the verb is Task 4's. **Task 2 shows its own probes red once before it is done (T2F1 to T2F8, in the brief), because Task 7's FOAS12 to FOAS15 patch the fold and will find a vacuous key-class probe too late if Task 2 does not.**

### Rulings on Task 2's corrections

**43. `Classes` has one reader, and the plan's "four places" is withdrawn.** [obs, and `git grep` here agrees: `PublishedKeys.Classes` is read by no `src/` file; its one reader is `LRV33_TheClassesAreSeparateListsEachWithItsOwnHeaderTyp`] Amendment 2 ruling 40's guess and its "unsure of" item 2 are closed: no reader disagrees with the class rule. **Consequence, stated so that nobody reads it as a clean bill:** the third row's header `typ` (`curia-operator-act+jws`) lives in a list nothing in `src/` reads, and Task 3's fold will carry the same literal in its own step 5. Two copies of a string with no probe that ties them to the text drift without a failing test, so **Task 3 writes OAS9b**: both literals (the third row of `PublishedKeys.Classes` and the fold's) are compared with the `typ` parsed out of R6.66's paragraph **in the errata**, a third artifact, and neither is derived from the other. The fold does **not** read `Classes`.

**44. `sign-head` (and `PublishDeclarationKey`) append at a version that the third class makes wrong: Task 4 fixes both, one probe each, and files item 300.** [read, `Program.cs:518`, `PublishDeclarationKey.cs:52`; the report's finding confirmed] A log aggregate's expected version is the **number of events it holds**; `sign-head` passes `acta.Keys.Length`, the number of keys that **hold a position**, and `PublishDeclarationKey` passes `acta.DeclarationKeys.Length`. They were equal on every sound log. They differ when the aggregate holds an entry that holds no position, and ruling 40 (c) makes one more way to write such an entry: a `log.key` (or `log.declaration-key`) row that republishes the operator key's public half. **The harm, stated as the first form:** one `INSERT` of that row, by the credential holder, makes the **first head publication** (a `log.key` for a key not yet published) and the **first declaration-key publication** conflict, so no head can be signed by a new key until someone finds the row. It is the lock-out shape of item 273 (one row, one stalled operator act) and it is **not new in kind**: a repeated-`kid` row, which holds no position under rule (a), already did the same on the tree before N2 [read; a leg of the probe is built on it so that the red text says so]. N2 widens it by one way and the fix is to stop counting the wrong thing, which `PublishOperatorKey` already does (`log.Count(e => e.AggregateId.Value == LogEntries.OperatorKeysAggregate)`). **Rule, for every publisher:** the expected version of the aggregate is the count of **its events in the log read**, not the length of a projection. **Task 4's** (it edits `Program.cs`; `PublishDeclarationKey.cs` is one line and goes with it, so one task makes one change in two places, and Task 3 touches neither). **Probes, written before the fix and shown red once by Task 4:** **OAS19c** (process): a log holding `log.operator-key` operator-1 at 0 and a `log.key` row for the same public key under another `kid` at 1, then `sign-head` with a log key no row publishes: exit 0, the key published, the head signed, `log:keys` holds 3 events; a second leg on a repeated-`kid` `log.key` row (the pre-N2 form) and a control with no such row. **OAS19d** (Application): the same two shapes for `PublishDeclarationKey` (a `log.declaration-key` row republishing the operator key, then the first declaration key: `Ok`, index equal to the log's length at the call). **Red text [predicted]:** a version conflict from the event store and nothing appended; Task 4 records the real text. **Falsification (Task 7, rows 37 and 38):** FOAS37 `sign-head` computes the version from `acta.Keys.Length` again: OAS19c; FOAS38 `PublishDeclarationKey` from `acta.DeclarationKeys.Length` again: OAS19d. **Item 300 (working label):** *a publisher that counts keys instead of events conflicts after any row that holds no position* (closed by N2, Task 4; the register line is written by Task 8 from the run). **Cost of not fixing, if the controller wants to defer:** none for the build, and the owner's first `sign-head` on the hosted instance is the one that meets it, so it does not defer.

**45. T2F1 was patched in `From`, not `Decide`, and that is the right patch; but the discharge of rule (c) is thinner than the report says, and Task 3 thickens it (OAS11d).** [obs: T2F1b, (c) removed from `Decide`, reds OAS11 alone; read: `OperatorKeysProbes.cs`] With one function (ruling 40) both readers change alike under a patch to it, so **OAS11c, which compares the two readers, can never discharge the function's rule**; its value is T2F2 (one reader drifts from the function) and nothing more. Rule (c) is discharged by **OAS11 alone**, and OAS11's (c) leg is a republication under the **same `kid` and the same key**, so it exercises both arms of the `Same` test at once and neither alone. The two arms are therefore not separately shown: a mutant that compares by `kid` only, or by public key only, survives every probe on the tree [read, argued; Task 3 runs it]. This is the shape this project has shipped before (a probe green because the input satisfied it two ways, vacuity question 2). **Task 3's first step adds OAS11d** to `OperatorKeysProbes.cs`: an operator key `operator-1` (key K1); then a `log.key` entry **under another `kid` carrying K1** (public-key arm only), a `log.declaration-key` entry **under `operator-1` carrying another key K2** (`kid` arm only), and, as the control, a `log.key` entry under a fresh `kid` with a fresh key: expected `log keys [<control>]; declaration keys []; operator keys [operator-1]`, written from R6.66 and not from the function. **T3F1** (the public-key arm removed) and **T3F2** (the `kid` arm removed) are the rows that show it. **Rows:** Task 2's nine (T2F1 to T2F8, T2F1b) are recorded as run and are not FOAS rows; the FOAS count is Task 7's.

**46. A probe that puts two classes' keys in one log builds them from distinct key material, and says so.** [obs: OAS11 needed it; read: `InMemoryLifecycleStatementSigner` has one fixed key, `InMemoryOperatorActSigner` a fresh one per instance] Under rule (b) and (c), **identical public keys are the data**: a probe that builds its log key, its declaration key and its operator key from the one fixed in-memory key silently turns every operator entry into "first published elsewhere", and a test written for the fold would then fail with `act-key-unpublished` for a reason that is in its own arrange step. **Rule, Task 3 and every later task:** a probe that publishes keys of more than one class asserts in its arrange step that their public JWKs are pairwise different (`Assert.NotEqual`), **except** the legs whose point is that they are equal (OAS11, OAS11b, OAS11d, OAS19), which name that in their comment. The three signer types are used as each is meant: operator keys from `InMemoryOperatorActSigner`; the other two from `InMemoryLifecycleStatementSigner` (log key and declaration key are therefore **one key material** unless a second instance is made distinct: the existing probes that need a log key and a declaration key to differ already do this, and the arrange check is what keeps the next one honest).

**47. The route tables and the allowlist rule: yes, and as a rule, not a patch.** [obs: Task 2 stopped, did not edit `ForumEndpoints.cs`, did not rename the constants to dodge the reflecting test, and said so; the controller added the four 409s] A brief's allowlist is a list of **files that the task's own acceptance requires**, and `R4_18_EveryRefusalTheUseCasesReturnIsMappedByTheRoutes` (`KeyRotationEndpointTests.cs:1025`) reflects over every `KeyActErrors` and `KeyStatementErrors` constant and demands that `ForumEndpoints.StatusForKeyAct` not answer 500 for it (except a three-row `raisedByTheRoutes` set, `NotHonouredType` among them, a test-owned exception list). **Rule:** (a) any task that adds a problem-type constant **lists in its allowlist the mapping file for every layer that can raise it** (`ForumEndpoints.cs` for a route, `Curia.Operator/Program.cs`'s `RefuseAsync` for an exit code) **and puts the reflecting tests in its success commands** (`KeyRotationEndpointTests`, `CredentialErrorTypeFenceTests`); (b) the brief names the class the constant goes in, because the reflecting test reads only `KeyActErrors` and `KeyStatementErrors`, so a type in a class it does not read is unchecked and says nothing. **Task 3 adds no problem type** (a skipped row is never a refusal, R4.43) and lists neither file for that reason; it edits no `Curia.Api` file at all. **Task 4 adds `curia/acts/not-honoured`** in a **new `ActErrors` class, not in `KeyActErrors`**, and its brief states whether a route can return it (none is expected: the writers are the operator tool's); if one can, `ForumEndpoints.cs` joins the allowlist and the type is mapped to the deliberate 500 beside `NotHonouredType` with the sentence that explains it. A task that finds a mapping file is missing from its list **stops and says so, as Task 2 did**.

### The order changes: Task 3 is split, and the consumers come after the writers

**48. Tasks run 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, then 3b, with Task 5 started after Task 3 and run beside Task 4; 6, 7, 8, 9 follow.** [read] The plan puts every consumer in Task 3 and the writers in Task 4, "in parallel where the files allow". **The files do not decide it; the tests do.** `ForumFixture.AttestOwnerAsync` (and `WithholdAsync`, `QuarantineAsync`, `RestoreAsync` through `ModerateAsync`) call the **genuine use cases**, `AttestOwner.RecordAsync` and `ApplyModeration.RecordAsync` [read, `ForumFixture.cs:280-297`], which write unsigned rows until Task 4 signs them; Task 1's M9 found 43 Api test files, and `grep` here counts 64 that name the fixture's writers. **The moment a consumer reads `HonouredEvents`, every one of those tests reads a skipped row and fails**, and stays failed until Task 4 ends. A task whose done condition is "the Api suite as it was, plus these probes" cannot be gated when its own change turns half the Api suite red by construction, and the genuine path of every consumer (the control that a consumer still honours a genuine act) is exactly what that suite checks. This is the shape of the defect this project warns of: **a consumer edited, every probe green, and the control that shows it still works not run**. **Rule:** the consumers switch **last**, when the writers sign and the existing Api suite is the regression check.
- **Task 3 (the fold).** `OperatorActFold`, `OperatorActPredicates`, `HonouredEvents` (the type), `VerifiedLog.HonouredEvents`, `ActSkipped()`, `AllSkipped()`; **no consumer, no writer, no `Curia.Api`, no `Curia.Operator`, no `db/`**. The Api suite is untouched by it, and its gate is that the failing set equals Task 2's.
- **Task 4 (the writers and the tool), unchanged but for what it now also fixes (ruling 44) and what it reads:** `AttestOwner` and `ApplyModeration` sign, decide over `HonouredEvents` and check their own row; `ForumFixture`'s four writers sign (a test-side signer, `InMemoryOperatorActSigner`, published once per fixture); `Program.cs`. The consumers are still raw, so the whole Api suite is green apart from the OAS reds, **and a writer's extra members (`statement`, `authorization`) must not change what a raw consumer reads: the suite is the check that they do not.**
- **Task 3b (the consumers).** Every row C1 to C20 of Amendment 1's table **less C14 and C15** (the writers' own reads, Task 4's), `LogFacts.ServableAfter` (ruling 10), `EnrollAgent.cs:142`, `TokenEndpoint.cs:153`, `OperatorCommands.FlagsAsync`; OAS12 turns green here (not at Task 4: ruling 36's "when Task 4 ends" is replaced, because the list holds both the writers and the consumers and the last of the two to be edited turns it); rows C8 and C20 are observed first; OAS2's `curia read` exit-5 leg; OAS33 over the skipped rows of the 38 vectors. **Task 3b's brief is written after Task 4 returns**, from its report and from the fold Task 3 built.
- **Task 5 (Rust, blind) starts when Task 3's report is in**, not when Task 1b ends, and runs beside Task 4. **Reason:** Task 3's first run on the vectors is the C# fold's first contact with a corpus it did not derive from, and a vector that is wrong or ambiguous is far cheaper corrected before builder B has read it than after (PR N's four, two, three and one undecided cases are the measure). Builder B stays blind to every C# file; the only thing Task 3 hands it is a corrected corpus, if there is one.
- **Predicted allocation of the probes** (each brief states its own, from its own read): **Task 3** turns green OAS8, OAS14, OAS26, OAS38, OAS9's comparison, and the new OAS8b, OAS8c, OAS9b, OAS11d, OAS30a, OAS30m, OAS33b. **Task 4** turns green OAS5, OAS7, OAS13, OAS16, OAS17's clause 1, OAS19b, OAS19c, OAS19d, OAS21, OAS22, OAS23, OAS24, OAS32's comparison. **Task 3b** turns green OAS1, OAS2, OAS3, OAS4, OAS6, OAS12, OAS15 (both legs), OAS25, OAS27, OAS31, OAS34, OAS35, OAS36, OAS37, and OAS33's extended data. [predicted; a probe that turns green earlier or later than this says its task's brief or the code is not as read.]

**49. What Task 3 decides, which the plan left open (its brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-3-brief.md`).** (a) **OAS8 compares `state` as well as `rows`.** [read: the drafted OAS8 builds its comparison from `rows` alone] Every vector's `expected.json` carries a `state` (`owner` and `verified` for an agent's stream, `served` for a post's), written from the case's construction; a fold that decides every row right and a `HonouredEvents` that feeds the projectors wrong would pass `rows` alone. The state half reads the **raw** projectors (`AgentStandingProjector.Fold`, `FlagProjector.Fold`) fed **`HonouredEvents` only**: the one place a test feeds them, and the end-to-end check that "honoured rows alone decide the owner and the withholding" (R4.43, R10.60). (b) **`HonouredEvents` is not a log.** It holds the events of the log less the skipped act rows, **in log order with each event's own `Seq`**; the position of an event in it is not a log index (R6.47), and the type is built so that nobody can read it as one (it does not implement `IReadOnlyList<AppendedEvent>`; it exposes `Events` for the raw folds, which check only that `Seq` ascends, and `Log` for the whole log). (c) **`OperatorActFold` never throws and never returns a failure for a payload** (ruling 35, FOAS36): any JSON value in the payload position decides to a row with a predicate from the eight, **OAS33b** shows it, and the fold's own steps run in R6.67's order with the signature last. (d) **A skipped act row is a `SkippedRow`** (the existing record: index, type, aggregate, predicate; never a payload member, R10.27, R10.28), so that `AllSkipped()`, the audit, the 7010 line and the head stop read both kinds in one shape; `Skipped()` and `SkippedPast` keep their lifecycle meaning (ruling 9). (e) **OAS30 splits.** A timing assertion at 25 ms in the suite is a flake, and 200 honoured rows at the plan's 0.11 ms per signature is already about 22 ms [read from ruling 11; the figure is item 278's, not re-measured]. **OAS30a** (deterministic, in the suite) counts primitive calls: a row that fails a step before the signature calls the primitive **zero** times; N honoured rows call it **N** times; and asking a `VerifiedLog` for `HonouredEvents` twice does not verify twice (the memo). **OAS30m** (a measurement, in the suite) runs the plan's two cases, records the numbers into the report, and asserts only a **ceiling ten times the rule** (250 ms for the 25 ms rule), which guards a gross regression and is **not** ruling 11's discharge: ruling 11's decision is made from the report's numbers by the architect, as ruling 11 says. (f) **Task 3 may not read `expected.json` or `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures/OperatorActs.cs` before its first OAS8 run**; the fold is written from R6.67's text and the errata, so that the first run is a contact between two artifacts and not a restatement of one.
