Marks: **[obs]** observed by Task 3 and read in its report; **[read]** read here, not run; **[predicted]** an outcome this amendment expects and no run has shown.

### Rulings on Task 3's three findings

**50. The order of R6.67's steps is undiscriminated by the corpus, and the family gains a ladder.** [read, and it is wider than T3F3] The generator's own comment says "each case holds one defect, so the predicate cannot come from another cause" (`OperatorActs.cs`, doc comment): no vector has a row that fails two steps. "The first step that applies deciding it" is a clause about **order**, and order is exactly what a second implementation can get wrong and every vector would still agree. T3F3 (step 7 before step 3) went red only on OAS30a, which counts calls to the primitive: it caught the mutant by cost, not by the predicate a reader of the log would see, and a Rust fold has no such counter. [obs] T3F3 stayed green on OAS8. **Ruling:** the family gains a **ladder**: for each step *i* from 2 to 6 a vector whose row fails step *i* and **every step after it**, with the expected predicate step *i*'s. A fold that runs step *j* before step *i* (any *i* < *j*) then reports *j*'s predicate on the vector for *i*, so one vector per step pins every pair at once, not only the adjacent ones. Step 1 against the rest is pinned by one more (an unsigned row in a stream with no root, carrying a garbage authorization). Moderation shares the order by the text, but a second implementation may keep one function per kind, so two moderation ladder vectors are added (steps 3 and 4: the steps whose content is kind-specific). **Each new vector except the first ends with a genuine honoured row** (prev naming the stream's root: a skipped row moves nothing), so a fold that skips every row fails it and the vector carries its own control (the lesson of Task 3's rule 12). Vectors **39 to 47** below. `OAS30a` stays: after the ladder it is a second, independent discriminator (a row that stops before step 7 must call the primitive **zero** times, and the ladder's rows do).

**51. Step 5's arms, and four more arms, were pinned by nothing; the family gains thirteen vectors, and several need a text fix first.** [obs] T3F14 (step 5 not applied at all) stayed green on every probe: "no vector has a parseable header with a wrong `typ` or `alg`". Reading the fold against the generator, the same is true of five other arms of the procedure: [read] (i) a **detached** JWS (the fold calls `ReadProtectedHeader` and never looks at the payload part; a compact JWS with the statement's bytes as its payload, validly signed, reaches step 7 and may well verify); (ii) a statement that is **not its own canonical form** (`IsOwnCanonicalForm`, step 3's first arm: no vector carries a non-canonical statement); (iii) the same with a text that is canonical JCS but **not NFC**; (iv) the **JSON types** of the closed form (`IsClosedForm` types each member: `owner_verified` a boolean, `prev` a non-negative integer, `adjudicates` an array of strings; **R6.67 says "the closed form of its kind" and lists member names only**, so the types are Task 3's reading, which it said so itself); (v) an `alg` of `none`; (vi) a header with no `kid`. And one gap in the **state** half: [read] `ApplyOwnerAttestation` ignores a later attestation naming another owner **whole** (its `owner_verified` too, `AgentStandingProjection.cs:384-392`), R6.67 says such a row "binds nothing" and is silent on the flag, and vector 25 cannot tell the two readings apart because both rows say `verified: true`. **Ruling:** vectors **48 to 60** below (header `typ` wrong, `alg` wrong, `alg` none, authorization attached, authorization absent, header with no `kid`, statement not canonical, statement not NFC, `prev` a string, `prev` negative, `owner_verified` a string, `adjudicates` not strings, a second owner's lapse changes nothing), each isolating **one** arm: for the step-5 arms the signature is **valid** (so only step 5 can stop the row); for the step-3 typing arms the **row omits the member under test** (a row that states it would be stopped by "the row differs from the statement" and hide the typing). The text fixes of ruling 54 (b) make the text decide what the vectors pin: the types, "detached", "a header with no `kid`" and "sets nothing". **Task 3's closed-form typing is confirmed as the reading and is written into R6.67; the Rust builder reads the fixed text and meets the vectors.**

**52. Ruling 11's case (a): the 25 ms line is retired and restated on a derived basis; case (b) stands.** [obs] 200 honoured rows measured 27.5 to 32.7 ms over baseline at p95 (p50: 26.2 over baseline), about 0.14 to 0.16 ms per row, **and the same per-row figure for the 1,000 forged rows** (160 to 178 ms: 0.160 to 0.178 ms per row), so the fold's cost is, to within noise, one primitive call per row. **The 25 ms was never measured: it was item 278's 0.11 ms per lifecycle row times 200 (22 ms) plus 14 percent**, a figure from another fold on another day, written as a stop line "before Task 3 measures", and the primitive's cost on the builder's machine is the one number that decides whether it holds. Moving it to 35 ms would be the move this task was told not to make. **Keeping it** would require the cost of the primitive to be taken out of the request, which is **the memo of signature verdicts**, and ruling 11 (b) already says that memo is item 278's decision at the hosted-instance gate and is not built on this PR's authority: so the line cannot be kept and stay a stop, and cannot stop the PR for the same reason (b) does not. **Restated** (the question the stop was for: *does the fold cost more than the verification it has to do, and is it linear in the acts?*). Let **N** be the honoured act rows, **B** the lifecycle-only baseline, **F** the baseline plus `HonouredEvents`, **c** the median of 2,000 calls of the **same** ES256 verifier port over the same signing input and key material, measured in the same process immediately before the timed runs (so the machine, the JIT, the load and the key import cancel).
- **(a1) linear in the acts (a stop).** For N in {50, 100, 200, 400} (each over the 10,000-event log), the per-row cost `(F p50 - B p50) / N` of the dearest N is at most **1.5 times** the cheapest's. *Basis:* the two runs Task 3 has differ by 1.19 times (noise); a prefix rescan or a per-row copy of the log is quadratic and shows as 8 times across 50 to 400; a super-linear term too small to exceed the noise at that spread is not one a hosting stage will meet.
- **(a2) the fold's own cost does not exceed the primitive's (a stop).** `(F p50 - B p50)` at N = 200 is at most **2 times N times c**. *Basis:* the memo (item 278: the only remedy ruling 11 (b) allows) removes the primitive from every repeat request and leaves the rest; if the rest is more than the primitive, the memo yields less than a halving and the design, not the cryptography, is the cost, so the PR returns. Task 3's numbers, with c about 0.12 to 0.15 ms, are 1.0 to 1.3 times N times c: a pass **[predicted; Task 3c measures c and applies the rule]**.
- **Recorded, not a stop:** the absolute `F p95 - B p95` for N = 200 (27.5 to 32.7 ms), the per-row cost, and the row count at which one request passes 25 ms (25 divided by the per-row cost, about 170 honoured acts): **these go into item 278 as the figure the memo decision weighs**, and the PR body tells the owner in one sentence that every request which folds standings or moderation pays about 0.15 ms per operator act until the memo exists (an operator with a few hundred acts pays tens of milliseconds a request: affordable at beta, and the reason item 278 is gated before the hosted instance).
- **(b) stands as ruling 11 wrote it:** 1,000 forged well-structured rows must not cost more than 250 ms over baseline, or the number goes into item 278. [obs] 160 to 178 ms: a pass, with the break-even at about 1,400 to 1,560 forged rows (250 divided by the per-row cost), recorded in item 278 beside (a).
- **Task 3 is accepted as built.** It stopped and returned as its brief told it to; the stop is retired, not breached. Its counts and its commit stand.

**53. Does OAS30m running alone hide anything? Yes, three things, and the assertion changes so that it does not matter.** [obs] The test failed its 250 ms ceiling inside the full suite (342 ms over baseline for case (a), a tenfold slowdown against 27.5 to 32.7 ms alone), so it was moved to its own serial collection (`DisableParallelization`). What running it alone hides: **(i) contention is the production case, not the exception.** The fold will run **per request** [predicted: no consumer exists yet; Task 3b's consumers build a `VerifiedLog` per request, its memo lives and dies with it, and nothing caches across requests unless Task 3b adds one, which is item 278's memo], so K concurrent requests pay K times N times c of CPU, and the Forum's throughput ceiling for requests that fold standings or moderation is roughly cores divided by N times c (200 acts at 0.15 ms is about 33 requests a second per core). The 342 ms is the only measurement this PR holds of what a loaded host does, and it is **a result, not noise**. **(ii) A serial collection serialises within one assembly.** `dotnet test Curia.sln` may run test assemblies at once (MSBuild's parallelism; this amendment did not establish whether CI's invocation does), so "alone" is a property of the xUnit collection and not of the machine, and the ceiling can fail again in CI for a reason no one can reproduce locally. **(iii) A ceiling in milliseconds is the wrong unit under load; a ratio to the primitive measured beside it is not.** When the host is ten times slower, c is ten times larger in the same breath, and `(F - B) / (N * c)` stays near 1. **Rulings:** the serial collection **stays** (harmless) and is **not relied on**; OAS30m's assertions become **ratios to c**, in the same run: case (a) `(F p50 - B p50) <= 5 * N * c` and case (b) `(F p50 - B p50) <= 5 * forged * c`, a gross guard that holds under load (the stops of ruling 52 are the implementer's mechanical decisions from the same run, recorded in its report, and are **not** assertions: a stop that fails a build on a noisy host teaches people to rerun); the absolute milliseconds are written to the test output and the report; and a **report-only concurrency leg** is added: K = min(8, processor count) folds of K fresh `VerifiedLog`s started together, wall time per fold against the serial figure and process CPU seconds per fold, so that item 278 holds the contention number as well as the quiet one.

### The text fixes (ruling 54) and the findings they come from

**54. Eight substitutions in the errata for four findings, each an exact old-to-new string that Task 3c applies (the entry's `Status` line is Task 8's), each a finding the blind builder would otherwise find for itself.** (a) **R6.66 contradicts R6.67 step 6 and vector 12.** [read] R6.66 says a later `log.operator-key` entry "holds no position and changes nothing a row is decided by", and R6.67 step 6 and vector 12 say a row naming the **second** operator key is `act-key-not-first`, which needs that key to **hold a position** (it is listed, `operator_keys`, "in log order"). The C# rule is Amendment 2's ruling 40: a later operator entry for **another key** is listed and is not the first; an entry that repeats a `kid`, or whose key the log published first in another class, or a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` that publishes a held operator key (by `kid` or by public key), holds no position. A reader of R6.66 as written decides vector 12 `act-key-unpublished`. (b) **R6.67 step 3 gains the types** of the closed form (ruling 51 (iv)); step 5 says **detached** in words (a compact JWS whose payload part is empty); step 6 says a header with no `kid` names none; the closing paragraph says a later attestation naming another owner **sets nothing** (the owner binding is R4.1's, the flag is the latest honoured attestation's naming the bound owner). (c) **R10.60 says the opposite of R4.43.** [read, and it was not in G70's revisions table as a *text* change, only as an annotation] R10.60 (errata) reads "For the human arm, R10.37's 'signed' is discharged by the record being a leaf (R6.46) under a head signed with the log key (R6.49): a human moderator holds no key". After G70 the human holds a key and the leaf is not what "signed" means (R4.43, R10.37 `(add.)`). A blind builder given R10.61 and R10.60 reads two rules; the sentence is **replaced**. (d) **R11.97 lacks the refusal that keeps a writer from appending a row it knows will be skipped** (ruling 56 (b)); one sentence is added, and the log-key publisher's refusal (`curia/keys/log-key-is-operator-key`) is named beside `publish-operator-key`'s. The exact strings are in "What Task 3c does", step 1 (A, B1 to B4, C, D1, D2). **No requirement number is allocated:** every substitution is inside R6.66, R6.67, R10.60 or R11.97, and the revisions table of G70 carries them as it carries R6.62's annotation (Task 8 writes the table row).

### Tasks 3c, 4 and 5: order, owners, files

**55. A Task 3c is inserted between Task 3 and Tasks 4 and 5, and it runs alone.** It owns **everything under `conformance/`**, the generator, the eight errata substitutions and the restated OAS30m; Task 5 owns `rust/` and Task 4 owns the writers, the tool and the tests around them. **Why before, and why alone:** (i) Task 5 is blind and its worth is that it reads the corpus **once**, finished (Amendment 3's reason, now stronger: this amendment adds 22 vectors and three text changes, and a builder who read the old corpus has read a different specification); (ii) Task 3c's falsification patches `OperatorActFold.cs` and rebuilds, and Task 4's builds and its whole-Api run read the same `bin/`: [obs] Task 3 lost a batch to "a concurrent stray driver" on exactly this; two builders of one solution at once is the defect, not a risk. Tasks 4 and 5 then run in parallel (ruling 58). The cost is one short task (about the size of Task 2) and Task 5's start moving by that much, which Amendment 3 already preferred to a corrected corpus read twice.

**56. What Task 4 decides, which the plan left open (its brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-4-brief.md`).** (a) **The signer is a parameter of `RecordAsync`, not of the constructor.** [read] `Curia.Api/Program.cs:289` and `:308` register `AttestOwner` and `ApplyModeration` as singletons (for the end-to-end fixture, which resolves them from the host); a constructor that takes `IOperatorActSigner` would make the Forum's composition root build a signer, and OAS17 clause (a) and R4.43 ("the Forum SHALL NOT hold, read or be configured with the operator-acts key") say it must not. The two use cases gain a `LifecycleVerifier` in the constructor (the Forum's registration resolves the existing singleton) and take the signer **per call**; the Forum's registrations pass no signer, the operator tool passes its key's, the fixture passes a test twin. (b) **A writer refuses before appending when the key it holds is not the log's first operator key** (`curia/keys/operator-key-not-published`, a `KeyActErrors` constant beside `DeclarationKeyNotPublishedType`, exit 2, nothing written), **and still re-reads after appending** (R11.97: the check that catches a race). [read] Without the first, an operator who forgot `publish-operator-key` appends a row the Forum then skips: it stays in the log for ever, `lifecycle-audit` exits 7 for ever, and `sign-head --if-grown` stops the unattended signer until a person signs (Q-N2-3): **the operator's own mistake would be the surface the credential holder's forged row is**. R6.62's own sentence ("SHALL NOT sign a declaration under a key the log has not published") is the precedent. This also corrects Task 1's **OAS16 control** [read: `OperatorActsOperatorTests.cs:57-61`: `attest-owner` with a fresh, **unpublished** `Pem()` expecting exit 0, which neither design can satisfy]: Task 4 publishes the key first in the control's arrange and records the edit as a correction to a Task 1 probe. (c) **New problem types:** `curia/acts/not-honoured` in a **new `ActErrors` class** (`Curia.Application.Credentials`, a new file; not `KeyActErrors`: that class is the one `R4_18_...` reflects over, and a writer-only type placed there is either a false row in a route table or a deliberate 500 that needs the test's `raisedByTheRoutes` list edited); `curia/keys/operator-key-not-published` and `curia/keys/log-key-is-operator-key` in `KeyActErrors` (the log-key publisher's refusal, OAS19b), **both added to `StatusForKeyAct`'s 409 list in `ForumEndpoints.cs`** with the sentence the G67 refusals carry ("the operator tool's, and no route reaches them"), because `R4_18_...` demands it. **Every table where a new type can appear is named in the brief's allowlist** (ruling 47): `StatusForKeyAct`, `OperatorCommands.RefuseAsync` and the **inline refusal branch of `attest-owner`** [read: `Program.cs:392-398` prints the error itself and returns `ExitCode.Refused` for every error without calling `RefuseAsync`, so exit 7 would never be reached from that verb; it is routed through `RefuseAsync`], `lifecycle-audit`'s exit and the usage text. (d) **`ForumFixture`'s four writers sign with `InMemoryOperatorActSigner`, whose key the fixture publishes lazily, once, on its first signed act** (not at fixture start: a `log.operator-key` event at every start would move every test that counts the log, a tree size or a log index). Tests whose literal expectation of a log position or count moves by that one event are edited and **listed**; a test whose edit is anything else stops the task. (e) **The writers' statements are composed from the errata's closed forms, not from the fold's `Kind` table**, and a probe (OAS13b) parses the closed forms out of R6.67's paragraph and compares the **members of the writer's output** with them: OAS13 (the fold decides the writer's row honoured) is a contact between two artifacts only if the writer was not written from the fold. (f) **`HonouredCount` and the audit's last line.** [read] `HonouredCount()` is lifecycle-only; `lifecycle-audit` prints `honoured <h> skipped <s>` over **both kinds** (R11.97), so `VerifiedLog` gains an all-kinds honoured count and an all-kinds `SkippedPast(treeSize)`; the lifecycle-only log prints **the bytes it printed before** (OAS21's golden, captured from the unmodified operator first).

**57. What Task 5 decides.** (a) **Builder B works in a sparse copy of the repository, outside it,** holding `rust/curia-testis/` (without `target/`), `conformance/` and the white paper (one Rust test reads it) and B's requirements file: no `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/`, `tools/`, `.superpowers/` and no plan, so blindness is a property of the filesystem and the controller's check (the brief, Part A) is a second probe on it, not the only one. PR N's rounds relied on the instruction and a self-reported list of reads; the copy costs one `rsync`. (b) **OAS28 compares rows and state**, as OAS8 does, so `state` (an agent's `owner` and `verified`, a post's `served`) is part of what B decides from R4.1, R6.67's closing paragraph and R10.61. (c) **OAS20d** (the Rust key-set parser reads a key set that carries `operator_keys` as it reads one without) is Task 5's, as Amendment 2 ruling 39 said. (d) **OAS29 stays Task 6's first step** (it needs both folds). (e) **The loader**: the Rust runner does not know the family [obs, Task 1: `cargo test --locked` fails in `tests/vectors.rs` with three lines about `operator-acts` and a charter total of 274 against 236]; Task 5 makes the four edits in `conformance.rs`, the two lists in `loader_errors.rs` and the pinned totals in `vectors.rs`, **to the corpus Task 3c leaves** (60 vectors: 290 directories and 296 in all with the six vendored pairs; B reads the number from the run's failure text, and the controller checks it against Task 3c's report). (f) **`conformance/` is not B's.** A vector that disagrees with B's reading is a finding for the architect: B leaves the case red or lists it in a named `EXPECTED_FAILURES` with the reason and does not edit the vector, the loader's expectation of it, or the errata text (it has none).

**58. Tasks 4 and 5 run in parallel because their file sets are disjoint; the shared things are named.** Task 4 writes `src/` (Application, Operator, Api), `tests/` (Application, Api, Architecture if a fence needs a row) and `.superpowers/.../task-4-*`; Task 5 writes `rust/curia-testis/` inside its copy and the controller copies back exactly the files its report lists; **neither writes `conformance/`, `tools/`, the errata, `docs/`, `db/`, `.github/`, or any other task's files.** Shared and not collisions: (i) **`git status --short`** lists the other task's files: each brief scopes its own checks by path (`-- . ':!rust'` for Task 4, `-- rust` for Task 5); (ii) **the machine**: `dotnet` builds, the Api suite's databases and `cargo` builds run at once; Task 4's timed re-measure of `AttestOwner` (ruling 25's M2) and any timing must not run while a cargo build runs (the brief says so and asks for the load to be recorded); (iii) **the commits are the controller's, one per task, by explicit path.** A task that finds it needs a file on the other's list **stops and says so** (ruling 47).

### What Task 3c does (the brief is the controller's to cut from this section; it is Task 3's with these differences)

Base: the Task 3 commit and Amendment 4. **Rules as Task 3's brief** (no version-control writes; restore a falsification patch by plain `cp`, `touch`, `--no-incremental`; foreground or a bounded poll, never `tail -f`; the three-attempts rule; scratch notes are data; no username, home path or host in anything written; drop `curia_e2e_*` and `curia_n2_*` databases after the last run). **Files it may write, and nothing else:** `curia-whitepaper-ERRATA-AND-ADDENDUM.md` (the eight substitutions of step 1, **nothing else in it**), `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures/OperatorActs.cs`, `conformance/operator-acts/` (22 new directories; **the 38 existing are byte-identical afterwards**: `git diff --stat -- conformance/operator-acts` shows additions only), `conformance/index.json` (the count), `conformance/README.md` (the section's counts and one paragraph for 39 to 60, and its "what these do not pin"), `tests/Curia.Canon.Tests/Vectors/OperatorActsFamilyTests.cs` (`Cases = 60`), `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/OperatorActs/OperatorActFoldTests.cs` and `OperatorActsCostLog.cs` (OAS30m, step 4), `src/Curia.Application/Credentials/OperatorActFold.cs` (**only** to fix a disagreement the protocol of Task 3's rule 10 assigns to the fold, each recorded; and the falsification patches, each restored), `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-3c-report.md` and `keep/t3c/`. **Not:** every other file (the `rust/` crate, `db/`, `.github/`, `docs/`, the plan, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `src/Curia.Api`, `src/Curia.Operator`, `src/Curia.Client`, `src/Curia.Mcp`, `tests/Curia.Api.Tests`, every other `src/` file).

1. **The eight errata substitutions (A, B1 to B4, C, D1, D2)**, applied by one script that asserts each Old occurs **exactly once** and applies the groups independently (print each failure; a mismatch of one does not drop the rest), then `python3 tools/spec-checks/check-spec.py` and `python3 tools/spec-checks/falsify-spec-checks.py`, both clean. (A) R6.66. Old: `A later `log.operator-key` entry, and any entry that publishes the operator key's public key as a log key or a declaration key, holds no position and changes nothing a row is decided by: a key's class is the class under which the log first published it.` New: `A later `log.operator-key` entry for another key is listed after the first (in `operator_keys`) and is not the operator key: a row whose header `kid` names it is `act-key-not-first` (R6.67 step 6). A `log.operator-key` entry that lists a `kid` an earlier entry of that class lists, or whose key (by `kid` or by public key) the log published earlier as a log key or a declaration key, holds no position; so does a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` entry that publishes (by `kid` or by public key) a key an earlier `log.operator-key` entry that holds a position lists. None of these changes what a row is decided by after it is decided: a key's class is the class under which the log first published it.` (B1) R6.67 step 3. Old: `or is not the closed form of its kind, or` New: `or is not the closed form of its kind (exactly the members listed below, each a string except `owner_verified`, a boolean, `prev`, a non-negative integer, and `adjudicates`, an array of strings), or`. (B2) step 5. Old: `5. The row has no `authorization` that is a detached JWS whose header `typ` is` New: `5. The row has no `authorization` that is a detached JWS (a compact JWS whose payload part is empty, `<header>..<signature>`, RFC 7515 Appendix F) whose header `typ` is`. (B3) step 6. Old: `6. The authorization's header `kid` names no operator key published at an earlier log index than the row (R6.66): `act-key-unpublished`;` New: `6. The authorization's header `kid` (a header with no `kid` names none) names no operator key published at an earlier log index than the row (R6.66): `act-key-unpublished`;`. (B4) closing paragraph. Old: `(R4.1: a later honoured attestation naming another owner is honoured as an act and binds nothing) and sets whether that owner is verified;` New: `(R4.1: a later honoured attestation naming another owner is honoured as an act, binds nothing and sets nothing, whatever `owner_verified` it states) and sets whether that owner is verified (the `owner_verified` of the latest honoured attestation naming the bound owner);`. (C) R10.60. Old, **three line breaks included** (copy it from the file; the errata wraps this paragraph): `For the human` / `arm, R10.37's "signed" is discharged by the record being a leaf (R6.46) under a head signed with the` / `log key (R6.49): a human moderator holds no key, and a per-entry signature would add nothing against` / `the party R6.25 exists to hold to account, which can withhold without writing any record at all.` New (one logical sentence, wrapped as the file wraps): `For the human arm, R10.37's "signed" is discharged, since errata G70, by the record's `statement` and `authorization` under the operator-acts key (R6.66, R6.67), which a verifier checks: the operator holds that key and the Forum holds none (R4.43). Being a leaf (R6.46) under a head signed with the log key (R6.49) shows only that the record is in the log.` (D1) R11.97. Old: `SHALL sign the act under the operator-acts key (`CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`), append it,` New: `SHALL refuse, before signing and appending nothing, a key the log has not published as the operator key (`curia/keys/operator-key-not-published`, exit 2), and otherwise SHALL sign the act under the operator-acts key (`CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`), append it,`. (D2) Old: `` `publish-operator-key --by <role>` SHALL publish the key once and refuse a second, and a key already published in another class.`` New: the same sentence followed by ` `sign-head` SHALL refuse to publish a log key that is an operator key (`curia/keys/log-key-is-operator-key`, exit 2).` That is eight old strings for four findings (a: A; b: B1 to B4; c: C; d: D1 and D2). **The plan's own copy of the G70 text (its section "The errata entry") is superseded by the errata from here; nothing edits it.**
2. **The generator: 22 cases.** Add to `OaAttest` and `OaModerate` only what the table needs: an override of the **header** (typ, alg, kid omitted, none), an **attached** form (the compact JWS `header.base64url(statement bytes).signature`, the same signature the detached form carries), **no authorization**, an **authorization with no statement** (vector 39), a **raw statement text** (members out of JCS order; a `reason` with a decomposed accent), **typed members** (a string `prev`, a negative `prev`, a string `owner_verified`, `adjudicates` of `[1]`), **row members omitted**, and a **control row** appended after the defective one. The generator writes each `expected.json` from the case's construction, **never by running the fold**; it signs with `SignData(..., IeeeP1363FixedFieldConcatenation)` over `header.payload` for the header overrides (the existing `Signing.Sign` builds only the standard header; read it first). **Characters that are invisible or decompose are built from code points** (`(char)0x0301`), never typed: a tool call can decode an escape into a literal invisible character (scan the added lines for U+0301 and every other Cf, Cc, Zl, Zp, Zs). (Task 8 re-greps the traps for the first free number.)
- Trap **270 (proposed):** *a requirement paragraph that was right when written can be contradicted by the one paragraph an amendment did not edit.* R10.60's "a human moderator holds no key" survived an entry (G70) that gave the human a key; the blind builder is the reader who is shown both.

### Rulings on Task 4's stop

**60. The re-cut Task 4 may edit exactly these further files, by these classes of edit, and does not stop on the next gap.** [read] The stop was right about the two classes of caller it named and did not see three more. The full list, found by `grep -rlE "\bAttestOwner\b|\bApplyModeration\b"` over `src`, `tests` and `tools` and by `grep -rn '"attest-owner"\|"moderate"'` over `tests`:

(a) **Application tests that construct a writer** (`KeyPositionTests.cs:150,184`, `SearchProjectorTests.cs:258`, `AgentStandingProjectorTests.cs:77`, `RaiseFlagTests.cs:410`, `FlagWordsTests.cs:48`, `ApplyModerationTests.cs` (58 `RecordAsync`/constructor sites), `RationaleReferenceTests.cs:95,101`) **change their construction lines and nothing else of their own**. The mechanism is one new helper, `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/OperatorActs/SignedActWriters.cs` (new, on the list): `SignedActWriters.Attest(store, clock)` and `SignedActWriters.Moderate(store, details, clock)` return thin wrappers (`SignedAttestOwner`, `SignedApplyModeration`) whose `RecordAsync` has **the old parameter list** and forwards to the real use case with an `InMemoryOperatorActSigner`, a `LifecycleVerifier` over the harness's ES256 verifier, and the operator key published into the same store through `PublishOperatorKey` once, before the wrapper's first act (a store that already holds an operator key is left alone). Every `RecordAsync` call line of the seven files is then unchanged, and the diff of each is its constructor lines. **Allowed besides, each listed in the report with the old and the new literal:** (i) a literal expectation of a log position, a tree size or an event count that moves by the one `log.operator-key` event (Amendment 4, ruling 56 (d), extended from the Api suite to these files); (ii) a seed whose stream does not begin with its root (`agent.enrolled` for an agent's stream, `post.accepted` for a post's) and so would make every row `act-broken-link`: the seed is made to begin with the root, in that test only. A test that needs anything else: see (g).

(b) **Api tests that run the operator verbs with their own helper** (`HeadsSignedUnattendedTests.cs` `OperatorAsync` `:70`, `ModerationLoopTests.cs` `:80`, `OperatorAttestationTests.cs` `:31`, `OperatorModerationTests.cs` `:30`, `ShadowRowWritersTests.cs` `OperatorAsync` `:432`): **the helper only** passes the new last optional parameter `readOperatorActsSigningKeyPem` of `OperatorCommands.RunAsync`, taken from a new `ForumFixture` member (`OperatorActsKeyAsync(ct)`: publishes the fixture's key once, under a `SemaphoreSlim`, and returns its PEM) which is the same key the fixture's four writers sign with. Without it `attest-owner` and `moderate` refuse exit 3 (no key) before the log is read, and every test of a refusal path (a missing agent, a missing post) would read exit 3 for exit 2. The literal-position allowance of (a) (i) applies. `ShadowRowWritersTests.cs`'s doc comment (`:91-95`) says `AttestOwner` is a by-aggregate reader and `ApplyModeration` reads nothing by aggregate; after this task the first is false: **the sentence may be corrected in words, no assertion touched.** The other 62 Api files that name the fixture's writers call `AttestOwnerAsync`, `WithholdAsync`, `QuarantineAsync`, `RestoreAsync`, whose signatures do not change: untouched, and they are the regression check.

(c) **Architecture: one test file, in one direction.** `LifecycleShapeFenceTests.cs`: the row `("Curia.Application.Credentials.AttestOwner", ...)` of `ByAggregateReaders` **is removed** (the type stops calling `ReadByAggregateAsync` and reads the whole log, so the row would be "stale" and the test red), and `"Curia.Application.Credentials.AttestOwner"` and `"Curia.Application.Moderation.ApplyModeration"` **are added to `LifecycleDecisions`**, which asserts that each is found in the scanned IL, reads `ReadAllAsync` and is not a by-aggregate reader. **That is a strengthening, not a weakening:** the listed by-aggregate readers shrink by one, and a future `AttestOwner` that goes back to reading its own stream is red. **`PortStringFenceTests.cs:55` and `PortStringAllowlist.cs:22` need no change** [read]: the fence keys a row by `<DeclaringType>.<Method>` and the parameter's **name** (`agentId`, `reason`), not by signature; the new parameters (`IOperatorActSigner`, the constructor's `LifecycleVerifier`) are not string-like; the two rows stay in scope because `AttestOwner.RecordAsync` stays host-referenced by the operator tool. **If a fence nevertheless demands a row for a member this task adds** (a host-called member with a string-like parameter), the implementer **may add** one row to `PortStringAllowlist.cs`, only for that member and parameter, with a reason in one of the established classifications (a Forum-minted value, host configuration, a stored value, free text that is never stored or looked up) and never a `"*"` row; each is listed in the report; **no row is removed, edited or loosened.** `CredentialErrorTypeFenceTests`, `LayeringTests`, `JsonReaderFenceTests`, the IL fences, HSU1 and HSU6 are not edited: a red there is a finding about the shape of what was added, to be fixed in the shape.

(d) **Operator `Program.cs`, Api `Program.cs`, `ForumEndpoints.StatusForKeyAct`, `ForumFixture.cs`, `OperatorActsApi.cs`, `VerifiedStopSupport.cs`**: already on Task 4's list; the registrations resolve the `LifecycleVerifier` the Forum already holds (`Curia.Api/Program.cs:133`). **Not touched, and not needed:** `tests/Curia.Infrastructure.Tests` (`PostgresFlagDetailStoreTests.cs` names `ApplyModeration` in a comment), `tools/` (no caller), `src/Curia.Client*`, `src/Curia.Mcp`, `ci.yml`, `db/`.

(e) **The order inside the writers is kept**, so no existing test reads a different refusal: (1) the checks that read no log (the operator prefix, a blank operator name, the reason or rationale, R10.68's size cap, which a test pins as happening before any read); (2) `ReadAllAsync`; (3) the honoured pre-append refusals in their old order; (4) the key refusal (`curia/keys/operator-key-not-published`); (5) compose, sign, append; (6) re-read.

(f) **A stream with no root.** If the stream's first event is not of the root's type (R6.67: "the stream's root is its first event"), no `prev` can be computed and the fold would skip every row `act-broken-link`. The writer then **refuses before signing with the refusal the existing check gives for an identity or a post the log does not hold** (`NotEnrolled` for an attestation, `NoSuchPost` for a moderation): no new type. [read] Task 4's brief's step (5) said "else the stream's root" and did not say what a writer does without one.

(g) **The stop rule is narrowed.** The task stops only on: an edit to a consumer (a Forum decision reading `HonouredEvents`, `Standings()`, `Moderation()` or `ActSkipped()` beyond the writers' own reads and EventId 7010's scan), a change to `db/` or `ci.yml`, a fence loosened, or a patch to a file outside rule 3's list and this ruling. **Any other test file that stops compiling or fails because a writer's signature changed or the log holds one more event is edited by classes (a) (i), (a) (ii) or (b) and listed; one that needs a different edit is left as it is, written into the report under "left" with the failure text, and the task goes on with everything that does not depend on it.** A gap the amendment did not foresee is the controller's to rule after the task, not a stop.

**61. Item 300 is restated: half of its first form was wrong.** [obs: `task-4-report.md` section 4; `keep/t4/oas19c-red.log`, `oas19d-red.log`; read in `ActaProjector.cs:270-285`] `ActaLog.Keys` is the Acta's own list: it keeps a repeated-`kid` `log.key` entry (it removes only an entry the operator class owns, `ofOperatorClass`), so `sign-head`'s `acta.Keys.Length` equalled the aggregate's event count for a repeated kid and the old code happened to work; only `PublishedKeys.LogKeys`, the verifier's list, drops it. **What is red on the unmodified tree is the republishing-by-public-key form** (R6.66 rule (c): a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` entry that publishes a held operator key under another `kid`, which `ActaLog` removes and the aggregate still holds): for `sign-head`, OAS19c's first leg; for `PublishDeclarationKey`, **both** of OAS19d's legs (a declaration-key list that excludes a row the aggregate holds). **Restated register text, replacing the plan's (300):** *(300) (working label; Task 8 re-greps the first free number) a publisher that passes a projection's length as an aggregate's expected version conflicts after any row the projection leaves out: `sign-head`'s `log:keys` append (`acta.Keys.Length`) and `PublishDeclarationKey` (`acta.DeclarationKeys.Length`) after a `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` row that republishes the operator key's public half (R6.66 rule (c): `ActaLog` removes it, the aggregate holds it), and `sign-head`'s `log:heads` append (`acta.Heads.Length`, ruling 62) after a well-formed `log.head` row in another aggregate; one `INSERT` by the credential holder stalls the first head or the first declaration key, or every head after it, until someone finds the row; a repeated-`kid` `log.key` row does not (the Acta lists it, and OAS19c's second leg is a characterization that says so). Closed by Task 4a (the first two) and the re-cut Task 4 (the third): the version is the count of the aggregate's events in the log read; OAS19c, OAS19d, OAS19e; FOAS37, FOAS38.* The plan's and Amendment 3's wording ("a repeated-`kid` row ... already did the same on the tree before N2") is **superseded** by this paragraph; ruling 44's other content stands.

**62. `sign-head`'s `log:heads` append is the same bug, and the re-cut Task 4 fixes it with a probe.** [read, `Program.cs:556`: `AppendAsync(store, ids, LogEntries.HeadsAggregate, acta.Heads.Length, ...)`; `ActaLog.Heads` is every `log.head` entry that reads as a head, in any aggregate (`ActaProjector.cs:ReadHead`)] A well-formed `log.head` row in **another aggregate** is a head the projection counts and `log:heads` does not hold, so the version is one too high and the store refuses every head from then on: **this is the unattended signer's availability, the thing R6.63 and Q-N2-3 exist to protect, stalled by one `INSERT` of a shape any holder of the credential can write** (R6.63's own reason: "a head is an entry any holder of the Forum's database credential can append in a shape that reads as one"). It is not N2's row, but it is item 300's class, the lines are Task 4's already, and the fix is one expression: **the expected version is `acta.Events.Count(e => e.AggregateId.Value == LogEntries.HeadsAggregate)`**. **Probe OAS19e** (Api, process, a theory of two): a log holding a `log.operator-key` and a first head, then (leg 1) a well-formed `log.head` row appended in an aggregate other than `log:heads`, then `sign-head --by ops` with the same log key: expected exit 0, a second head in `log:heads`, `log:heads` holding 2 events (counted from the database); (leg 2) the control, with no such row. **Red text [predicted]:** exit 2, `curia/domain/concurrency-conflict`, nothing appended; the brief records the actual. **Falsification T4F24:** `acta.Heads.Length` back, red on leg 1. [predicted] Not filed with a gate: a gate would leave the operator's own timer stallable by anyone with the credential for the whole of PR O and the hosting join, and the fix costs less than the register entry.

### B's twelve decisions, each ruled (the C# fold is `OperatorActFold.cs` and `PublishedKeys.cs`; Rust is `operator_acts.rs`)

The method: each decision is read against R6.66, R6.67, R6.62 and R10.61 **as the errata stands after Task 3c**, then against the C# line that decides the same thing. **Where the folds agree and the text decides, no action; where they agree and nothing in the corpus pins it, a vector; where they disagree, the ruling says which follows and why, and both a text edit and a vector are named.** All 60 existing vectors agree, so every disagreement below is something the 60 do not pin.

**63. Decisions 1, 3, 5, 12: both folds agree, no action** (one vector for 5). (1) `LogEntry` has no actor: an implementation detail of the Rust reader; both compare the event's actor with the statement's `actor_id`. (3) *Which entries read as a key*: C# `ReadEntry` requires `ClosedJwk.Read(jwk, kid)` (R6.62's closed form with the payload `kid` the key's own) and ignores `valid_from`; Rust uses `is_readable_key_entry` and the payload `kid` equal to the key's, and ignores `valid_from`: the same set. (12) the loader's `MalformedOperatorActsVector` is a runner detail. (5) *An off-curve operator key holds a position and fails at step 7*: [read] `ClosedJwk.cs:12-13` says on-curve is asked "of a key that signs, at the signature", `Material` returns null, step 7 answers `act-signature-invalid`; Rust's `jwk: None` does the same. R6.62 settles it ("whether a point lies on its curve is asked of the declaring key alone, at the signature"). **One vector** (62 below): a fold that asked on-curve at publication would decide `act-key-unpublished` and every one of the 60 would still pass.

**64. Decision 2, the stream's root: both folds agree, the text is explicit, one vector.** [read] C#: `Stream(firstIndex, firstType)` is the aggregate's **first event whatever its type**, and the root exists only if that type is the root's (`OperatorActFold.cs:96-104`, `:158`); Rust: `events.first().filter(|e| e.event_type == root_type)`. R6.67: "the stream's root is its first event (`agent.enrolled` ... `post.accepted` ...)". The alternative reading (the first event *of the root's type*) differs when a forged row sits before the enrolment, and **no vector has a stream whose first event is not the root's type and holds one later** (`attestation-with-no-root` has no root at all). **Vector 61.** The writer's `prev` follows the same rule and refuses without a root (ruling 60 (f)).

**65. Decisions 4 and 7, which entries hold a position: the folds DISAGREE in two places, and the C# fold follows R6.62's wording.** [read] (i) **A `kid` listed twice.** R6.62: "A `kid` listed more than once is the key of its earliest listing, and a later entry under it is not a key", and R6.66: an entry that "lists a `kid` an earlier entry of that class lists" holds no position. C#: `kids.Add(entry.Kid)` for **every entry that reads as a key, whether or not it holds a position**; Rust: `operator.iter().any(|(k, _)| k.kid == key.kid)`, over the **position-holders only**. They part when an operator entry E1 (kid K) holds none because the log published its key as a log key, and a later E2 lists K with another key: C# gives E2 no position; Rust gives it one, and it becomes **the** operator key. **Ruling:** C#, because "lists" is the word of both rules and an entry that lists a `kid` lists it whether or not it holds a position. (ii) **A log key that repeats a `kid` publishes nothing.** C#: such a `log.key` entry holds no position, so it is not in `heldLogOrDeclaration` and its public key does not make a later `log.operator-key` entry "published elsewhere"; Rust pushes **every** readable `log.key` and `log.declaration-key` entry into `other`, repeated or not, so a later operator entry for that entry's public key is skipped. **Ruling:** C#: "a later entry under it is not a key" (R6.62) means the log did not publish it, and R6.66's clash is with a key "the log published". **Rust changes** (per-class kid sets for the log-key and declaration-key classes; an entry is added to the clash list only when it holds a position); **text edits E1a and E1b** below; **vectors 63 and 64**. Decision 4's other half (a key held by an entry that holds no position is no input to a later clash) is the same ruling, now written.

**66. Decision 6, step 5's header: DISAGREE on a header that repeats a member (and, by the same cause, on anything ADMIT refuses). Rust follows: the header is read as ADMIT reads a document.** [read] C# `ParseHeader` uses `JsonDocument.Parse` and `TryGetProperty`, which tolerates a repeated member name (RFC 7515 section 4 allows a verifier either to reject it or to take the lexically last) and a number ADMIT would refuse; Rust reads the header with the crate's ADMIT parser, which **rejects** a repeated member name at any depth, so the row fails step 5 (`act-typ-mismatch`). A header `{"kid":"<stranger>","kid":"<O>", ...}` signed validly under O is honoured by a last-wins reader and skipped by Rust: **a verifier's choice becomes the Forum's act**, the exact shape of the R6.37 ambiguity the Rust `verify_typed` comment names. **Ruling:** reject, because the project's invariant is that a document with a repeated member is refused and never repaired (R6.12 to R6.17, the ADMIT profile); **the C# fold changes** (step 5 parses the header bytes with the guarded reader and a failure is `act-typ-mismatch`, before `ReadProtectedHeader`); **text edit E3**; **vector 65**, whose signature is valid under both orders of the repeated `kid`'s reading, so that last-wins honours it and first-wins skips it as `act-key-unpublished`: either way C# disagrees. Not decided here: the base64 trailing-bit leniency of the two decoders (see "What I am unsure of").

**67. Decision 8, `b64` and `crit`: both folds require them at step 7, the text is silent, a text edit and two vectors.** [read] C#: `DetachedJws.Verify` refuses a header whose `b64` is not `false` (`B64MustBeFalse`) or whose `crit` is not exactly `["b64"]` (`CritUnsupported`) **before** the primitive, and the fold answers `act-signature-invalid`; Rust: `verify_typed` does the same (`B64NotFalse`, `CritInvalid`). R6.67 step 7 says "does not verify over the statement's canonical form under that key" and names neither; R6.67 step 5 asks `typ` and `alg`. **Does R6.67 require `b64: false`? By reference only (R6.66's header sentence says "a signature under the key carries header `typ` `curia-operator-act+jws`, `alg` `ES256` and the key's `kid`"), and a reader who verifies a plain RFC 7515 detached JWS (signing input `header.base64url(payload)`) honours a row both folds skip, passing all 60 vectors.** Text edit **E4** (step 7 says what the signing input is and that R6.37's header holds) and **vectors 66 and 67** (`b64` absent with a signature over the RFC 7515 input; `crit` absent with `b64: false` and a signature over the RFC 7797 input). Each is **valid under the other reading**, which the generator asserts by verifying it (a probe on the vector's construction, since no one-line fold patch removes `DetachedJws`'s check: see the falsification rows).

**68. Decision 9, step 3: the folds DISAGREE in two places and agree on the rest.** Agree: the closed form's member names and types (C# `IsClosedForm`, Rust `member_type_ok`), step 2 applying only to a statement that parses as an admitted object with a string `typ`, the actor and subject comparison, `moderator` `human`. **Disagree:** (i) **What "differs from" means for a row's member.** C# compares `CanonicalizeWithNfc` of both values, so a row stating `reason` as `e` + U+0301 beside a statement stating `é` is "the same", and the row is **honoured**; Rust compares the JSON values exactly, so it is skipped `act-statement-mismatch`. R6.67 does not say. **Ruling: exact** (strings code point for code point, never normalized): a row member is a *copy* of the signed one, and a consumer that reads the row (the raw projectors do, ruling 71) must read the bytes the signature covers; a normalizing comparison is a place where an unsigned spelling stands in for a signed one. **C# changes** (`RowAgrees`/`SameValue` compare `CanonicalJson.Canonicalize`, JCS without NFC); **text edit E2** (the same clause); **vector 68**. (ii) **The values of `method`, `effect` and `category`.** The closed form types them as strings; C#'s consumers (`ApplyOwnerAttestation`, `FlagProjector.ApplyModeration`) **drop** an honoured row whose `method` is not one of R4.24's four, whose `effect` is not one of the four, or whose `category` is not one of R10.35's seven, while Rust (decision 11) binds the owner and holds the category for any string. So the same honoured row **changes the state in one fold and not the other**; and, worse, an honoured **withhold** with a category the projector cannot read serves what a human withheld (the comment in `FlagProjection.cs:172` names the failure). **Ruling:** the closed form closes the values: a statement whose `method`, `effect` or `category` is outside those sets is **not the closed form of its kind** (`act-statement-mismatch`), so no honoured row carries a value a consumer cannot read and "honoured" means "acted on". **Both folds change** (C#: `IsClosedForm` asks the Domain parsers `OwnerVerificationMethods.Parse`, `ModerationEffects.Parse`, `FlagKinds.Parse`; Rust: three constant sets); **text edit E2**; **vectors 69, 70, 71**.

**69. Decision 10, an agent's state: both folds agree on every corpus row and differ on a row that omits a member the statement states; the statement is the authority and Task 3b carries the fix.** [read] Rust takes `owner_id`, `owner_verified` and `method` from the **statement** (the signed bytes); the C# state is the raw projector over `HonouredEvents`, which reads them from the **row** (`AgentStandingProjection.cs:368-392`: a row without `owner_id` or `owner_verified` is ignored; `FlagProjection.cs:158-170` likewise for a moderation row). Step 3 allows a row to omit a member (the vectors 56, 58 and 59 depend on it). Every honoured row the shipped writers write states all of them, so nothing reaches the difference today. **Ruling:** the state follows the **statement**, since it is what the key signed (R4.43: "decide from the attestations it honours alone"); **no vector in this amendment** (the C# state half is the projectors', which Task 3b reaches, and a vector now would redden OAS8's state half with no task to fix it); **Task 3b's obligation**: `HonouredEvents` hands the projectors an honoured act row whose payload members are the statement's, in memory and never persisted, and Task 3b adds the vector (an honoured attestation whose row omits `owner_id`, `owner_verified` and `method`, and a moderation row that omits `effect` and `category`; expected state from the statement) and its falsification row. Recorded as open in the register until then.

**70. Decision 11, R10.61's held-category set: both folds implement the text; the README's shorter sentence is wrong; one vector.** [read] C# `ModerationPolicy.ServingEffect` keeps a dictionary keyed by category (quarantine or withhold sets it, a restore citing that category removes it, a dismissal does nothing; served iff empty), and Rust keeps the same set: both are R10.61 ("a permitted quarantine or withholding SHALL hold its post in the category it cites until a permitted restore citing that category; a post SHALL be served only while nothing holds it"). `conformance/README.md:645-646` says `served` is "false while the last honoured record is a `withhold` or a `quarantine`" and `verified` is "the last honoured attestation's `owner_verified`": both shorter than the text (vector 60 already pins the second against it). **README edit R1** (below); **vector 72**: withhold in `spam`, quarantine in `incorrect`, restore in `spam`: `served` is **false** (the last record is a restore; `incorrect` still holds). Not provokable in C#: the state half is the Domain's `ServingEffect`, not the fold (T5bF9 patches Rust only; Task 7 holds the row for C#).

**71. Where a row's state is read from is therefore one rule, and the corpus now says so in two places:** a row member the row states equals the statement's (vector 68), a row may omit one (vectors 56, 58, 59), and **the state is the statement's** (Task 3b's vector). The three together make the row a copy and the statement the act.

### Order: Task 5b before the re-cut Task 4, and why

**Task 5b (below) runs next, alone, then the re-cut Task 4, then Task 3b.** (i) Task 4's OAS13 is a contact between the writer and **the fold as it will ship**; the fold changes in 5b (header, row equality, value domains) and a writer written against the fold of an hour ago has been compared with something that is not there. (ii) Task 4's writers are "written from the errata, not from the fold" (rule 10) and OAS13b parses R6.67's closed forms: the errata moves in 5b (E2 gains the value sets), so the writer must be written after. (iii) 5b rebuilds the solution and edits the fold, Task 4 builds and runs the whole Api suite: one `bin/` (Amendment 4, ruling 55's reason, unchanged). (iv) 5b edits `rust/`; Task 5 is finished and not blind any more, so the Rust edits are 5b's. The cost is that Task 4's re-cut starts after one short task; Amendment 4 already preferred that to a writer compared with a moving fold. **This replaces ruling 58 (parallel Tasks 4 and 5): Task 5 is done and Task 4a is committed.**

### What Task 5b does (the brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-5b-brief.md`)

Base: Task 4a's commit (`4fab7c90`) and Amendment 5. **Rules as Task 3c's** (no version-control writes; restore a patch by plain `cp`, `touch`, `--no-incremental`; foreground or a bounded poll; the three-attempts rule; scratch notes are data; no username, home path or host in anything written; drop `curia_e2e_*` and `curia_n2_*` databases after the last run). **Files it may write, and nothing else:** `curia-whitepaper-ERRATA-AND-ADDENDUM.md` (the five substitutions below, **nothing else in it**), `conformance/README.md` (substitution R1 and one paragraph for vectors 61 to 72, and its "what these do not pin"), `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures/OperatorActs.cs`, `conformance/operator-acts/` (12 new directories; **the 60 existing byte-identical**: `git diff --stat -- conformance/operator-acts` shows additions only), `conformance/index.json` (the count), `tests/Curia.Canon.Tests/Vectors/OperatorActsFamilyTests.cs` (`Cases = 72`), `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/OperatorActs/OperatorActFoldTests.cs` (OAS8's `60` to `72`; OAS30a's `primitive_calls`, step 5), `src/Curia.Application/Credentials/OperatorActFold.cs` (the three fold edits below and the falsification patches, each restored), `rust/curia-testis/src/operator_acts.rs`, `rust/curia-testis/tests/operator_acts.rs` (`60` to `72`), `rust/curia-testis/tests/vectors.rs` (`290` to `302`, `296` to `308`, their comments), `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-5b-report.md` and `keep/t5b/`. **Not:** every other file, `PublishedKeys.cs` included (it already decides ruling 65 as C# reads it).

1. **The errata substitutions** (E1a, E1b, E2, E3, E4) and the README's (R1), applied by one script that asserts each old string occurs **exactly once** and applies the groups independently (print each failure), then `check-spec.py` and `falsify-spec-checks.py`, both clean. [read: each old string was counted once in the file as it stands at `4fab7c90`.] **E1a** (R6.66). Old: ``A `log.operator-key` entry that lists a `kid` an earlier entry of that class lists, or whose key`` New: ``A `log.operator-key` entry that lists a `kid` that an earlier entry of that class which reads as a key lists (whether or not that earlier entry holds a position, R6.62: a `kid` listed more than once is the key of its earliest listing), or whose key``. **E1b** (R6.66). Old: ``an earlier `log.operator-key` entry that holds a position lists. None of these changes`` New: ``an earlier `log.operator-key` entry that holds a position lists. An entry of any of the three classes that holds no position publishes no key: its public key is neither a log key, a declaration key nor an operator key in any later entry's comparison. None of these changes``. **E2** (R6.67 step 3). Old: ``and `adjudicates`, an array of strings), or a member the row states beside `statement` and `authorization` is not the statement's member of the same name or differs from it,`` New: ``and `adjudicates`, an array of strings; and `method` is one of R4.24's four proofs (`domain`, `email`, `attestation`, `manual`), `effect` one of `quarantine`, `withhold`, `restore`, `dismiss`, and `category` one of R10.35's seven flag types), or a member the row states beside `statement` and `authorization` is not the statement's member of the same name or differs from it (the same JSON value: strings compared code point for code point, never normalized),``. **E3** (R6.67 step 5). Old: ``5. The row has no `authorization` that is a detached JWS (a compact JWS whose payload part is empty, `<header>..<signature>`, RFC 7515 Appendix F) whose header `typ` is`` New: ``5. The row has no `authorization` that is a detached JWS (a compact JWS whose payload part is empty, `<header>..<signature>`, RFC 7515 Appendix F) whose protected header is a JSON object that the ADMIT profile accepts (a repeated member name, at any depth, refuses it) and whose header `typ` is``. **E4** (R6.67 step 7). Old: ``7. The authorization does not verify over the statement's canonical form under that key: `act-signature-invalid`.`` New: ``7. The authorization does not verify over the statement's canonical form under that key, as R6.37 shapes a detached signature (the signing input is the encoded header, a full stop and the canonical form; the header's `b64` is `false` and its `crit` is exactly `["b64"]`): `act-signature-invalid`.`` **R1** (`conformance/README.md`, operator-acts section; the two physical lines are one old string with a line break). Old: ``` `verified` (the last honoured attestation's `owner_verified`, `false` when none); for a post's stream\n`served`, false while the last honoured record is a `withhold` or a `quarantine`.``` New: ``` `verified` (the `owner_verified` of the latest honoured attestation naming the bound owner, `false` when none); for a post's stream\n`served`, true only while no category holds the post (R10.61: a `withhold` or a `quarantine` holds the category it cites until a `restore` citing it; a dismissal holds nothing).``` **No requirement number is allocated:** every substitution is inside R6.66, R6.67 or the README; Task 8 writes the revisions-table row.
2. **The generator: 12 cases** (vectors 61 to 72 below). Add to `OaAttest` and `OaModerate` only what the table needs: an override of the **authorization's header bytes** (the text built by hand, with a repeated `kid`, with no `b64`, with no `crit`) and of the **signing input** (the RFC 7515 attached form `base64url(header).base64url(statement)`, the RFC 7797 form `base64url(header).statement`), a **row member that differs from the statement's only by normalization** (`reason` as `e` + `(char)0x0301`), a **statement `method`, `effect` or `category` override**, a **category** and a **`restore`'s category** on `OaModerate`, a **forged first event** in a stream, an **operator key whose point is off its curve** (x from a real key, `y` changed in its last base64url character set until `ECDsa.ImportParameters` throws; the coordinate's last character stays in R6.62's set), and a **second `log.key` entry that repeats a `kid` with another key**. The generator writes each `expected.json` from the case's construction, **never by running a fold**; for vectors 66 and 67 it **asserts that the signature verifies under O over the signing input the vector uses** (the generator's own probe that the vector is valid under the other reading). Invisible and decomposing characters are built from code points. Run `dotnet run --project tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures -- --operator-acts-cases` until two consecutive runs agree (Amendment 2, ruling 37).

The Rust loader ignores unknown `meta.json` members [read: it reads `stream` and `subject` and no other]; if it does not, the loader reads `primitive_calls` and ignores it.

The C# `PublishedKeys.cs`, `ClosedJwk.cs` and the Domain parsers are **read, not edited**.

- **(302)** (working label; **open; gate: Task 3b**) *an honoured act row's state is read from the row's members by the raw projectors and from the statement by the Rust fold; a row may omit a member the statement states (vectors 56, 58, 59 need it), so a row that omits one is honoured and ignored; no shipped writer writes such a row (ruling 69).* Closed by Task 3b (`HonouredEvents` hands the projectors the statement's members, a vector, a falsification row).
- Trap **271 (proposed):** *a conformance corpus generated by the implementation's author pins the author's choices as well as the text's; the cheapest second reader is one who gets only the text and the corpus, and what that reader decides differently is a list of choices the text left.* (Task 8 re-greps the traps for the first free number.)
- Trap **272 (proposed):** *a counter that tests "reached step 7" and a counter that tests "called the primitive" are the same until a header check sits between them; OAS30a's rule said the first and counted the second.* Found by reading OAS30a against vectors 62, 66 and 67 before they existed.

2. **The base64url decoders may differ on a header segment whose last character carries non-zero trailing bits** (.NET's `Base64Url.IsValid` and `DecodeFromChars` against the Rust `base64` crate's strict default). A row with such a header segment could decide `act-typ-mismatch` in one and proceed in the other. **Not pinned by any vector and not ruled here**: it is the R6.37 path's question as much as this one's; Task 5b's report says whether its first-contact run found it, and a vector is a one-line follow-up.

Ruling 68 says "the same JSON value" for strings and does not say it for numbers; a row's only numeric member is `prev`. Not pinned; Task 5b's report lists it if its Rust edit touches the comparison.

### Task 3b: what it is, and the decisions that shape it

**73. OAS15's undecidable-delta leg rests on a premise that is false, and ruling 10's "fails closed" sentence is withdrawn.** [read] Ruling 10 said a delta the verifier cannot decide (a `VerifiedLog` over the concatenation that cannot be made) refuses the write transiently (`503`) and never decides "servable"; the pending probe passes a `moderation.applied` event whose payload is the string `"?"` and expects not servable. **The fold is total**: `OperatorActFold.cs`'s own header says it "never throws and never returns a failure for a payload" (any JSON value in the payload is a row, and step 1 answers `act-statement-mismatch` to a payload that is not an object); `VerifiedLog` is built by a constructor that cannot fail; `LifecycleVerifier.OfLog` returns no `Result`. So there is no delta the verifier cannot decide, and the "503" branch has no input. The case the leg was written for decides as **skipped**, and a skipped act is inert: the post stays servable, which is OAS15's forged leg again. **Rulings:** (a) the sentence in ruling 10 is restated: *a delta that holds an operator act is decided over the verified log of the read plus the delta; a skipped act changes nothing; the guard's only failure is an exception from a defect in the fold, which aborts the append (500, nothing recorded), never a decision of "servable"*; (b) **the leg is replaced by two probes** and the `.pending` file is **deleted from the working tree** by Task 3b (the shell's delete of that one path; no version-control command): **OAS15b** (a genuine, honoured withhold in the delta makes the post not servable: the control, built with the signing writers over an in-memory store, split into a read and a delta) and **OAS15c** (a delta row whose payload is not an object, and one whose `statement` is not a string, each leave the post servable: the replacement of the undecidable leg, and the proof that the delta path inherits the fold's totality); (c) **non-property**, to be written into G70's non-properties by Task 8: no consumer refuses a request for the content of an act row (OAS33's property), and none can, because nothing in the path fails.

**74. Ruling 69's and 71's mechanism is not constructible, and the property they state is reached in the projectors.** Amendment 5 said `HonouredEvents` hands the projectors "an honoured act row whose payload members are the statement's, in memory and never persisted". [read] `AppendedEvent`'s constructor is `internal` and `Curia.Domain.csproj:14-16` grants `InternalsVisibleTo` to `Curia.Domain.Tests`, `Curia.Application.Tests` and `Curia.Infrastructure` only: **`Curia.Application` cannot construct an `AppendedEvent`**, so `HonouredEvents` cannot rewrite a payload, and a public copy method in Domain would weaken the guarantee that type's own doc comment makes (a value that says "the store assigned this" cannot be fabricated). **Ruling:** the property is the same and is reached where the row is read: **`AgentStandingProjector` (the owner attestation) and `FlagProjector` (the moderation record) read an act row's members from the row's `statement` member (the canonical JSON text, parsed once per row) when the row has one, and from the row's own members only when it has no `statement`** (the form before this PR, which only unit tests and unfiltered replays feed: a production feed holds honoured rows alone, each of which has a statement that is its canonical form, so the fallback is unreachable from the Forum). The projectors keep their public overloads. **The rule is stated in the errata by one sentence** (substitution E5, below) so that the text, not the code, decides it, and **two vectors (73, 74) pin it** in both folds. **Task 3b carries it; item (302) closes with it.**

**75. The fence's shape: honoured entry points are new names, the unfiltered siblings stay for the tests and are banned from production by name.** [read: `IlCallSites` reports a callee as `(declaring type, member name)` and cannot tell two overloads apart; OAS12 lists 17 callers] A raw list-taking overload and a `VerifiedLog`-taking one of the same name would be one callee to the scan, and a production call of the raw one would pass. Dozens of test call sites across four test assemblies call the unfiltered entry points (`LogFacts.Of`: 21 sites in 7 files [obs by grep]; `SearchProjector.Fold` and the raw folds: ruling 5 counted 47 sites in 13 files, and a union grep over tests finds 24 files naming one of the projectors, `PostureQuery`, `InboxSelector`, `DuplicateCheck` or `HybridSearch`), and changing every one buys no property the fence does not already give. **Rulings:** (a) the honoured entry points get **new names**: `SearchProjector.FoldHonoured(VerifiedLog)`, `LogFacts.OfHonoured(VerifiedLog)`, `InboxSelector.SelectHonoured(VerifiedLog, AgentId)` (the implementer's equivalent, named by the same pattern, if the signature differs), `VerifiedLog.StandingOf(AgentId)`; `PostureQuery.Of(LifecycleVerifier, log, agent)` keeps its name and becomes honoured in place (it has the verifier; no unfiltered sibling remains) and gains `Of(VerifiedLog, AgentId)`; `HybridSearch.SearchAsync` keeps its signature (it holds a verifier and builds one `VerifiedLog` for the corpus and the dispute mark); `DuplicateCheck.AssessAsync(IReadOnlyList<AppendedEvent>, ...)` has no production caller (the route passes a corpus) and is **deleted**, its test sites edited by ruling 79 (c). (b) The unfiltered siblings **stay public and unchanged** (`SearchProjector.Fold(IReadOnlyList)`, `LogFacts.Of`, `LogFacts.Extend`). (c) **OAS12's `RawFolds` gains four rows: `SearchProjector.Fold`, `LogFacts.Of`, `LogFacts.Extend`, `InboxSelector.Select`** (the last because `InboxSelector.Select(list, agent)`, which eight tests call, folds `SearchProjector.Fold(list)`; it is kept as an unfiltered sibling of `SelectHonoured`), so a production caller of an unfiltered sibling is red; and **its `Allowed` list gains three reasoned rows, `SearchProjector`, `LogFacts` and `InboxSelector`**, each saying it is the unfiltered sibling's own body and that production callers of it are banned by the callee rows. Those two rows are not a loosening because the callee rows close what they open; **any other change to OAS12 that removes a row, or adds an `Allowed` row for a type some production code outside it calls, is a fence loosened (a stop)**. (d) **Register item (303) (working label, proposed):** unfiltered entry points live in production assemblies for the tests' sake; the ban is a name-level IL scan; a later cleanup moves them into a test-support assembly. It is the cost of not editing sixty call sites inside a PR whose risk is elsewhere, stated.

**76. One `VerifiedLog` per request, built where the route reads the log, and no cache across requests.** [read] Each route today builds `VerifierOf(http).OfLog(log)` for `DisputedPosts` and, separately, `PostureQuery.Of` builds its own: the fold's ES256 verifications (about 0.15 ms per act row, ruling 52) would be paid once per `OfLog`, so a write route that builds three would pay three times. **Rulings:** (a) each route builds **one** `VerifiedLog` at the point it holds the log and passes it down (`PostureQuery.Of(VerifiedLog, ...)`, `SignalRefusal`, `Servable`, `DecidedLog`, `DisputedPosts.Fold`, `SearchProjector.FoldHonoured`, `StandingOf`); `DecidedLog` carries it; a retry's fresh read builds its own. (b) **Nothing is cached across requests**: no memo of signature verdicts, no `VerifiedLog` keyed by log tail. That is item 278's decision at the hosted-instance gate (ruling 11 (b), ruling 52); a cache added here would change the cost model Amendment 4's second "unsure" names. (c) **OAS41 discharges (a)** (below): for each of five routes, the number of ES256 verifications a request makes over a log holding N act rows exceeds the number over the same log holding none by **exactly the number of act rows that reach R6.67's step 7**, never a multiple. The count is from a counting `IContentVerifier` registered through `WithWebHostBuilder`; if the host cannot take it, the implementer says so and substitutes the Application-level chain measurement of ruling 83 with a **stop** at `2 * N * c` (ruling 52's (a2)) in place of the exact count. (d) The Application-level cost chain of ruling 83 is a **second**, independent measure.

### What I am unsure of

1. **The projectors' fallback** (ruling 74): a row with no `statement` is read from its own members, so an unfiltered feed of a row whose statement disagrees with its members is read from the statement. Both feeds are test-only by the fence; I judge a one-way rule (the statement, always, and a row without one is ignored) stricter and the right end state, but it would turn the existing projector tests (which build unsigned rows) red for no property the Forum lacks, so it is not asked here. If Task 3b finds a production path that feeds a row without a statement, that is a defect, and the fallback is the hole.
2. **The name-level fence** (ruling 75) trusts distinct names. A future edit that gives a honoured entry point the unfiltered one's name is caught by OAS12 (its callers become raw callers) only if the old name is one of the four `RawFolds` rows; the behavioural probes (OAS31, OAS34, OAS35) are the second guard.
3. **OAS41's seam** (`WithWebHostBuilder` replacing the verifier dictionary on a second host over the same database) was not read in `ForumFixture`; the implementer may find the fixture's `ConfigureWebHost` does not compose with it. The fallback is stated.
4. **The Api counts** rest on 61 vector cases and five OAS41 routes; the implementer's run is the authority, and a difference is reported with its cause, not matched.
5. **`ServableAfter`'s concatenation** (the read followed by the delta as one log) assumes the delta is the events appended after the read's last sequence, with none between; I did not read the store's delta source. OAS44 pins the `Position` half; if the guard's delta can overlap or leave a gap, `OfLog` over the concatenation indexes wrongly and the implementer stops and says so.
6. **C20's outcome is predicted, not observed** (ruling 78).
7. **I ran nothing**: every count and every red is read or predicted. I did not open `OperatorActFold.cs` below step 4's check or the Rust fold; the Rust half of ruling 81 rests on Task 5b's report.

### Rulings on Task 3b's four left-red tests and the intermittent probe

**85. No production path writes an automated moderation record, and none is specified to; N2 disabled nothing that worked, and the cost was already stated.** [grep over `src` for `ModeratorKind.Automated`, `"automated"`, `moderation.applied`] The only writer of a `moderation.applied` row is `ApplyModeration.RecordAsync`, which writes `ModeratorKind.Human` and nothing else (`ApplyModeration.cs:182` [read]); the operator's `moderate` verb is its only caller in `src`. `ContentScreener` annotates, accepts or rejects and appends no moderation row (R6.16; principle P1's screen is a gate, not a moderator). The other `Automated` hits in `src` are the credential lifecycle's `AutomatedPostureTrip` (Table 6), a different state machine that no moderation row touches. The only producers of an automated record anywhere are two **test helpers** that insert one raw (`ForumFixture.QuarantineAsAutomatedAsync`, whose own comment says "the host has no producer of one", and `ModerationLoopTests.AppendAnAutomatedDismissalAsync`). R10.36 says automated moderation **MAY** quarantine: permission, not obligation, so no SHALL is unmet. Amendment 1's ruling 20 stated this exactly ("R10.36's permitted automated quarantine cannot be written after this PR (nothing wrote it before either)") and filed Amendment 1's "What I am unsure of" item 2 as the owner question; non-property (f) in the errata says the same. **Therefore: not a finding that N2 silently disabled a feature; Task 3b's report is right that the three tests arrange a state that can no longer arise, and wrong only in treating it as new.** What it **means**, stated so the PR body can say it: until item 294 gives an automated or delegated arm a key class and a statement kind, R10.36's quarantine-pending-review by a detector is **unreachable by construction**, so a detector's false positive cannot hide a post and no detector can, either. That is the safe default under R10.36's own reasoning (a detector must not silence an author). **Owner question: no new one.** Amendment 1's item 2 (human-only is more than the owner wants) stands as filed, still unanswered in the record I read; the PR body names it once. It becomes the owner's the moment someone proposes to build a detector that quarantines, and not before.

**86. Tests 1 to 3 restate for the human arm; none is weakened, none deleted without its coverage named.** The property each guards is "a post the Forum is not serving is answered as withheld, and a comment under it is refused, whichever state holds it". Quarantine is the state the automated row stood in for, and a **signed human quarantine** is genuine and reachable (`forum.QuarantineAsync`, `ForumFixture.cs:151`). Rulings: (a) `NotServedEndToEndTests.R10_91_RLW12_...`: the third state `("an automated quarantine", ...)` is **removed from the states list**; the list keeps "a human withhold" and "a human quarantine" (the quarantine state is already there, so no coverage is lost); the comment above the list is rewritten to say that no automated state exists (ruling 85) and where the characterization lives. (b) `WithheldPostReadTests.R9_42_AnAutomatedQuarantineIsAnsweredAsAHumanOneIs` and `WithheldParentIngestTests.R8_72_ACommentUnderAnAutomatedQuarantineIsRefused`: **if** the file already holds a human-quarantine twin that asserts the same thing (the implementer greps the file for `forum.QuarantineAsync(`), the automated test is **deleted** and its name is listed in the report with the twin's; **otherwise** it is **renamed** to `...AHumanQuarantine...` and its one arrange line becomes `await forum.QuarantineAsync(<id>, ct);`, the assertions byte-identical. (c) **The characterization of the dead arm already exists and stays:** OAS2's `[InlineData("automated", "quarantine")]` (`OperatorActsForgeryTests.cs:64`: a forged automated quarantine leaves the post served), OAS38 and vectors 34, 35 (`moderation-moderator-automated-is-not-honoured`). The implementer **confirms by reading** that OAS2's `automated` row asserts a served post with the forged row **seen** (the skipped count), and states that in the report; if it does not assert the skip, one assertion is added to it (no new test). (d) `ForumFixture.QuarantineAsAutomatedAsync` is **deleted** once no caller remains (a helper that writes an unsigned automated row, kept for nothing, is the next reader's trap); a `git grep` shows zero callers before the deletion. **The reason this is not "weakening":** the assertion text of every kept or converted test is unchanged; the only thing removed is an arrangement the Forum now refuses to honour, and a probe that arranged it would pass for the wrong reason (it would be asserting the skip, under a name that says "withheld"). **Vacuity (question 1):** the converted tests go red if the Forum stopped treating a quarantine as a withhold; the dead arm goes red in OAS2/OAS38 if an automated row were honoured again.

**87. The Domain's (Automated, Quarantine) and (Automated, Dismiss) cells stay, with a comment and no new fence.** [read: `Moderation.cs:249-260`, `:422`] The `Permitted` table is R10.61's table, cell for cell, and the Domain tests (`ModerationTests`, `FlagProjectorTests`) pin it and the "reviewing record" rule `Reviewed` (`:420-422`). Removing the cells would make the Domain diverge from the published table for no behavioural change: after R6.67 step 3 the Application never feeds the fold an automated row from the Forum's log, and the Domain is the layer that must not know which key class exists (CS-6/CS-7). R6.67 step 3 honours `human` statements only (errata G70, ruling 20), so no row reaches this fold with this moderator kind from the Forum's log; the cells are R10.61's table and stay until item 294 gives the arm a key." **No new fence:** OAS38, OAS2 and vectors 34/35 already fail if an automated row is honoured, and a fence on a Domain constant would restate the table against itself (question 4). The edit is comment-only; `git diff --stat` shows `Moderation.cs` with comment lines alone, and Release IL for the Domain is unchanged (the implementer need not show this, the whole run does).

**88. `ModerationLoopTests.R10_39_...` is still discriminating, but for a different reason; its comment says so and it gains one assertion.** [read: `:250-300`] It appends two raw automated dismissals through `AppendAnAutomatedDismissalAsync` and expects R10.39's figures to ignore them.
