A probe whose rows the Forum never read cannot pass for the fold's skip (the vacuity guard of ruling 80 (b), applied here).

**89. LCB13 gets a signed-late-row builder in `tests/Curia.Api.Tests/`, over public types only.** Options weighed against "no unsigned writer in production, and nothing new in a production assembly's surface": (1) `InternalsVisibleTo` `Curia.Application` to `Curia.Api.Tests`: rejected, it exposes every internal of the Application layer to the Api suite for one test, and CS-5's convention in this repository is to make a type public rather than widen visibility (`QueryParameterRefusal.cs`: "without a new `InternalsVisibleTo`"); (2) a public signing seam in `Curia.Application` (`OperatorActStatements.SignModeration` made public): rejected, a public statement-builder in the production assembly is a second door that signs a row for any caller holding a signer, and it would be the first public member of that class whose only caller is a test; (3) the fixture's `SignedActWriters`: it lives in `Curia.Application.Tests` and uses the internal writers; the Api fixture's `ModerateAsync` appends at once and cannot be held (report 3b section 9). **Ruling: a new file `tests/Curia.Api.Tests/LateSignedModeration.cs`** (a test type; no `.csproj` edit, because the file is in the project's own directory) that builds a signed `moderation.applied` row **from public types alone**: `CanonicalJson.CanonicalizeWithNfc`, `DetachedJws`/`IContentSigner` (`Curia.Canon`, public) and the fixture's published operator key (`OperatorActsKeyAsync`, which hands back the PEM and the `OperatorActsSigningKey`, public in `Curia.Operator`). It writes the ten members of R6.67's closed form for a moderation statement (`actor_id`, `adjudicates`, `category`, `digest`, `effect`, `moderator` = `human`, `post_id`, `prev`, `rationale`, `typ` = `curia.moderation/1`) and a row that states **only `statement` and `authorization`** (vector 74's shape, which the fold reads from the statement by ruling 74). **Why this is a check and not a restatement (question 4):** the builder derives its member list from R6.67's published text and the vectors' README, not from `OperatorActStatements`; a drift between the two shows as the fold skipping the row (`act-statement-mismatch`), which LCB13's control turns into a red. **`prev`:** the log index of the stream's last act row that is not skipped or, when none, of the stream's root (`OperatorActStatements`' `Chain`, ruling 60's reading); for case (i) the root is `r1`'s `post.accepted`, **inserted in the same held transaction**, so the builder is called **after** `InsertAsync(holder, r1)` and takes the index from what `InsertAsync` returns (`seq`; the implementer reads how the fold counts a `log_index` against `seq` and states it, and the control below catches an off-by-one); for case (ii) the withhold is committed before the hold, so `prev` is the index of the fixture's own withhold row, read with `ReadAllAsync` before the hold. **The vacuity guard, in the same `[Fact]` and not a new test:** after each hold commits, the test reads the post's serving state through the route (the late withhold in (i) must make `r1` not served, the restore in (ii) must make `s1` served again), so that a row the fold **skipped** (wrong `prev`, wrong header) cannot pass as "honoured": were it skipped, (i) would read 409 already-revised and (ii) 201, exactly the failure the report shows. No unsigned writer is added to any production assembly; the old `LateModeration` helper is replaced, not kept.

**90. OAS36's intermittency: a root-cause procedure, and whether it blocks.** [read: `OperatorActsForgeryTests.cs:367-398`] **What is known:** the control leg (a genuine original, then a duplicate that must be refused `409 curia/posts/duplicate-question`) answered `201` twice in the whole `OperatorActs` filter, never alone (12 of 12 green), before any forged row exists in that leg's board; so the cause is **not forged-row handling**. The test builds the duplicate as the original's body plus `" Please advise."` under the **same title**, on a fresh random board, and the duplicate check compares an embedding (the hashed n-gram test adapter, `ForumFixture`'s one explicit door) against a published near-duplicate threshold, for an original that must already be **indexed** (R8.83: the index is kept in step with the log by a check). Two live hypotheses, one more, and the procedure that separates them **without a retry**:

1. **H-sim (a deterministic function of the random suffix):** the random title suffix changes the hashed n-gram vector, and for some suffixes the similarity of original and duplicate falls under the threshold, so the Forum is right to answer `201`. *Probe:* a scratch test (not committed) that, for 2,000 suffixes drawn as `OperatorActsApi.Suffix()` draws them, embeds the original's and the duplicate's text with the test adapter and prints the minimum similarity and the threshold. **If any similarity is below the threshold, H-sim is the cause**, the failure rate predicted from the 2,000 matches the observed 2 of 7 only if the near-miss mass is about 25 to 30 percent (state it), and the fix is in the test (fix the suffix, or use a body whose similarity does not depend on it).
2. **H-index (the original is not yet in the index when the duplicate arrives):** the check misses a duplicate that arrives before the catch-up has indexed its original, and a concurrent sibling test (the class runs in one collection with other hosts' load) delays the catch-up. *Probe:* a temporary diagnostic (not committed) that, at the moment a `201` is observed on the control leg, queries the vector-index table through the operator's database connection for the original's post id and prints present/absent and the index's high-water mark against the log's last sequence; run the whole `OperatorActs` filter in a loop (`for i in $(seq 30)`, each run's output kept) **with** and **without** a sibling load (run the filter once beside a second `dotnet test` of the same class, which is the case that fails). **If the row is absent at the `201`, H-index is the cause.** That is **a Forum behaviour, not a test race**: §8.5's dedupe refusing a duplicate is a published guarantee, and a duplicate admitted because the original is not yet indexed is a **defect in the dedupe, pre-existing**, to be registered as a new item, and OAS36 then needs a deterministic wait for the index **through the mechanism R8.83 publishes** (not a sleep).
3. **H-N2 (the honoured corpus drops the original in some state):** `SearchProjector.FoldHonoured` (the dedupe's corpus since Task 3b, `ForumEndpoints` `DuplicateRefusal`) omits a post it should include, for example when the original's author's standing is read from a `VerifiedLog` built before the author is enrolled, or the corpus is folded from a log read that does not yet hold the original. *Probe:* the **same 30-run loop on two exported trees** (`git archive` of `adc8cfce`, before the consumers switch, and of `1daff933`), **the control leg alone** (`--filter "FullyQualifiedName~OAS36"` with the forged leg commented out in the export only, or a scratch test that runs only the control leg on both): a failure rate on `1daff933` and none on `adc8cfce` is H-N2. (At `adc8cfce` the whole probe is red for the forged leg, which is why the control leg must be isolated.)

**Rulings on blocking:** the intermittency is **classified, not tolerated**. **H-sim or H-index-in-the-test: Task 3d fixes the arrangement, runs the control leg 50 consecutive times green under the load that failed it, and the PR is not blocked after that.** **H-index as a Forum behaviour (pre-existing): the PR is not blocked by it** (it is not N2's and the signed-act property does not depend on the dedupe), **but it is a stop for the implementer:** record the cause with the diagnostic output, make OAS36 deterministic by the published mechanism, and return to the architect to register the item (a duplicate admitted before its original is indexed is a property the dedupe states and does not keep). **H-N2: blocks.** It is a defect this PR introduced in the thing OAS36 guards (the dedupe now reading honoured acts), and Task 6 does not start. Whatever the cause, **no retry attribute, no `[Fact(Skip)]`, no widened timeout is a fix** (the project's named failure: a probe that is quiet). **Unknown after the procedure** (all three probes negative): stop and return; the probe stays in the PR but Task 6's whole run is not taken as a gate while it flakes, because a gate that flakes carries no information about the next real failure.

### Rulings on the review's findings and leads

**94. Zero trust (b): `lifecycle-audit` printing `aggregate=` is within R4.43 and R11.97; one clause makes the text say so.** [read: `Program.cs:734` prints `aggregate={DisplayLiteral.Of(row.Aggregate)}`; the same line form is in `68657f78:src/Curia.Operator/Program.cs:680`, which PR N shipped for lifecycle rows; R11.97: "list skipped operator-act rows beside lifecycle rows **by the existing line form**"] R4.43 bans the **payload** ("never by its payload") and names three fields the report carries (index, type, predicate). The aggregate is the stream key, an event column and not a payload member; the operator needs it to know which agent or post the row names (an index alone does not), it is written as a display literal (R10.63) so it cannot end the line or carry control characters, and the audit is operator-only. It is a fourth field R4.43's list does not name, so a literal reader could call the line wider than the text; the line form is PR N's and R11.97 adopts it. **Ruling: within; no code change; Task 8 substitutes E8** (R4.43's "by its log index, its type and the predicate that failed" gains "and, in the operator's audit alone, the aggregate it names, as a display literal"). **EventId 7010 stays index, type and predicate (R11.97's words): a log sink reaches more readers than the operator.** [grep: the aggregate is `TEXT` in `db/0001` with no length cap in the column; the audit lists at most 100 rows, one line each, so a row's line length is the credential holder's, as PR N's lifecycle audit's already was; not new, not closed here.] **The `check-spec` and the R11.97-words probe (OAS32) do not read R4.43's sentence** [read: OAS32 parses R11.97's published words]; Task 8 runs both after E8.

**95. Zero trust (c): the agent-facing key set does not report skipped operator acts, by reason, and this is not a new owner question.** [read: errata G68's non-property (e): "A skipped row is reported to the operator and, to an agent that asks, in the key set (R4.41, R11.96)"; R11.96's closing reason] The 2026-11-11 decision ("agents are told of skipped rows") concerned the rows PR N skips: **lifecycle rows, which are the agent's own acts** (its enrolment, rotation, retirement), where the agent's own profile is the one independent record that can show the Forum skipped a genuine act of its own (R11.96: "the agent's own record is the only independent evidence"). **An operator act is not the agent's act**: an attestation is the operator's statement about an agent, a moderation record is a statement about a post (its stream is the post's, whose author is a different agent from any reader); the agent holds no record of either, so a list would tell it something it cannot check, and R11.96's other reason applies without change: "a list printed ... would give whoever can append rows a banner on that author's name" (an attestation row naming any agent id appends in that agent's stream; a forger could write every agent's key set a line). **Ruling: not built; the reason is recorded as non-property (k) by Task 8; the PR body (Task 9) says once that agents are told of skipped lifecycle rows and not of skipped operator acts, and why.** It becomes a question for the owner only if the owner meant the decision to cover **every** skipped row: **I did not read the owner's own words** (only the project's memory summary and G68's record of the scope), so if it is that, it is a new stage (a key-set member, `Curia.Client`, `Curia.Mcp` 0.13.0, Rust's `LogKeys`), not N2's: N2 changes no tool's words (ruling 12). Where an agent *can* learn of one today: the operator's audit tells the operator, and an agent whose own enrolment attestation is not honoured sees `owner_verified: false` on its re-announcement (OAS35).

**96. OAS17's stale comment: fixed in Task 6b, with the file it is in.** [read: `OperatorActsFenceTests.cs:70-74`] The comment on `OAS17_NoForumAssemblyHoldsTheOperatorActsKeysVariableName` says "VACUOUS UNTIL THE VARIABLE EXISTS ... the clause that is red today ... is the pending OAS17 file". The variable exists (the operator tool reads it), the pending file landed (Task 4's), and the scan is not vacuous: `curia-operator` is excluded by name and holds the string, so the scan's positive case is the operator assembly itself. The new comment says what the test is now: the scan reads every shipped assembly but `curia-operator` for the variable's UTF-16 spelling in the string heap, and **FOAS22 (Task 7) is the row that shows it red**; until Task 7 reports, the comment says "falsified by FOAS22 (Task 7)" and **not** "falsified". **A positive control inside the test is added:** the same scan over `curia-operator.dll` must find the string (a scan that reads the wrong heap or the wrong encoding finds it nowhere and passes for the wrong reason). [read: `Encoding.Unicode.GetBytes` over the PE bytes; the string heap stores literals UTF-16, so the encoding is right; the control is what would show it wrong.] **FOAS22 must patch a *read that survives the Release compiler***: an unused local `var _ = "CURIA_..."` is removed by Roslyn in Release and leaves no `ldstr`; the patch is `Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("CURIA_OPERATOR_ACTS_SIGNING_KEY_PEM")` with its result **used** (returned or stored in a static), in a `Curia.Api` method that runs at start-up or not, and the row is run **in Release and in Debug**.

**97. Consumers (1): OAS12 gains a second fence on the event-type literals, and its allow-list becomes per method.** [grep: `OwnerAttestedType` and `ModerationAppliedType` are `const`, so the compiler inlines each as an `ldstr` at every use; the uses in `src` are exactly `OperatorActFold.cs:67,78`, `AgentStandingProjection.cs:279`, `FlagProjection.cs:144`, `AttestOwner.cs:135,182`, `ApplyModeration.cs:149,223`, `LogFacts.cs:115`] The fence names **callees**; a new consumer that decides a tier or a withholding by reading `e.Event.Type.Value == "moderation.applied"` and its payload calls no raw projector and passes OAS12. That is the consumer R4.43 forbids and the one the unfiltered entry points were fenced to stop, so the constants are the second door. **Ruling (Task 6b): `OAS12b`**, in the same file: a scan of every shipped assembly's `ldstr` operands (a new `IlCallSites` helper over every type, the existing `StringLiterals` taking one type name) for a literal that **equals or contains** `agent.owner-attested` or `moderation.applied` (contains, because an interpolated string folds a `const` hole into its neighbouring literal), allowed only to the types above, each row with its reason (the fold, the two projectors, the two writers, `LogFacts`' delta trigger). Vacuity (question 1), both halves: **each literal is found at least once, and each allowed type owns at least one**, so a renamed constant, a moved use or a stale row fails; the scan's own test says so, as OAS12's pair does. **Cost, stated:** the scan sees a literal, not a computed or a `StartsWith` match, and not the Rust or the SQL; it is a second fence and not a proof. **And the allow-list of OAS12 narrows from type to method** [read: a reviewer's note, verified: `Allowed` is `(Type, Reason)`, so a raw call written inside `SearchProjector.FoldHonoured`, `LogFacts.OfHonoured` or `InboxSelector.SelectHonoured` passes while their unfiltered siblings sit in the same types]: the rows name `(Type, Method, Reason)`, the method being the source method `IlCallSites` reports; the finest the IL gives is a method, so `LogFacts.ServableAfter`'s two branches are one row. This changes FOAS31's expected reds (ruling 105 and the Task 7 brief): it is now a row the fence shows too, recorded `masked-by-fence` beside the behavioural probes.

**98. Vacuity, OAS12's guard: per callee, by reflection where there is no caller.** [grep over `src`: `FlagProjector.Fold`, `FlagProjector.Extend`, `AgentStandingProjector.Fold` and `SearchProjector.Fold` have call sites in the Application (the verifier, the projectors, `InboxSelector.Select`); **`LogFacts.Of`, `LogFacts.Extend` and `InboxSelector.Select` have no caller in `src` at all, by design** (production uses `OfHonoured` and `SelectHonoured`; their rows exist to ban a future caller)] A per-callee "at least one site" guard would fail on three rows that are correct. **Ruling:** (a) every `RawFolds` row **resolves by reflection** (the type in `Curia.Application` and at least one method of that name), which fails a renamed callee at once; (b) the four rows that have callers also have **at least one call site**; (c) every `Allowed` row's type exists and owns at least one raw-fold site, so a stale row fails. The single total `sites.Length > 0` is replaced, not kept beside. Task 6b states, from the run, which rows fall under (b).

**99. Vacuity, OAS31: each consumer is first shown able to move, and the forged rows are shown read.** [read: `OperatorActsConsumersTests.cs:36-139`: before/after strings joined and compared by `WholeText.Same`; controls only for C12 and the ask statuses; `SearchCountAsync` returns `-1` for a missing `results`; no check that any forged row was read] `before == after` is true for a consumer that cannot change at all (an observer on the wrong route, a search that returns nothing both times, a tier that is `T0` whatever is in the log), and for a Forum that skipped every row and every genuine one too. **Ruling (Task 6b):** (a) the table is run **twice**, once with the genuine signed act of the same shape (the fixture's signing writers: an attestation for the aged agent and for the six diversification authors, a withhold of the victim and of the inbox question, a withhold of the C12 answer) and once with the forged row; for **every** consumer row `genuine != before` (the consumer can move) and `forged == before` (this is the probe); a row whose genuine case equals its before names itself in the failure; (b) the forged rows are shown **read**: the count of rows the operator's audit reports skipped (ruling 100's helper) rises by exactly the number of rows the test forged (10 today: 1, 1, 6, 1, 1; the test counts its own calls); (c) `SearchCountAsync` fails (an assertion, not `-1`) when the response is not `200` or has no `results`. **Falsification T6bF7 and T6bF8** show the first two red; T6bF7 shows OAS31 **green** before this edit under the patch that makes the fold skip every row.

**100. A finding of the architect's own: the audit's listing stops at 100 rows, and both skip-count helpers count listed lines.** [read: `Program.cs:698` `AuditListed = 100`, `:736` "and <n> more"; `OperatorActsForgeryTests.SkippedRowsListedAsync` and `ModerationLoopTests.SkippedRowsAsync` both `Count(l => l.StartsWith("skipped  log_index="))`] Once a class's database holds more than 100 skipped rows, both helpers read 100 before and after and the vacuity guards of OAS33c (`listedBefore + inserted == after`) and R10_39 (`skippedBefore + 2 == after`) go red for a reason that is not a defect, **and the order a database reaches 100 in is the order the tests ran in**: the shape of Part A's order-dependence (the lazily published key). OAS33c alone inserts 63 rows (61 cases) and the class's other legs forge more; the whole runs are green, so the class is under 100 today, by a margin nobody measured. **Ruling (Task 6b):** one helper, `OperatorActsApi.SkippedRowsAsync(ct)`, parses the audit's first line (`honoured <h>  skipped <n>`, which is the total and is not capped) and **throws when the line is absent or does not parse** (no default of 0); OAS31, OAS33c and R10_39 use it; Task 6b prints the largest `n` any class reaches, so the margin is a number in the report. Trap 274 (proposed): *a count read from a list that a tool truncates is a count of the truncation; read the total the tool prints beside it.*

**101. R10_39: comments corrected, no new dependence on the skip.** [read: `ModerationLoopTests.cs:290-307`, `:330-340`; `Moderation.cs:420-422`] The two rows are automated dismissals; the test's public derivation **drops every moderator that is not `human` or `delegated_agent` itself** (`:335`), and the private join reads the raw `FlagProjector.Fold`, whose `Reviewed` rule (`Moderation.cs:420`: `Permits(action) && action.Moderator is not Automated`) already ignores an automated record whatever the act fold decides. So **both derivations ignore the rows with or without the skip**, and the comments at `:290-293` ("A consumer that counted it would time B's action at 30 minutes") and `:301-304` ("A consumer that honoured it would release A") are false of this test: a consumer that honoured them would still be ignored by `Reviewed`. What the test guards, and still does, is **R10.61's rule at the Domain** and that the Forum **read and skipped** both rows (the count assertion, ruling 88). **Ruling: the comments are rewritten to say exactly that and that the dependence of the Forum's decisions on the skip is OAS2, OAS4, OAS27 and OAS38 (forged `human` rows); no forged `human` row is added to R10_39, because its derivations are raw readers of the public log by design (the test's own claim is that the figures are computable from the public log alone) and would count the forged row, so the test would measure the forgery and not the Forum.** [One consequence, for Task 8: R10.39's "computable from the public log alone" now holds **over honoured records** (R6.67), which an auditor decides with `curia-testis`'s operator-acts fold; R10.39's published statistics are unbuilt (`CLAUDE.md`), so Task 8 adds one annotation sentence to R10.39 and no code.] The test's name and assertions are unchanged.

**102. Text against code: the C# fold chains per aggregate, not per kind of act; a real divergence, reachable by an enrolling agent, fixed with a vector.** [read: `OperatorActFold.cs:115-123`: `streams[aggregate]`, `stream.LastHonoured = i` for either act type; `:160-161`: `expectedPrev = stream.LastHonoured ?? root` with `root` the aggregate's first event **when it is of the row's root type**; Rust `operator_acts.rs:150-185` filters the aggregate's events to the act type and chains `prev` over those] They agree whenever an aggregate holds one kind of row. **Can one aggregate hold both?** [read] `PostId` is a server-minted ULID (`UlidGenerator`, `PostId.Parse` accepts only a canonical ULID); `AgentId` is any non-blank string of at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes with no NUL or lone surrogate (`AgentId.cs`: `StorableText.Check(..., names: true)`), so **an agent id may equal a post's id**, and `EnrollAgent` appends the enrolment pair to the aggregate named by the agent id [read: `:190-209`; **I did not run it, so whether the enrolment route refuses an id that already heads a stream is unverified**]. Then one aggregate is a post's stream (root `post.accepted`) and an agent's (root `agent.enrolled`), and **the root is its first event, so at most one kind has a root**. **The divergence:** a genuine moderation row at index 7 sets C#'s shared `LastHonoured` to 7; a validly signed attestation row in the same aggregate with `prev` 7 then passes C#'s step 4 (`expectedPrev = 7`) and reaches step 7, honoured; Rust has no root for the attestation kind and decides `act-broken-link`. An *unsigned* such row differs only in its predicate (C# `act-typ-mismatch` at step 5, Rust `act-broken-link` at step 4). **What it costs:** honoured against skipped needs the operator's key, so it is a parity gap between two implementations of one rule and not a forgery path; and R6.67's text does not say, which is the finding (the text read "the honoured prefix is the act rows of the stream", two readings). **Ruling (Task 6b): the text is fixed (E6: the prefix is the honoured rows of the row's own kind in the aggregate; a stream's root is the aggregate's first event and a kind has a root only when that event is its root type), the C# fold keeps one `LastHonoured` per kind, and vector 75 pins it** (`conformance/operator-acts/mixed-stream-attestation-does-not-chain-to-a-moderation-row`: the aggregate holds `post.accepted` at the root, then an `agent.enrolled` and the two keys' rows as the family's base needs, then a genuine human withhold honoured with `prev` the root, then a **validly signed** attestation whose `prev` is the withhold's index; expected from the text: the attestation skipped `act-broken-link`, the agent has no owner; C# today decides it honoured, so the vector is red on the tree and Rust is green). The writers already chain per kind (`OperatorActStatements.ChainOf(seen, subject, rootType, actType)`), so no writer changes. If Rust parts from the vector, Task 6b stops and returns: the vector is written from the text, and the fix is then in Rust.

**103. Text against code: base64url trailing bits: refuted as a divergence, measured; pinned by vector 76 because the guarantee is a BCL behaviour no probe holds.** [obs: on SDK 10.0.401, `Base64Url.IsValid("eyJhIjoxfQ")` true, `IsValid("eyJhIjoxfR")` false, `IsValid("eyJhIjoxfS")` false, `IsValid("QQ")` true, `IsValid("QR")` false; the same on 11.0.100] [read] C# calls `Base64Url.IsValid` before it decodes both segments (`OperatorActFold.cs:190` for the header, `DetachedJws.cs:178` and `:238` for the signature and the header), so **a last character that carries non-zero trailing bits is refused by C#**; Rust's `URL_SAFE_NO_PAD` with the crate's default (`decode_allow_trailing_bits` false) refuses it (`jws.rs:125`, `:199`, `operator_acts.rs:428`). **The two folds agree today.** What holds that agreement is `Base64Url.IsValid`'s behaviour, which the README states as "not pinned, on purpose" (its closing paragraph) and which no test holds: a runtime that decoded leniently would turn an operator-signed row spelled another way from honoured to skipped in one fold and not the other, silently. The project already guards the same property for a JWK coordinate by a round trip (`ClosedJwk.cs:78-82`), which is the idiom, but here the primitive is the BCL's own and it is correct. **Ruling (Task 6b): vector 76** (`non-canonical-base64url-segments-are-not-honoured`: an agent's stream, a genuine honoured attestation, then two rows signed by the operator key and **spelled another way**: row A's protected-header segment ends in a character with non-zero trailing bits (the header JSON's length must not be a multiple of 3: a space after `{` is JSON and shifts it), expected `act-typ-mismatch`; row B's signature segment ends in a character with the same top bits and other trailing bits, expected `act-signature-invalid`; both `prev` the genuine row, so each decides alone; expected from the text), **E7a and E7b** (R6.67 step 5 and step 7 say it), and the README's "does not pin" clause for it is removed. If either fold does not decide the vector as written, Task 6b stops and returns (the row names which fold). **No C# fix is made, because none is needed.**

### Task 7: falsification FOAS1 to FOAS39 (the brief is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-13-planN2/task-7-brief.md`)

**105.** Task 7 runs **after Task 6b is committed**, on a clean tree, alone. The rows are the plan's table as amended (FOAS1 to FOAS26, FOAS27 to FOAS35, FOAS36, FOAS37 and FOAS38, FOAS39); the brief restates each with its patch site **as the tree now has it** (Task 3b renamed and moved the consumers' call sites) and its must-red probes by their test names. **Rules of the run:** (a) a patch is applied only to a tree whose `git status --porcelain` is empty, from a kept copy, and a run is read only after a build line that says it succeeded for the patched tree (ruling 82); (b) restore by **plain `cp`**, then `touch`, then a `--no-incremental` build, and a run of the probes unpatched once; `cargo clean -p curia-testis` for a Rust row; (c) **each row has a verdict**: `red` (a named probe fails **by its own assertion**: not an exception of the patched code, not a fixture's or set-up's), `masked-by-fence` (only OAS12, OAS12b or OAS17 is red; the behavioural probes named for the row stay green: **a finding about the consumer's probe**), `masked` (red only by an exception or a fixture), `named-green` (a probe the plan named stayed green while another reds: recorded, with which), `undiscriminated` (nothing red) or `not-built`; a row whose verdict is not `red` is **not accepted**: for `undiscriminated` and `masked` the probe is the defect (a vacuous probe, question 1), and Task 7 **fixes the probe in the test file it lives in** (never the patch, never `src/`), shows the row red, restores, shows the original green, and lists the fix; more than three such fixes, or a fix that needs a vector or a `src/` change, is a stop; (d) **ruling 27 stands**: behavioural probes run with OAS12 and OAS12b **excluded**, and the fence's red is recorded apart; (e) a row whose red is an Architecture probe runs in **Release and in Debug** (the IL differs: D16); (f) **FOAS22 must show `OAS17_NoForumAssemblyHoldsTheOperatorActsKeysVariableName` red** (not only the implementers' test) in both configurations, with a patch that keeps its `ldstr` (ruling 96); (g) FOAS35, FOAS36 and the guards whose probe was green before the patch are recorded beside each other as **the rows whose "red" is a guard or a characterization**; (h) FOAS39 has **two forms**, C# and Rust, and FOAS37 has two forms, `log:keys` and `log:heads` (the second is T4F24's patch, re-run on the finished tree). **Counts to record:** rows run (**39**, with FOAS37's and FOAS39's forms: **41** forms), first forms red, the masked (with `masked-by-fence` apart), the named-green, the controls.

### What I am unsure of

1. **Whether `EnrollAgent` refuses an agent id that already heads another stream** (ruling 102). I read the append, not the guard; if it refuses, the divergence is unreachable from the Forum's own routes and reachable only by a row the credential holder appends, and the ruling stands (the vector and E6 pin a rule two folds hold).
2. **How close each class's database is to 100 skipped rows** (ruling 100): the run prints it; I did not count.
3. **Vector 76's construction**: a header JSON whose base64url length leaves trailing bits needs `len % 3 != 0` (a space after `{` shifts it, and the ADMIT profile accepts the header); whether Rust maps a non-canonical signature segment to `act-signature-invalid` or refuses earlier I read in `jws.rs:199`, not in the fold, so the vector's second row is the check.
4. **Per-method allow-list rows** (ruling 97): the `CallerMethod` names for lambdas and closures come from `IlCallSites.SourceMethodName`; I did not run the scan, so the row names (`.ctor` for `VerifiedLog`'s lazy lambdas, for example) are the implementer's reading of the red text.
5. **The Api counts after Task 6b** (113 and 1735) assume OAS33c's theory gains exactly the two new vectors, both of which have a skipped row; derived, not run.
6. **I did not read the owner's own words** for the 2026-11-11 decision (ruling 95).

**Controller's note on ruling ZT(c) (2026-11-14).** The owner's words of 2026-11-12, Q-B, are: "tell the agent too when a lifecycle row about it is skipped (not operator-only)". A forged owner attestation about an agent, or a forged withhold of its post, is a skipped row about that agent but not a lifecycle row. This amendment's narrow reading (not built; non-property (k)) is the architect's, made without the owner's words in front of it. The controller does not decide the extension: it is recorded as owner question **Q-N2-7** (does Q-B extend to skipped operator acts about an agent?), non-blocking for the build, listed in the PR body beside Q-N2-1 to Q-N2-6. If the owner says yes, it is a follow-up PR (an agent-facing report of skipped acts naming index, type and predicate only).

### As run: Task 0 (2026-11-14; `task-0-report.md` and `keep/`)

Run on the unmodified tree (`src/`, `tests/`, `db/` equal to `68657f78`), .NET SDK 10.0.401, scratch tests deleted after (copies in `keep/`), `git status --short` empty at the end, no `curia_e2e_*` or `curia_n2_*` database or role left. **Result:** the composed forgery of ruling 34 reaches T1 as the application role alone; a forged `moderation.applied` withholds, restores, dismisses and demotes; the owner binding is poisoned by one forged row; forged owners move Table 13's weight; `delegated_agent` is honoured on a forged row; a second `agent.enrolled` locks a genuine agent's token out and a planted root squats an identity; M1 (the role suffices for every insert), M2 (198 ms at p95), M5 (both fences pass in both configurations) and M6 (unknown `log.*` ignored; a malformed known one `500`s the Acta) measured; **M3, M4 and the consumers corrected here; M7, M8 and M9 not measured, ruled in Amendment 1 (rulings 25 and 26).** Every correction is C1 to C12 of Amendment 1.
